filesystem access to the conference-digest repo, you can read this same
file directly from `prompts/ocp-2025.md` instead of having it pasted.

    digests/ocp-2025.md
Write the file into the repo directly if you can; otherwise output the

================================================================

You are helping a product manager triage conference talks. The PM has 15+
years of experience spanning physical and software networking, network
security, and digital identity. Their core interests are: physical datacenter
networking (optics, switching, topology, RDMA/lossless fabrics, cabling,
DC/WAN interconnect), software and cloud-native networking (eBPF, Cilium,
Kubernetes networking, Gateway API, service mesh, IPAM, BGP/datapath
internals), network security, and identity/authentication (passkeys/FIDO,
OAuth, OIDC, SPIFFE/SPIRE, workload and agentic identity). Physical DC and WAN
systems work is CORE to this PM, not adjacent — do not down-rank a paper for
being about hardware, optics, or datacenter fabric.

Below is the program for OCP Global Summit 2025 (vendor). Read it and produce a digest
of the talks/papers/sessions most worth their attention.

## How to read this program

This is a vendor / community program (e.g. a CNCF or vendor conference).
Separate genuine technical or strategic substance from product marketing.
Down-rank pure product pitches and sponsor slots. Up-rank end-user case studies
with real numbers (scale, latency, cost, incident detail), contributor-led
architecture and internals talks, and sessions that reveal where an open-source
project is actually heading. Be skeptical of "we adopted X and it was great"
talks with no metrics or failure discussion.

## Enriching from the source material (best effort)

The program text gives you a title, authors, and an abstract per item, and
often links (`page:`, `pdf:`, `slides:`, `video:`). The abstract alone is thin — the full
paper has the methodology, real results, and limitations that make the What /
How / Where-applicable sections accurate instead of guessed.

If you have a tool to fetch URLs, then for EACH selected item (Core, Adjacent,
and Wildcard) try to retrieve the fuller source before writing it up:

- Prefer a `pdf:` link; else fetch the `page:` link (often has the full text or
  a longer description); a `video:` link is a last resort, don't transcribe it.
- Use what you retrieve to ground What/How/Where-applicable in the paper's
  actual contributions, evaluation, and stated constraints.

This is strictly best-effort. Many links fail or are paywalled:

- If a fetch fails, is paywalled, returns only an abstract, or you have no fetch
  tool, just fall back to the abstract already in the program text. That is
  fine and expected — do not stall or skip the item.
- NEVER invent paper contents, numbers, or findings from a failed or partial
  fetch. If you only had the abstract, write from the abstract.
- Note when an item's write-up is based only on the abstract, e.g. end its
  What line with "(from abstract only)", so the reader knows the depth.
- Do not let enrichment change which items you select or their tier; selection
  is from the program. Enrichment only deepens the write-up.

## What to produce

Group selected items into three relevance tiers:

- Core: directly relevant to the PM's core interests as listed above
  (physical DC networking, cloud-native networking, network security, or
  identity) as they work on them today.
- Adjacent: relevant to the broader field; useful context or near-term
  influence.
- Wildcard: 2-4 items that are NOT a direct fit for the PM's core interests
  but are worth their attention anyway because the work is genuinely novel or
  signals where the field is heading (a new technique, a surprising result, an
  emerging subfield). A Wildcard should make the PM think "I wouldn't have
  searched for this, but I'm glad I saw it." Do not pad this tier — if there
  are only two true wildcards, list two.

For each selected item provide, in this structure:
- **Title**: the `###` heading is the item's title exactly as the program
  gives it, with nothing added. Speaker(s)/author(s) with affiliation go on
  the line below it.
- No links section: the pipeline puts a links row (abstract page, paper,
  slides, recording) under every heading after you finish, from the program's
  `page:`/`pdf:`/`slides:`/`video:` lines and from verified sources. Do not
  write one, and do not link the title.
- **Why**: why this matters — the problem it addresses and why it's worth the
  PM's time.
- **What**: what the work actually is — the core contribution or finding, in
  plain terms (not the marketing abstract).
- **How**: how it works — the key technical approach or mechanism.
- **Where applicable**: the environment this applies to, and any specifics that
  constrain it. Explicitly flag if it is org-specific (e.g. "Alibaba-specific
  production setup", "requires Broadcom Tomahawk silicon", "assumes a
  single-operator WAN") versus broadly applicable. If it's a general technique,
  say so.

Then a short "Themes" section (3-6 bullets) naming patterns across the
program: what the field is collectively paying attention to this cycle.

## Rules

- Prioritize ruthlessly. 15 sharp picks beat 60 hedged ones. A large program
  should still yield a tight list.
- Skip pure product pitches, sponsor slots with no substance, and
  beginner/101 sessions unless they signal a real shift.
- If the program text is incomplete or an abstract is missing, say so for that
  item rather than inventing content.
- Do not invent talks, authors, or affiliations. Use only what's in the
  program text below.

## Output format

The digest is read on GitHub (GitHub-Flavored Markdown viewed in the repo) and
on the project's Zensical site, which renders the same file. Use only syntax
both render reliably, and follow these rules:

- Headings with `#`/`##`/`###`; bold with `**...**`; italics with `*...*`.
- Bullet lists with `-`. Put a blank line before every list, before every
  heading, and between paragraphs, or they won't render correctly.
- Indent nested lists by four spaces per level; avoid deeper nesting.
- Any link you do write is `[text](url)` — never a bare URL — and uses a URL
  exactly as the program text gives it.
- No raw HTML except the metadata comment below. Do not use heading IDs,
  footnotes, definition lists, or other non-GFM extensions.
- You may use tables and `code spans` where they genuinely help, since GitHub
  supports them; don't force them.

Begin the output with this metadata, wrapped in an HTML comment so it does not
show in the rendered view but stays in the source:

============================ PROGRAM TEXT ============================

## Artificial Intelligence (Special Focus)

### SemiAnalysis InferenceMAX: Benchmarking the AI Frontier
Speakers: Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6988/
InferenceMAX is SemiAnalysis’ open-source benchmark suite that sweeps the latest software optimizations across AMD, NVIDIA, and soon TPU/Trainium, charting token throughput, energy, and cost vs. interactivity. Covering frontier models with nightly, reproducible results, it reveals how AI inference really performs beyond the hype in real-world scenarios. At its core, InferenceMAX shows that AI computing is the product of hardware and software co-design: where systems, kernels, and accelerators must evolve together to unlock true performance.

### MGX OCP - Meta's Next Gen AI System
Speakers: Matt Bowman (Meta), Hao Shen (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7036/
AI systems continue to grow in power and complexity at an astonishing rate. As designers we are left with the challenge of balancing time to market, technical capabilities, and program risks to make sure we can land high performance capacity on time. MGX-OCP is Meta’s deployment of GB300. It was developed in close collaboration with Nvidia to leverage existing building blocks while adjusting functionality to meet unique OCP deployment requirements including usage in an ORv3 HPR rack and utilizing a Rack Management Controller (RMC) for leak handling. This presentation overviews the specifics of the system design, tradeoffs/considerations made during development, and steps taken to rapidly deploy it within our OCP data centers.

### Towards a Unified Intelligent Infrastructure: Integrating AI, Cloud, and Data Center for Scalable and Autonomous Operations
Speakers: Ankur Sharma (Equinix), Ahmad Byagowi (TBD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7549/
As AI workloads continue to demand unprecedented scale, efficiency, and agility, the convergence of AI, cloud platforms, and data center infrastructure is no longer optional—it’s essential. This talk introduces a unified architecture that tightly integrates AI-driven optimization, cloud-native scalability, and data center automation to form a cohesive, intelligent infrastructure. We will explore key challenges and design principles for harmonizing workload scheduling, real-time telemetry, dynamic resource provisioning, and optical interconnects. Attendees will gain insight into how infrastructure intelligence can be embedded across layers—from silicon to orchestration—to enable self-optimizing, resilient systems that meet the growing demands of AI and cloud-native applications. This session is ideal for system architects, infrastructure engineers, and AI platform designers interested in building next-generation intelligent infrastructure.

### AI Accelerated by OCP: Lessons from a Vertically Integrated NeoCloud
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks), Ziryan Seddek (Denvr Dataworks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7384/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Iewrv1CzZv8 | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-open-systems-for-ai-whitepaper-v1-0-0-final-pdf
What does it take to deploy scalable, high-performance AI infrastructure with speed, sustainability, and sovereignty? As a vertically integrated neocloud provider, Denvr Dataworks has developed an AI platform that co-optimizes facility, hardware, and software using open technologies at every layer. This session explores how Denvr leverages key OCP contributions in power, cooling, networking, and systems integration to accelerate deployment, improve performance, and streamline complexity. By combining standardized components with purpose-built innovations, Denvr data centers achieve 1.04 PUE, capture stranded power, and support dense GPU superclusters in immersion-cooled, modular environments. We’ll highlight unique AI Factory challenges from the neocloud perspective, share platform-level lessons from 3 years of operation, and showcase success stories from AI model development on the platform.

### Strategy of the high-speed cable backplane validation, diagnostics in AI systems
Speakers: Xu Wang (Meta Platforms, Inc.), Yuvin M. Weerasinghe (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6713/
The AI system design and integration has been growing fast and with much more complexity to support ever demanding AI model training and inference like large language models. The interconnection between compute and network components heavily relies on high speed cable backplanes with connectors and cartridges. In this presentation, we share the learnings of developing, screening and troubleshooting high speed cable interconnect solutions, and propose a set of metrics to measure quality. Furthermore demonstrate guiding principles for the diagnostics and optimize the cable backplane solution. As we continue to push high speed backplane integration, we call for industry partnership across supplier, system integrator, testing, MFG to work together to deliver reliable backplane solutions at scale.

### Reinventing the Backplane: Why AI Demands an Active Approach
Speakers: Chris Blackburn (Astera Labs), Matthew Ho (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7444/
Many AI rack architectures leverage passive cable backplanes to create scale-up network fabric, linking together GPUs/XPUs with fabric switches. AI architectures are evolving to increase the number of accelerators in the scale-up domain, increasing the quantity and physical length of the connections. At the same time, the protocol landscape with PCIe, Ethernet, and UALink is targeting higher frequencies. These trends make it challenging for passive cable backplanes to support next-gen architectures. Astera Labs and TE Connectivity have partnered to implement signal conditioning devices in the cable backplane cartridges, solving many of these challenges. A functional demonstration of this technology will be shown at the exhibitors' booths. This session will dive into the trends and challenges of passive cable backplane architecture and cover the implementation details of an active cable backplane solution. Tradeoffs for mechanical, thermal, and signal integrity will be discussed.

### Super Cluster Builder: A Tool for Topology Design and Visualization, Complexity Analysis, TCO Estimation, and Performance Assessment for GPU Superclusters
Speakers: Siamak Tavallaei (Samsung), Ardavan Sherafat (Cal Poly Pomona)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6847/
As AI workloads scale rapidly, the need for designing and evaluating compute infrastructures with up to 1M GPUs becomes evident. The Super Cluster Builder is a comprehensive tool and methodology for designing and analyzing large-scale GPU superclusters. It supports exploration of fully-connected and hierarchical designs—while providing insights into architectural, performance, and cost trade-offs. It enables users to model interconnect configurations using various physical layers (e.g., copper, photonic), visualize topology diagrams, and quantify the number and variety of components such as switches and cables. It includes detailed complexity analysis covering hop-counts, peer-to-peer bandwidth, cross-sectional bandwidth, and latency metrics, which are essential for accurate performance prediction. Additionally, the tool facilitates total cost of ownership (TCO) analysis by correlating infrastructure complexity with cost drivers, including hardware density and cable specifications.

### New Strategic Initiative: Open Cluster Designs for AI
Speakers: Lowell Lamb (Broadcom), Loren Staley (Celestica)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7341/
The rapid growth of the AI/ML market has fostered the emergence of new categories of equipment and network-infrastructure designs. Integration of these components into reliable, high-performance systems is a key problem in the industry. To address this challenge, this new Strategic Initiative will focus on the development of standardized designs for Open Pod Groups and Open Clusters. These designs will promote the adoption of current best practices and will rely on OCP platform and component contributions wherever possible. Solutions for scale-up and scale-out networks will be in-scope, and both physical and logical topology will be in-scope. Modularity will be a fundamental objective in these designs, thus simplifying network scaling. Management solutions will also be in-scope and will leverage the OCP management specifications. Join us to learn more and contribute to this vital community effort.

### NeoClouds vs Hyperscalers: 1MW Racks, 1% Budget, and the Battle for the AI Margin Stack
Speakers: Jonmichael Hands (FarmGPU), Rohit Mittal (UpscaleAI), Dylan Patel (SemiAnalysis), Andy Hock (Cerebras)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6830/
As AI pushes power and cooling past 1MW per rack, the battle for scale and margins intensifies. NeoClouds like CoreWeave and FulcrumCloud challenge hyperscaler dominance with leaner builds—but can they match Google's 400V racks or Cerebras’ vertically integrated AI clouds? Meanwhile, hardware vendors bypass OEMs, and even cooling becomes a strategic lever. This session explores whether true differentiation lies in chips, racks, or full-stack services—and how OCP’s open, interoperable designs reshape who owns the AI margin stack. Is tightly integrated infra still a hyperscaler privilege, or can startups thrive with agility, openness, and sovereign-ready infrastructure?

### Technical Aspects of Ultra Ethernet 1.0
Speakers: J Metz (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7303/ | materials: https://ultraethernet.org/
The recent publication of Ultra Ethernet 1.0 is an ambitious project of tuning the Ethernet stack to accommodate AI and HPC workloads. It covers everything from physical layer to software APIs. What makes it different? How does it work? This session explains the whys, hows, and whats of UEC 1.0 and describes the future of high-performance networking.

### Scale-out Ethernet Architectures for AI Networks
Speakers: Bhaskar Chinni (Broadcom), Arihant Jain (Arista Networks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7435/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI
The rapid growth of AI/ML networks demands innovative, scalable networking solutions. Key requirements include low-entropy, long-lived data flows; mitigation of incast from synchronized training bursts; and full support for RDMA, necessitating lossless transport. Scalability within the rack and compatibility with diverse deployments—air-cooled, DC, and open rack—are also critical. This presentation explores scale-out Ethernet architectures that enable horizontal scaling through distributed nodes. It focuses on how these architectures address the performance and operational needs of next-generation AI/ML workloads. In addition, we highlight how the OCP SAI (Switch Abstraction Interface) project is evolving to enable these architectures by standardizing programmable, AI-ready switch behavior across diverse platforms.

### CSIG: Congestion Signaling in the AI Era
Speakers: Abhiram Ravi (Google), Jai Kumar (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7196/
Modern AI workloads send millisecond-duration bursts of traffic, and their progress is limited by network bottlenecks. Because today’s limited network congestion signals cannot capture detailed fabric bottleneck conditions, they limit congestion control’s responsiveness and the quality of load balancing. We describe Congestion Signaling (CSIG), now under standardization at UEC, supported by multiple switch vendors, and deployed at Google. CSIG provides always-on, line-rate telemetry in application context, including sub-millisecond-granularity switch traffic measurements, and fine-grained, per-packet feedback for congestion control that captures the congestion state and location of the bottleneck along a path. We present results from Google’s CSIG deployment, including performance improvements to congestion control and load balancing, and examples showing how CSIG telemetry uniquely facilitates understanding real-world workloads’ behavior.

### Enable IBGDA support in a GPU-agnostic manner for open AI systems
Speakers: Eddie Wai (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7433/
RDMA has been the technology used to enable high performance networking for AI scale-out interconnects. The advancement in IBGDA (InfiniBand GPUDirect Async) has shown improvement in latency and message rate by allowing GPUs to directly initiate RDMA transactions from one endpoint to another. However, the technology has mostly been limited to Nvidia GPUs and InfiniBand networks. In this presentation, we want to share our experience in extending this service (IBGDA) in a GPU-agnostic manner for open AI systems using Ethernet NICs running RoCE (RDMA over Converged Ethernet). We will discuss some of the challenges we encountered and how the service can be optimized for OCP AI systems and GPU platforms.

### Server Scale-Up Interconnect Technologies - UALink SuperNode and CXL Memory Pool
Speakers: Vincent Kong (Alibaba)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7137/ | materials: http://cighao.com/papers/PolarDB_CXL.pdf
This presentation will introduce datacenter Scale-Up interconnect technologies, including super-node architectures and products related to UALink (GPU) and CXL (CPU). The key contents are as follows: UALink: From Cloud's perspective, the design, features, and value of the UALink protocol will be analyzed. -Key features of the UALink protocol -Data center cluster architectures CXL: The presentation will detail server memory pooling solution. This system provides high-capacity, high-performance hierarchical memory pooling based on CXL technology.It offers 10TB-level memory capacity and enables CXL tiered memory pool solutions with varying performance tiers. Database workloads on CXL mempry pool work: SIGMOD 2025 Best Paper, "Unlocking the Potential of CXL for Disaggregated Memory in Cloud-Native Databases"

### The Scale-Up Ethernet (SUE) Framework for AI/ML Accelerators
Speakers: Mohan Kalkunte (Broadcom), Hugh Holbrook (Arista Networks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7078/ | materials: https://docs.broadcom.com/doc/scale-up-ethernet-framework
AI/ML workloads in Scale-Up networks require low latency, high bandwidth, lossless communication across tightly-coupled XPU clusters. Traditional network interfaces are area-intensive and limit scalability. The OCP-contributed Scale-Up Ethernet (SUE) Framework is an Ethernet-based AI/ML architecture designed for efficient implementation. SUE standardizes a memory semantics XPU interface and enables a lightweight transport, leveraging AI Forwarding Headers (AFH), Credit-Based Flow Control (CBFC), and Link Layer Retry (LLR) to ensure deterministic performance and low tail latency. Aligned with OCP’s mission of open infrastructure, SUE enables scale-up clusters to use merchant Ethernet silicon and open protocols. SUE is seamlessly supported by open OSes such as SONiC. In this session, we present how the SUE framework enables efficient scale-up interconnects for AI/ML clusters, simplifies deployment, and advances the vision of open, Ethernet-based Scale-Up AI.

### Performance Evaluation of Interconnect Technologies for AI Scale-Up Computing: UAL vs UALoE/SUE vs RoCE
Speakers: Srihari Vegesna (Upscale AI), Srinivas Gangam (Upscale AI)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7262/
AI workloads increasingly demand low-latency, high-bandwidth interconnects. This paper compares Ultra Accelerator Link (UAL), Ethernet-based UALoE/SUE, and RoCE for AI-optimized data transfer. We evaluate transaction-level load balancing, DMA thread efficiency, and bandwidth utilization for memory-semantic XPU workloads. UAL outperforms in small transfer scenarios, leveraging deterministic latency and memory transaction-based load balancing. Ethernet protocols, in contrast, suffer from variable latency and inefficient handling of small packets, limiting their effectiveness in scale-up AI systems.

### Unleashing AI Scale-Up: UALink and OCP Collaboration
Speakers: Kurtis Bowman (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7330/
The rapid proliferation of AI and HPC workloads demands unprecedented levels of interconnect performance, scalability, and efficiency within data centers. Proprietary solutions have historically limited choice and driven up costs. The UALink Consortium, formed by leading industry players, is tackling this challenge head-on by developing an open industry standard for high-performance accelerator-to-accelerator communication. This session will delve into the newly released UALink 1.0 Specification, highlighting its architectural innovations designed for low-latency, high-bandwidth communication among hundreds to thousands of AI accelerators within a single pod. We will discuss how UALink enables simple load, store, and atomic operations with software coherency, crucial for maximizing AI model training and inference efficiency. We will detail the strategic collaboration between the UALink Consortium and the Open Compute Project (OCP) Foundation.

### Applications and Use Cases for UALink: Enabling Efficient, Open Networking for AI Scale-Up Systems
Speakers: Rohit Mittal (UpscaleAI), Chris Petersen (Astera Labs), Josh Collier (Intel), Priyank Shukla (Synopsys)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7408/
As AI compute demands continue to rise, Cloud Service Providers, System OEMs, and IP/Silicon Providers require a high-performance, efficient solution to meet the challenges of scale-up AI infrastructure. The UALink Specification delivers a low-latency, high-bandwidth interconnect designed to enable fast, efficient communication between accelerators and switches within AI computing pods, boosting performance while improving power and cost efficiency. In this panel, UALink experts will explore real-world applications and use cases for the technology, highlighting how UALink supports the development of next-generation AI/ML scale-up systems. The discussion will also cover the importance of supply chain diversity and system interoperability enabled by UALink. Attendees will gain insight into the specification roadmap and upcoming enhancements to UALink technology.

### Scale Up and Scale Out AI Fabrics: A Polymorphic Ethernet Architecture for 'Systems of System'
Speakers: Jai Kumar (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7320/ | materials: https://docs.broadcom.com/doc/scale-up-ethernet-framework
Understand the competing and conflicting requirements of scale-up and scale-out AI fabrics, and how to converge them using Ethernet technology to create a robust system of systems. Scale Up fabrics are optimized for inference, prioritizing low-latency and efficient bandwidth for a unified GPU memory domain. These single-tier fabrics are designed for resilience, fault tolerance, and minimal transport overhead. Conversely, Scale Out fabrics are used for training large language models. They are multi-tiered and manage distributed GPU domains, addressing concerns like incast/outcast, congestion control, multipathing, and remote memory access. In this presentation we will delve into details like memory vs network semantics, protocol overhead, latency considerations , fabric topology and congestion control algorithms using a standard ethernet technology to create a polymorphic architecture.

### Beyond the Spec: What it Takes to Deploy UALink at Rack-Scale
Speakers: Ahmad Danesh (Astera Labs), Chris Petersen (Astera Labs)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7410/ | materials: https://www.asteralabs.com/building-the-case-for-ualink-a-dedicated-scale-up-memory-semantic-fabric/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4bItCOsWQh0
Momentum is accelerating for UALink, with the Consortium now exceeding 100 member companies and the release of the UALink 200G 1.0 specification in April 2025. However, delivering a truly robust ecosystem requires far more than a specification. As demand in scale-up AI grows, hyperscalers and cloud service providers are asking a pressing question: what can we do to accelerate deployment of a rack-scale UALink solution? This session will explore what it takes to bring a new fabric technology like UALink to life, including essential silicon, system designs, RAS features, comprehensive management software, and compliance/interoperability testing. Together with our partners, Astera Labs will offer a practical perspective on the critical workstreams and best practices that will transform UALink from a promising standard into deployed hardware and software infrastructure.

### Enhancing Optical Link Reliability for AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Susu He (Meta), Han Wang (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7381/
Optical link reliability is critical to sustaining AI workload efficiency and optimizing TCO. The talk introduces a comprehensive framework for enhancing E2E optical link reliability, specifically on the focus on reducing Annual Interruption Rate in large-scale AI environments. This initiative is a proactive approach for early failure detection in 100G/L+ optical modules. We propose a set of optics performance metrics - covering real-time optics health metrics, error correction & SI metrics which are further reinforced by trend-based reliability indicators, enabling predictive data-driven models that detect link degradation patterns before failures occur. Specifically, we use machine learning to forecast potential failure of optical links, ensuring robust monitoring and proactive maintenance across full lifecycle of optical modules. This framework offers a path to significantly lower link interruption rates, driving improved uptime, resilience, and performance in hyperscale AI infra.

### Scaling-up Next-gen AI with Optics
Speakers: Taylor Groves (Lightmatter)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7390/ | materials: https://www.eetimes.com/lightmatter-unveils-3d-co-packaged-optics-for-256-tbps-in-one-package/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Rnwguy1Af_I
Growth in scale-up networking is forcing companies to move away from electrical networksand towards optical networks. In this special focus on AI, we examine how optical scale-up can benefit training and inference workloads. We explore the tradeoffs of different approaches to optical integration (LPO, CPO and 3D Integrated) and the potential role that OCP can play in driving open standards within the ecosystem.

### e-Tube AI Interconnect - Leaping the Copper Cliff And Avoid the Optical Penalties to Scale Up AI Clusters
Speakers: Sean Park (Point2 Technology)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6841/ | materials: https://point2tech.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/240710-E-Tube-Whitepaper-clean-update-final.pdf
The increasing computational demands of AI workloads require high-performance, scalable interconnect solutions to maximize compute efficiency while minimizing power consumption and cost. e-Tube RF data transmission over plastic dielectric waveguide provides a groundbreaking alternative to copper and optical interconnects, offering superior power efficiency, lower cost, and dramatically reduced latency. Built with mature semiconductor process and connectorization derived from copper twin-ax, e-Tube enables 10x longer length than copper, 3× better power efficiency, 3× lower cost, and 1000× lower latency vs. optical tech. e-Tube is well-suited to replace copper cabling for short-reach top-of-rack (ToR) to server and cross-rack interconnects that are critical for accelerating AI workloads. e-Tube will initially deploy with 200G SerDes speeds, supporting 1.6T/3.2T MSA-compliant OSFP or QSFP-DD form factors, followed by near-packaged and co-packaged form factors.

### Data Compression for AI
Speakers: Yann Collet (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7536/
Discover the future of data compression at the OCP Global Summit! Our team has been working on a novel data compression library that leverages knowledge of data formats to deliver superior compression ratios and speeds. This is especially effective for AI workloads, were it outperforms existing compression tools like Zstandard. This library is already deployed at scale within Meta, processing vast amounts of data every day, we're now inviting the community to participate in shaping its v1.0 specification. Join us to learn more about this game-changing technology and how you can contribute to its development.

### Capacity Aware Adaptive Routing
Speakers: Yikai Lin (Meta), Midhun Somasundaran (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6897/ | materials: https://github.com/facebook/fboss
As backend networks continue to evolve and grow exponentially, Meta has been working on optimizing the routing design for its next-generation backend network architecture. Our current Adaptive Routing Scheme which relies on local load awareness, has limitations when applied to our larger topologies. The presence of parallel links and usage of shallow buffer switches can lead to congestion and decreased performance, particularly when remote links fail and reduce capacity without losing reachability. To address these challenges, we have developed a new approach that combines remote capacity information with local-congestion-aware spray. This solution dynamically adjusts the load balancing scheme based on end-to-end network capacity, ensuring more efficient traffic distribution and minimizing the impact of link failures on network performance. We also developed algorithms for efficiently merging ARS groups and leveraged non load aware ECMP spray groups to address scaling challenges.

### Optimizing AI Networks with Advanced Congestion Management
Speakers: Mohammad Hanif (Broadcom), Ajay Chhatwal (Arista Networks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7423/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI
AI networks demand a high-performance, lossless fabric to deliver optimal job completion times. Unlike traditional workloads, AI workloads exhibit unique communication patterns and sensitivity to latency and congestion, requiring advanced congestion management mechanisms. AttendIn this session, we will explore state-of-the-art congestion notification mechanisms designed to meet the demands of AI infrastructure. We will explain how the latest innovations such as expedited congestion notifications back to the sender (BTS), PFC-Aware ECN, and Packet Trimming can significantly improve network efficiency and workload performance. Additionally, we will also go over the SAI specification updates and share performance data.

### Analysis of flow engineering and balancing options on AI fabrics
Speakers: Danny Hanson (Supermicro), Kamini Santhanagopalan (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7383/
This session will help the attendee gain a better understanding of the background in packet handling on a dense AI Ethernet cluster. We will also review some best practice snippets available in open SONiC releases to engineer the best fabric. Comparisons to general industry terminology along with what is level of open applicability and/or nearest open alternative will be given that will achieve best performance on the cluster. We will add specific focus on Dynamic Load Balancing in the context of an alternative to traditional methods today.

### Design, Build and Test an OCP AI Network with Industry Leading Performance
Speakers: Matt Roman (Celestica), Jonmichael Hands (FarmGPU)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7471/ | materials: https://docs.farmgpu.net/docs/Executive_Summary
FarmGPU built a new AI cloud service with OCP network hardware from Celestica and OCP network software from Hedgehog in less than 90 days. These OCP members will present test results comparing FarmGPU’s AI network performance to proprietary infrastructure benchmarks for common AI workloads. FarmGPU CEO JM Hands has been an OCP member and storage project leader since 20XX. Celestica has been a platinum OCP member since 2019 and designed their DS5000 800GbE ethernet switch in collaboration with the OCP networking project. Hedgehog was born at OCP Global Summit 2022 building their open source AI network software on OCP SONiC and CNCF Kubernetes as a member of OCP, The Linux Foundation and Cloud Native Computing Foundation. FarmGPU built a new AI cloud service with OCP networking from Celestica and Hedgehog in less than 90 days. These OCP members will present test results comparing FarmGPUs OCP network for AI cloud services to Infiniband benchmarks.

### Welcome Address & Overview
Speakers: Rob Coyle (Open Compute Project Foundation (OCP)), James Kelly (Open Compute Project Foundation (OCP))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7705/

### Meta AI/ML System Error Handling Improvements - PCIe Completion Timeout error handling using RPPIO error reporting
Speakers: Anil Agrawal (Meta), Sukay Luhadia (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7525/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TpNNeMGEsdU
Meta's existing AI/ML infrastructure is built using Grand Teton Training (GTT) platform and contains a complex hierarchy of PCIe components including host CPU, PCIe switches, and end devices. In such a large cluster of GTT machines, PCIe completion timeout (CTO) events occur frequently resulting in job interruptions. In this presentation we will share our learnings of how we used Root Port PIO (RP_PIO) error reporting methods to improve the diagnosis and failure remediation to reduce subsequent job interruptions.

### Platform Efficiency Challenges and the OCP Opportunity: Context-Aware AI/ML Agents on DC-SCM for Real-Time Optimization and Security
Speakers: Raghu Kondapalli (Axiado Corporation), sundaram Arumugasundaram (Axiado Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7463/
Server/platform efficiency encompasses energy use, resource utilization, security, thermal management, workload distribution, and system health. However, recent platform studies highlight low infrastructure utilization across servers, racks, switches, and pods. GPU utilization is around 60%, with CPUs and networks similarly affected. Since post-deployment efficiency remains underemphasized, OCP is well-positioned to drive standardization. To improve ROI, the industry needs standardized metrics, real-time monitoring, and automated corrective systems. Context-aware resource management and continuous security assessments are essential. AI/ML solutions can synthesize contextual metrics for actionable insights and enable real-time platform adjustments. At 2024 OCP Brisbane, we shared a thermal management AI/ML model running on a DC-SCM, saving up to 50% of cooling power; Now, it predicts GPU energy for ML workloads. We also developed an LLM-based model for live security threat mitigation.

### Unified AIOps for Remote Management of Heterogeneous Open-Source AI Systems
Speakers: Muthukkumaran Ramalingam (AMI), Rami Radi (American Megatrends (AMI))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7050/
Modern AI infrastructure demands more than hardware—it requires intelligent coordination across open-source firmware, diverse platforms, and fragmented telemetry. Multi-vendor telemetry is normalized through AIOps pipelines, enabling predictive analytics to detect GPU degradation, hotspots, or inefficiencies. Automated remediation includes firmware patching, workload migration, and adaptive cooling. An AI chatbot simplifies operations via natural language. The session also explores closed-loop optimization that links workload behavior with infrastructure conditions to improve performance-per-watt and reduce carbon impact. Aligned with OCP’s values of openness, modularity, and sustainability, this model supports scalable, intelligent AI infrastructure management.

### Turbocharging Firmware Development and Deployment using Agentic AI
Speakers: Chitrak Gupta (American Megatrends (AMI)), Sudan Varadachari Ayanam (AMI)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7051/
The rapid evolution of server technologies and firmware complexity demands a shift from manual coding to intelligent automation. This session presents a novel framework using Generative AI to streamline platform code generation for server firmware orchestration. It enables automated creation of platform porting files, Redfish schemas for OpenBMC, and UEFI debug log analysis from simple prompts. Integrated test case agents validate generated code against system specs. Real-world examples demonstrate how Agentic AI drives efficiency, accelerates open standard adoption, and reduces time to market for ODM/OEM platforms.

### Cool IT!: Comparing Power and Throughput Performance of Liquid and Air cooled AI nodes
Speakers: Imran Latif (JCI), Muhammad Ali Shafique (ISCAAS Lab)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7034/
We evaluate the effect of cooling techniques on AI infrastructure performance through direct comparison of liquid-cooled versus air-cooled server configurations. Across model training, fine-tuning, and stress tests, we find liquid cooling increases computational throughput while reducing node-level power consumption up to 1.5 kW. Liquid-cooled systems maintain graphics processing units (GPU) temperatures 15-25°C lower than air-cooled counterparts, enabling sustained performance without thermal throttling. Most significantly, efficiency gains scale with computational intensity—modest for low-utilization tasks but substantial during high-intensity workloads. These findings establish the impact of cooling system on processor performance, offering measurable improvements in performance per watt, operational costs, and thermal stability that become increasingly critical to minimizing energy and water footprint as model complexity grows.

### Power Virus and Characterization solution from AI accelerator to system
Speakers: Jinghan (Jeremy) Yang (Meta), Samu Chakki (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6715/
With the fast growth of AI model, there is a continuous requirement to develop a high power AI accelerator/ GPU systems, racks and clusters. There are significant challenges on power, thermal, and the chip to system level stability, reliability. Furthermore, as we continue to evolve AI models and optimize the product use cases, it’s important we constantly evaluate power & performance modeling and characterization to demonstrate the perf/W analysis. In this presentation, we would like to share the strategy and work over power virus, workload evaluation, telemetry and monitoring, test orchestration for power characterization and data analytics which help to understand the at-scale impact. We call for industry collaboration to invest into tooling, testing and framework to better evaluate power, perf evaluation.

### Exploring Energy Efficiency in Scientific and Industrial AI Workloads
Speakers: Akshay Sharma (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory), Won Young Park (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6906/
Understanding and managing the energy consumption of emerging AI applications remains a significant challenge, despite extensive research on data center power usage and advances in software and hardware technologies. This presentation explores power management opportunities in large-scale scientific and industrial AI workloads. We analyze how selected AI models respond to energy-saving techniques such as power capping and dynamic voltage and frequency scaling (DVFS). By evaluating the trade-offs between performance and energy efficiency, we aim to provide insights that support more effective energy management strategies in data centers.

### AI in Manufacturing: Weighing Energy Footprints Against Efficiency Gains
Speakers: Jibran Zuberi (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7501/
The growing energy demands of AI systems have drawn increasing attention. However, deploying advanced AI in manufacturing presents significant potential for improving efficiency, reducing costs, and enhancing sustainability, surpassing traditional control systems. By leveraging deep learning and predictive analytics, AI can optimize processes, detect anomalies, and enable real-time decision-making. This study assesses the net energy impact of AI in manufacturing through a comprehensive literature review and a detailed case study in the cement industry, where AI services are currently being deployed. It examines energy savings alongside the electricity use of AI infrastructure, modeling various server configurations and conducting a sensitivity analysis. Despite concerns about AI's energy footprint, the findings suggest that its broader environmental and economic benefits in manufacturing may outweigh this footprint, offering valuable insights for future adoption and policy development.

### From AC to DC: The Future of High-Density, AI-Ready Data Centers
Speakers: Razvan Panati (Bloom Energy)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7567/ | materials: https://www.capacitymedia.com/article/2ex0zv1xde2askochifi8/partner-content/the-power-gap-how-data-centres-can-overcome-their-biggest-limiting-factor | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=W1C6m7juRX4
As AI workloads grow, data centers are evolving to support unprecedented power densities—up to 1 megawatt per rack, far beyond traditional levels. This shift demands a rethinking of power delivery systems. Conventional AC-based infrastructure involves multiple conversion steps, resulting in significant energy losses—often between 11% and 20%. A promising alternative is the adoption of direct current (DC) power architectures, such as +/-400VDC, which can deliver electricity more efficiently and directly to high-density racks. On-site DC power generation at the required voltage could eliminate many conversion stages, leading to 10–15% energy savings, reduced capital costs, and increased usable space. This transition signals a potential shift in data center design. In this session, industry veteran Razvan Panati explores the transformative potential of direct current (DC) power—specifically the emerging +/-400VDC standard—as a more efficient, scalable, and sustainable alternative.

### Project Deschutes: Google’s Open Spec CDU contribution to the industry
Speakers: Jorge Padilla (Google), Qi Wang (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7317/
AI is fundamentally transforming the compute landscape, demanding unprecedented advances in data center infrastructure. At Google, we believe that physical infrastructure — the power, cooling, and mechanical systems that underpin everything — isn’t just important, but critical to AI’s continued scaling. At Google, we’ve deployed liquid cooling at GigaWatt scale across more than 2000 TPU Pods in the past seven years with remarkable uptime — consistently at about 99.999%. Google first used liquid cooling in TPU v3 that was deployed in 2018. Google plans to contribute their fifth-generation cooling distribution unit, Project Deschutes, to OCP, helping to accelerate adoption of liquid cooling industry-wide. We will share design details and context around, design for high cooling performance, manufacturing quality, reliability and uptime, deployment velocity, serviceability and operational excellence, supply ecosystem advancements

### Enablement of Liquid Cooled GB200 At Scale
Speakers: Wenying Zhang (Meta), Cheng Chen (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7483/
In 2025, Meta has launched Liquid Cooled GB200 hardware at scale. It is an important expansion of Meta’s AI hardware capacity, while the technical, quality and productivity requirements are something we’ve never addressed before, especially on liquid cooled hardware. This presentation introduces the challenges we encountered in development of GB200 with regard to liquid cooling implementation, and how we designed the technical requirements and workflow process, to ensure the products are meeting the performance and quality expectations before landing at data centers. It also touches on the considerations on transportation, coolant health maintenance, and leakage handling in the field. With those dedicated efforts, we were able to deliver GB200 racks at the speed of multi-hundred racks every week, and limit the impact of liquid-based issues to below statistical significance.

### Enabling 1MW Data Center Racks through Innovations in Power and Liquid Cooling
Speakers: Stephanie Schmidt (Flex Ltd), Jim Nelson (Flex)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7181/ | materials: https://www.fierce-network.com/cloud/data-center-pulse-1mw-racks-are-way
As AI workloads scale, next-generation data centers require integrated solutions for power, liquid cooling, and services that can support over 1MW per IT rack. New power architectures combine high-voltage direct current (HVDC) power distribution with modular power components capable of supplying 1.2MW in an ORv3 standard rack, including AC and DC PDUs, power shelves, and energy storage shelves. These components enable efficient, scalable delivery of high current at reduced losses. For liquid cooling, end-of-rack coolant distribution units (CDU) manage flow and return across multiple nodes, enabling rack-level thermal design capacities exceeding 2MW. Together, these solutions enable efficient, scalable delivery of high current at reduced losses while re-defining the thermal-electrical envelope for AI-optimized infrastructure. This work is aligned to the open systems for AI working group.

### Near-GPU Storage: Requirements for Accelerating Storage to Scale AI Workloads
Speakers: Ta-Yu Wu (Meta), Ehsan Ardestani (Facebook)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6848/ | materials: https://drive.google.com/file/d/1iOVkgw1Hf-bdrHA8Vk5bqC5MqYOdmyOU/view?usp=sharing
As AI workloads and the hardware powering these workloads continue to scale and evolve; the need for a highly performant and flexible storage solution near the GPU is becoming apparent in order to design scalable systems that are able to serve a broader range of AI use cases. Specifically, having more performant and flexible storage building blocks are fundamental to achieving that goal. This presentation outlines the focus areas and landing zone for a new swimlane of “near-GPU” storage to serve as those building blocks so storage can be an enabler for scaling AI systems and workloads.

### Revisiting Jim Gray’s 5-Minute Rule in the Storage Next Era: A First-Principles Perspective
Speakers: Tong Zhang (ScaleFlux), Vikram Sharma Mailthody (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6929/ | pdf: https://dl.acm.org/doi/pdf/10.1145/3575693.3575748
The famous 5-minute rule, introduced in 1985 by Jim Gray, the legendary database pioneer, guided data placement by balancing memory cost against disk access savings. This talk presents a first-principles rethinking of the rule, recognizing that assumptions like infinite DRAM bandwidth and negligible CPU/GPU cost for I/O no longer hold. We introduce a new analytical model grounded in real hardware constraints. The framework is broadly applicable, especially to modern AI-centric workloads such as inference, graph neural networks, and large-scale data analytics, where massive working sets often exceed DRAM capacity. The Storage Next initiative, aiming to harness NAND flash as an active tier for GPU workloads, makes this rethinking especially timely. Co-presented by ScaleFlux and Nvidia, the session offers a quantitative foundation for evaluating memory vs. storage trade-offs and guiding caching decisions in future computing systems.

### Storage Benchmarking and I/O Characterization for Next-Generation AI workloads
Speakers: Devasena Inupakutika (Samsung Semiconductor Inc.), Javier Gonzalez (Samsung)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7517/
The AI landscape has evolved from compute-focused training workloads to include storage-intensive applications such as vectorDBs, large language model inference, and generative AI systems, where traditional storage architectures encounter challenges in serving both large sequential datasets and low-latency small read patterns. Through eBPF-based profiling, MLPerf storage benchmarking extensions, and real-workload analysis, this research discusses the use-cases for VectorDB operations consisting of small random reads and the system needs of LLM KV cache systems. This talk presents a comprehensive workload analysis and benchmarking methodology that encompasses vector similarity search for major platforms, and KV cache management for large parameter models workload characterization. This research provides actionable insights for AI infrastructure architects, storage system engineers, and practitioners seeking to optimize next-generation storage systems.

### Heterogeneous Memory Opportunity with Agentic AI and Memory Centric Computing
Speakers: Jinin So (Samsung Electronics), Khurram Malik (Marvell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6832/
Limited HBM capacity is a critical bottleneck for scaling LLM inference services on GPU systems. This memory capacity constraint on model size, KV cache, and RAG vector databases creates a severe imbalance with the relatively enough GPU's compute and memory bandwidth resources. This talk analyzes emerging 2nd tier memory solutions, including SOCAMM, MRDIMM, and CXL memory, as a means to overcome this memory wall. We will present experimental results on DGX GPU systems that validate the effectiveness of these technologies in a GPU system. Furthermore, we will show with experimental data how the CPU-GPU interconnect becomes the next bottleneck. To address this, we will conclude by sharing Samsung's vision for Processing-Near-Memory (PNM) technology with PIM as a fundamental solution to this data transfer challenge.

### Towards memory efficient RAG pipelines with CXL technology
Speakers: Arun George (Samsung Semiconductor India Research, Bangalore), Kyumin Park (Samsung Electronics (Semiconductor))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6967/
Various stages in the RAG pipeline of AI-Inference involve large volumes of data. Specifically, the data preparation stage to create and insert embeddings into a Vector DB requires significant transient memory. Furthermore, the search phase-depending on the sizes of the index trees, parallel queries, etc.- also result in an increased memory consumption. We observe that the peak memory consumption is dependent on the load of RAG pipeline, such as insertions or any transient behaviors. Thus, we find that having local memory attached to meet the peak usage is inefficient. To improve the efficiency of RAG pipeline under these scenarios, we propose the use of CXL memory to meet the high memory challenges while reducing the statically provisioned local memory. In specific, we explore two approaches to reduce locally attached memory costs: 1.) CXL Memory Pooling: Provisioning memory based on transient needs. 2.) CXL Memory Tiering: Using cheaper, larger capacity memory.

### Next-Gen AI/HPC Server Performance with CXL3.1 Tiered Memory and MRDIMM solution
Speakers: David Zhuo (Montage Technology)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7062/ | materials: https://symbioticlab.org/publications/files/tpp:asplos23/tpp-asplos23.pdf | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=NUmcE9BDvVE
AI and HPC servers push technological boundaries, and high memory capacity is crucial for their speed and performance. Memory capacity directly impacts how well a deep learning model can handle large-scale computations and deliver fast results. It is possible to page to/from CPU extended memory (far memory) with CXL NUMA node. This article will explore the next generation AI/HPC server market requirement, memory capacity and performance concerns with CXL NUMA node and MRDIMM solution. We will also review Meta’s CachLIB and Alibaba’s KVcache to see how “Big Memory Use Case with CXL Extended Memory Solution” and it’s POV (Prove of Value). AI and HPC servers need more memory capacity because they handle vast amounts of data and complex computations. For both the HPC servers (e.g., IMDB and DLRM) and the AI servers (e.g., training and inference), they have in common the fact that they run memory-intensive applications.

### Accelerating LLM Serving with Prompt Cache Offloading via CXL
Speakers: Don Moon (SK hynix), Taeho Hwang (SK hynix)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7065/ | materials: https://github.com/skhynix/LMCache/wiki/Accelerating-LLM-Serving-with-Prompt-Cache-Offloading-via-CXL
LLM inference uses a key-value (KV) cache to store intermediate activations for token-wise reuse, cutting redundant computation and latency. Prompt caching extends this across multi-turn or long-session workloads, but as user counts and sequence lengths grow, GPU HBM alone cannot sustain the full cache footprint, forcing offloads into higher-capacity media like host DRAM. However, host DRAM is limited in scalability. We address this by integrating CXL-attached memory via PCIe switches, unlocking multi-terabyte capacity beyond host limits. In ShareGPT benchmarks, augmenting a GPU with an additional 80GB of CXL memory (alongside host DRAM) raised prompt cache hit rates by 2.5x, reduced time-to-first-token by 32.9%, and boost overall throughput by 38.5%. This cost-effective tiered design delivers consistent low-latency responses under heavy load while notably reducing infrastructure costs , making LLM service deployment both faster and economical.

## Cooling Environments & Liquid Cooling

### Cooling Environments Panel
Speakers: John Fernandes (Meta), Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA), Cosimo Pecchioli (Castrol (BP))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6807/
As you may know, Projects represent the highest level of activity within OCP. By attending this session, you'll gain insight into the Cooling Environment project—its purpose, current focus, and key sub-projects: Door , Cold Plate , Immersion , Heat Reuse , and Advanced Cooling Facilities . In addition, the Cooling Environment project has launched two important initiatives: The High Velocity Erosion Test for PG25 Strategic coordination with ASHRAE Join the session to explore how these efforts are shaping the future of cooling technologies within the OCP ecosystem.

### Guidelines for Pre-Commision Preparation of Technology Cooling System (TCS) Row Manifolds in Liquid Cooled Data Centers
Speakers: Craig Myers (Nalco Water), Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6719/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-document-submission-guidelines-for-pre-commission-preparation-of-technology-cooling-system-tcs-row-manifolds-revq-pdf-1
Liquid cooled datacenter deployments require various procedures to ensure clean and correctly functioning secondary cooling loops for coolant to pass through to the racks. Pre-commissioning cleaning of the technology cooling system pipework is to ensure the system is brought to the appropriate level of cleanliness for commissioning, deployment, and ongoing operation of liquid cooled racks. Pre-commissioning steps described herein include: hydrotesting, cleaning, flushing, and preparation for commissioning. Steps and considerations for the development of a comprehensive plan to cover these activities are also provided. The primary audiences for this whitepaper are design and facility engineers and managers who will be responsible for the design, procurement, setup and operation of the TCS loop.

### Implications of Increased Flow Rates on Next-Generation Liquid Cooling Systems
Speakers: Bill Flaherty (Alloy Enterprises), Will Martin (nVent)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6998/
TDP for GPUs is projected to exceed 4,000 W in the near future. Rack power densities in data centers may approach 1 MW. These developments are driving operators to higher flow rates to manage escalating thermal loads. Pressure drop increases nonlinearly with flow rate, which could result in a 36× rise in pressure losses—impacting fitting selection, energy draw, and pump selection. Higher flow velocities also raise the risk of microchannel erosion and transition to turbulent flow regimes, which further compound pressure and reliability concerns. These increased flow rates and pressure drops also drive an increase in the scope and scale of required equipment on the infrastructure side. TCS manifolds, rack manifolds, and DTC liquid cooling units all require larger pipes, higher pressure delivery, and larger space claims. This paper seeks to quantify the impact of rising flow rates on thermal management systems and identify where innovation is required to overcome current limitations.

### OCP Cooling Environments (CE) & ASHRAE TC 9.9: Updates on Collaboration
Speakers: Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA), Mark Steinke (NVIDIA), Matt Koukl (AEI Affiliated Engineers), Cosimo Pecchioli (Castrol (BP))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6932/
Cooling Technology is moving rapidly due to the demands of the market. The OCP Cooling Environments Project and the ASHRAE TC9.9 are building closer ties between organizations to help our communities move forward more quickly. This panel will discuss progress made and our combined plans as we move into the future.

### Mega AALC for High Power Density AI Data centers
Speakers: Rajesh Kasukurthy (AMD), Yuzhou Lin (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7299/
The rise of high-performance AI chips and their integration into data centers have made Air-Assisted Liquid Cooling (AALC) a compelling hybrid solution. To meet increasing thermal demands, AALC is evolving toward double and multi-rack-wide sidecars with large liquid-to-air heat exchangers. This presentation outlines a design and validation framework for such systems, focusing on airflow, pipe routing, and serviceability. It introduces enhanced test metrics—approach temperature, LPM/kW, and CFM/kW—that better reflect real-world performance than traditional P-Q curves. The framework guides the OCP community and broader industry in evaluating scalable, maintainable cooling architectures. It also examines the impact of wide-format AALC on airflow management, aisle compatibility, and logistics. Applying these principles early helps reduce integration risks, improve deployment efficiency, and generate actionable data for future designs.

### Total Cost of Ownership model for liquid-cooled datacenter
Speakers: Eduard Roytman (Microsoft), Andy Young (Asperitas)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7161/ | materials: https://drive.google.com/open?id=17CbKZ4f1Ybcj5QVsdjmrx698YuNYT-bk&usp=drive_fs
Explosive growth in AI drives demand for rack power density increases by more than 10X. Datacenter fleet is slow to adapt and projects air-cooled remains majority over next 5+ years while industry leaders pivot to investment in Direct to Chip Liquid, Immersion, Hybrid and other types of cooling, augmenting air-cooling at IT rack level. At a datacenter level there is growth in modular Liquid-to-Air and Liquid-to-Liquid heat exchangers allowing liquid-cooling in both air and liquid cooled facilities. OCP TCO Workstream developed Open and Transparent TCO tool to help model and compare CAPEX, OPEX and CO2 footprint of different power and cooling strategies. The tool will increase confidence and help make informed and fact-based decisions. This presentation showcases the latest capabilities of OCP TCO tool and presents a study of retrofitting liquid-cooled AI clusters into air-cooled datacenters and discusses CAPEX and OPEX implications and opportunities.

### PG25 High Velocity Erosion Test
Speakers: Cosimo Pecchioli (Castrol (BP))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6808/
ASHRAE’s 1.5 m/s velocity limit in piping systems aims to reduce erosion but increasingly constrains thermal system design as heat loads rise. To meet higher thermal design power (TDP) demands, designers must either lower coolant temperatures—an energy-intensive option—or increase flow rates by raising fluid velocity. The latter is more energy-efficient. To evaluate erosion risk at elevated velocities, the Open Compute Project and bp/Castrol launched a study using Castrol PG25 coolant across copper, stainless steel, and EPDM pipes. Early results focus on copper at 6 m/s, the most erosion-prone scenario, with further testing underway for other materials and flow conditions.

### Integrating Aluminum Heat Exchangers in Modern Data Centers
Speakers: Mohamad Hnayno (OVHcloud), Dominik Sporna (Valeo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7086/
This session delves into the adoption of aluminum heat exchangers for data center cooling, tackling long-standing concerns such as corrosion and system-level compatibility. Through a collaborative effort between Valeo, OVHCloud, and ecosystem partners, the project explores the use of advanced coating technologies and integration techniques to improve reliability, thermal performance, and energy efficiency. The workstream focuses on developing actionable guidelines and validating solutions through extensive testing. Attendees will gain an inside view into how joint innovation is enabling the seamless deployment of aluminum-based components and advancing sustainability in modern data center infrastructure.

### Cold Plate Subproject Update
Speakers: Emily Clark (Dell), Berhanu Wondimu (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7499/
This presentation is devised to provide updates on cold plate community driven activities and contributions this year and future plans

### PBMC (Pivoting Blind Mate Coupling)– Liquid Cooling
Speakers: Glenn Charest (Meta), Nick Goenner (SafeWay Custom Fluid Transfer)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6818/
This presentation will provide an update on the development effort for a new blind mate valve design, PBMC (Pivoting Blind Mate Coupling). PBMC is being developed as a new workstream in the Blind Mate CLA group in collaboration with multiple suppliers to support deployment at scale and provide an interoperable solution. This new valve design will have significantly higher flow capability as compared to BMQC to support higher power systems while working to minimize the form factor. The team will outline the design and how the design adapts to misalignment, basic design requirements, performance targets,, and development status updates since we last shared updates at the Regional Summit.

### Impact of Die Warpage Changes on TIM Thermal Performance
Speakers: Yin Hang (Meta), Willie Chen (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7119/
TIM thermal resistance is typically measured on flat test platforms per ASTM D5470. However, bare die chips in real systems often warp, causing major gaps between standard test data and actual performance. In some cases, the same TIM showed performance differences of tens of percent across platforms due to die surface variation. By analyzing ASIC and HBM junction temperatures relative to GPU temperature, we observed that initially concave dies tend to flatten during operation. These results suggest that conventional testers and thermal test vehicles (TTVs) may not reflect in-system behavior. We compared TIM results on H100 and ASTM D5470 setups and found die warpage significantly affects thermal performance. Our findings reveal the limitations of conventional validation methods and introduce a new approach that identifies the direction of warpage change, improving the accuracy of TIM evaluation and selection for real world applications.

### Cooling High Power Racks (1 MW+)
Speakers: Emily Clark (Dell), Paul Artman (AMD), Husam Alissa (Microsoft), Chris Campbell (Vertiv)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7503/
Join the Cold Plate Subproject for an insightful discussion with industry leaders on the opportunities and challenges of cooling high powered racks at 1 MW and beyond.

### Introducing...UQDv2.0!
Speakers: Elizabeth Langer (CPC), Travis Gaskill (Etched)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7345/
The Universal Quick-Disconnect (UQD) has played a significant role in the cooling ecosystem for GPUs and genAI. In order to scale, and to further enable the adoption of liquid throughout the ecosystem, a workstream was established at the end of 2024 to develop a UQD Version 2. The purpose of this workstream was to update the UQD/UQDB v1 specification such that gaps in requirements and performance are resolved, ambiguity is removed, and true interoperability is defined and achievable. Key deliverables include unification of the UQD and UQDB as a singular specification, defined performance and interoperability testing requirements, and realization of a new mating configuration. Enhanced performance attributes and technical detail of the official v2 will be discussed, as well as guidance for working with participating vendors in the OCP ecosystem for immediate inquiries.

### Turbidity and Electrical Conductivity Measurements to Determine Glycol-Based Fluid Quality
Speakers: Lauren Huffman (Dow Chemical)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7220/
Through our work on developing standards for propylene glycol based fluids for the datacenter direct-to-chip (DTC) liquid cooling application, discussions around the validity of some measurements to determine fluid quality and lifetime have arisen. In this talk, I will detail the value and drawbacks of using turbidity and electrical conductivity to determine the quality and longevity of propylene glycol based fluids. This presentation will cover fluid faults such as biofouling, particulate matter, suspended solids, propylene glycol degradation, additive stability and corrosion. We will also briefly discuss measurement methods and variability.

### Methods to support higher power rack cooling
Speakers: Mark Steinke (NVIDIA), Bahareh Eslami (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7016/
Increasing rack power desnsity requires careful consideration on how to supply cooling for those racks. Traditional methods of liquid cooling racks may not be sufficent to support a long term outlook. Coolant distribution form factors may need to evolve to meet cooling requirement. The rack architecture and CDU architecture should allow for felixable designs to support the varied methods of instation and customer preference. This overview will describe how this can be achieved and look at what a future close coupled rack and cooling solution may look like.

### The latest developments in two-phase cooling technology: performance and the future role
Speakers: Liang-Ching Cheng (Wistron Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7140/
This presentation will showcase the current two-phase CDU design and the two-phase cold plate samples. The test results of the two-phase cold plate samples are compared with those of the same samples filled with PG25 as the working fluid. Based on the comparison, the potential of the two-phase cold plates can be discovered. Without significantly altering the existing single-phase architecture, the two-phase coolant can be distributed freely to various racks, providing a solution for the chips with locally higher heat flux. Lastly, the future role of the pumped two-phase solution in the cooling environment, and the forthcoming business model will be discussed.

### ACS Door Heat Exchanger Sub-Project Updates
Speakers: Jabari George (J.M. Gross Engineering)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7085/
The Door HX sub-project, under the Cooling Environments track at the Open Compute Project (OCP), is accelerating the adoption of liquid cooling by producing open specifications, white papers, and real-world case studies. This work stream supports next-generation IT deployments by exploring the practical application of Door Heat Exchanger (HX) solutions in liquid cooling-ready facilities, evaluating various heat exchanger technologies, and driving innovations for Open Rack V3. Cross-collaboration with other OCP sub-projects remains a key priority—emphasizing sustainability assessments, higher rack power densities, and the development of reference architectures informed by hyperscale deployments. This session will provide a comprehensive update on project milestones, highlight collaborative progress, and invite community input on advancing Door HX integration strategies.

### Advanced Cooling Environments (ACF) to Coolant Distribution Unit (CDU) Transition
Speakers: John Menoche (Vertiv), Matt Koukl (AEI Affiliated Engineers)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6917/
Advanced Cooling Environments (ACF) to Coolant Distribution Unit (CDU) Transition

### TCS Flow Management with Door Heat Exchangers
Speakers: Edwin Hipolito (Danfoss), Saul Aguirre (Grundfos)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7087/
This session explores the implementation and optimization of TCS (Technology Cooling System) flow management in data centers equipped with Door Heat Exchangers. It highlights how TCS flow control—via precision liquid flow modulation—drives energy efficiency across variable load conditions, reduces pumping overhead, and improves rack-level thermal performance. The presentation shares results from collaborative implementation from industry partners Danfoss, Grundfos, and Rittal GmbH to evaluate loop-level design parameters and their impact on temperature stability. The team also introduces control design strategies for dynamic flow balancing and showcases a TCS Flow Control reference architecture for Open Rack V3. Attendees will gain insight into how flow-controlled Door HX configurations can deliver both sustainability and performance gains in next-gen deployments.

### Coolant Sampling Cart: Automating Sample Collection and Analysis
Speakers: Cam Turner (Microsoft), Shane Drexler (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7255/
The rapid deployment of liquid-cooled racks in datacenters has introduced a new operational challenge: how to efficiently scale the sampling of PG25 heat transfer fluid to ensure compliance with the OCP Guidelines for Using Propylene Glycol Based Heat Transfer Fluids in Single Phase Cold Plate-Based Liquid Cooled Racks . A datacenter may contain hundreds of Technical Cooling System (TCS) loops — either Air-Assisted Liquid Cooled (AALC) configurations paired with compute racks or larger CDU-based systems supporting multiple racks — all of which require periodic fluid sampling. This process places a significant burden on operations teams and is susceptible to human error. To address this, the Liquid Cooling Sampling Cart introduces an automated solution for fluid sample collection, on-site testing, data logging, and label printing for lab analysis. This cart aims to streamline compliance workflows, reduce effort, and improve data accuracy across large-scale liquid cooling deployments.

### Community - Unified Redfish Schema for Managing OCP Liquid Cooling Equipment
Speakers: Michael Jones (Vertiv)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6877/
Managing advanced liquid cooling hardware shouldn't require custom tooling for every vendor or component. Standardization is key to scaling liquid cooling, and the Hardware Management workstream is building the tools to make it possible. This session will showcase the progress made in schema development, mockup creation, and interoperability profiles. Attendees will see how this work improves manageability, efficiency, and integration for current and next-generation OCP liquid cooling deployments.

### Cold Plate Design Considerations for Improving Liquid Cooling Efficiency and Serviceability in E1.S Modules
Speakers: Dennis Trieu (Microsoft), NENG CHIEH CHANG (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7147/
E1.S modules, with their slim profile and dense layout, pose serious thermal and mechanical challenges in liquid-cooled data centers. To address this, Microsoft and Wiwynn co-developed a high-performance cold plate tailored for E1.S. The design maintains form factor compatibility while integrating all key components—such as a specialized internal flow channel for even cooling and an elastic interface mechanism to reduce shear stress on TIM pads during module swaps. The abrasion-resistant TIM and modular construction improve reliability, cooling efficiency, and ease of maintenance, making it a strong solution for next-gen storage thermal needs

### OCP Plate Heat Exchanger Specification Workstream Presentation
Speakers: Gemma Reeves (Alfa Laval), Florian Hudelmaier (Wieland Werke)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6866/
This OCP base specification was developed in collaboration with leading manufacturers to define standards for plate heat exchangers in OCP-ready data centers. Plate heat exchangers are critical components in thermal management, enabling efficient heat transfer between fluids without mixing. In the white space, they support systems such as cooling distribution units, immersion cooling, and direct-to-chip (DTC) systems for both single- and two-phase solutions. In the gray space, they are used in chillers and work with cooling towers, dry or adiabatic coolers, and natural sources like river or borehole water. They also enable a range of heat recovery applications, including district heating, greenhouses, and swimming pools. This presentation outlines the development of the specification’, the defined standards and applications for plate heat exchangers and the importance of standardizing this essential technology within the OCP ecosystem.

### Air-Assisted Liquid Cooling (AALC) Systems: Deployment at Scale and Design of Next Generation
Speakers: Zach Miller (Meta), Jiu Xu (Meta), Chloe(Kexin) Xu (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6867/
To meet the growing demand for AI/ML systems, Meta developed a portfolio of liquid cooling solutions, including Air-Assist Liquid Cooling (AALC) and Facility Cooling (FLC). These solutions are designed to seamlessly scale-out compute deployments, enabling Meta's infrastructure to support the complexity of AI workloads. The introduction of liquid cooling in datacenter environments marks a significant paradigm shift, requiring adaptations in architecture, integration, validation, and tooling. To address these challenges, Meta utilizes a robust validation architecture, ensuring the reliability, performance, functionality, and safety of liquid cooling systems. This validation strategy provides signal into the effectiveness of our liquid cooling solutions, enabling Meta to deliver robust cooling systems with AI platforms.

## Cooling Environments: Immersion

### Immersion Cooling Community Outreach and Enablement - Engaging with Immersion
Speakers: Allison Boen (Alcatex and Shell Fluid), Rich Lappenbusch (Super Micro Computer, Inc)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7296/
Since launching in June 2024, the Immersion Cooling Community Outreach Team has become a dynamic force within the Open Compute Project ecosystem. In just 18 months, we’ve hosted four successful networking events, raised over $50,000 from 45+ sponsors, and are on track to exceed our $100,000 goal by the end of 2025. Our mission is simple but impactful: Community First. With the support of the OCP Foundation, we’ve contributed to their webinar series, profiling the engineers, innovators, and advocates driving immersion cooling forward. In this session, we’ll highlight our progress, outline Q4 2025 goals—including expanded partnerships and outreach—and share what’s ahead for 2026. From global collaboration to strategic education, we’ll show how community-led efforts are shaping the future of immersion cooling.

### Breaking Through the Ceiling: Liquid Cooling Insights and Forecasts to 2030
Speakers: Rolf Brink (Promersion)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6856/
Liquid cooling is no longer an emerging trend, it's a necessity. In this session, Rolf Brink, Chair of the OCP Immersion Project and founder of Promersion, will unveil the latest industry analytics and share updated forecasts for the liquid cooling market, with a sharp focus on immersion technologies.
