Drawing from global market modeling, ecosystem-wide data, and firsthand industry engagement, this session will dissect the evolving thermal demands of high-density compute platforms and explore how immersion cooling is positioned to meet them. Attendees will gain unique visibility into growth trajectories, inflection points, and the strategic shifts shaping the future of data center cooling.

### Community - Scaling Standards and Adoption for a Liquid Future
Speakers: Rolf Brink (Promersion), Jessica Gullbrand (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6855/
The OCP Immersion Project has become the industry’s hub for open standards and interoperability in immersion cooling. This session will present key milestones and new specifications coming up from the community efforts. With a growing global community, the project is enabling safer, more efficient, and interoperable immersion solutions through structured collaboration and practical standardization.

### OCP Industry Liaison Team: Strengthening External Collaboration on Cooling Standards
Speakers: Kelley Mullick (Avayla), Maryam Mohagheghi (HF Sinclair)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7072/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=AgN5rjU9r8w
The OCP Industry Liaison Team continues to expand engagement with key external standards bodies to advance collaboration and alignment on liquid cooling technologies. Our mission remains focused on fostering bilateral communication between OCP and external organizations to accelerate the development of critical standards needed for scalable, interoperable liquid cooling solutions. Recent activities include coordinated outreach to ASTM, ASHRAE, IEEE, and other relevant consortia to ensure OCP priorities are reflected in broader industry efforts. The team is also facilitating cross-functional dialogues between OCP Project Teams and external technical working groups to reduce duplication, identify gaps, and promote convergence. By strengthening these relationships, we aim to drive faster adoption of open, vendor-neutral cooling solutions that support emerging high-density workloads. We welcome continued input from the community to shape and prioritize future engagements.

### Immersion Reliability Community Update
Speakers: Samantha Yates (Intel Corporation), Brian Kinkade (FUCHS LUBRICANTS CO.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7106/
The Immersion Reliability committee is focused on delivering collaborative contributions to assess the quality and reliability of IT hardware in immersion fluids due to the interactions of the IT hardware with the fluid. Three workstreams are providing content on the material compatibility of hardware in fluids and the thermochemical reliability of hardware in fluids. The contributors within the immersion reliability community have collaborated on an update to the existing document Material Compatibility in Immersion Cooling, they are finalizing a new whitepaper discussing the Material Compatibility of Components, and they are starting an effort to describe how to test the Thermochemical Reliability of Components. This presentation will discuss the current status and the future contributions of the Immersion Reliability team and how you can participate.

### Thermochemical Quality and Reliability of the Intel Datacenter Certified Shell-Supermicro-Submer Immersion Solution
Speakers: Samantha Yates (Intel Corporation), Punith Shivaprasad (Shell), Rich Lappenbusch (Super Micro Computer, Inc)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7111/
An ecosystem collaboration between Intel, Shell, Supermicro, and Submer embarked on a journey to comprehensively test a complete immersion solution to assess its thermochemical quality and reliability. This presentation will familiarize the community with the series of tests executed by Intel on the collaborative immersion solution to achieve Certification. The material compatibility test methodology developed by Intel, and the comprehensive results of the Supermicro server components in Shell S5 LV will be described. In addition, the detection and resolution of a critical material incompatibility within the Supermicro server will be presented. An innovative approach to execute a rack scale immersion reliability demonstration testing will be introduced and key results on thermal performance, signal integrity, and fluid contamination will be discussed.

### Long-Term Reliability Testing of Single-Phase Immersion Cooling
Speakers: NENG CHIEH CHANG (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7183/
As chip power density increases and sustainability becomes a global priority, data centers are exploring cooling methods that offer better efficiency and lower energy use. Single-phase immersion cooling is emerging as a strong candidate due to its superior heat dissipation and potential to reduce PUE. However, immersing servers in dielectric fluids raises concerns around material degradation and system reliability over time. To address these challenges, we partnered with Microsoft and utilized the Azure platform to conduct a 3–6 month immersion test. Our process involved selecting critical hardware, setting benchmarks, and performing regular inspections. Throughout the test, we tracked fluid and component conditions to identify early signs of wear or failure. This paper outlines our methodology and findings, providing practical insights for data center operators. The goal is to support safer and more reliable adoption of immersion cooling for future high-density computing environments.

### Evaluating Component Level Compatibility and Reliability of Memory and Storage Products for Immersion Applications
Speakers: Raj Bansal (Micron Technology), Oriol Chavanel (Submer)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7505/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-base-specification-for-immersion-fluids-20221201-pdf
This paper presents results from the collaborative work completed by Micron Technology, Inc. and Submer Technologies to systematically test the performance of DDR5 DIMM modules simulating 3-years of aging using Submer’s Hardware Validation Program in an operational immersion cooling test server, as well as follow-up analysis by Micron to assess thermo-mechanical and chemical compatibility. This work suggests a pathway for closing the gap between standard, in-air reliability tests and immersion fluid reliability testing for memory products, provides component and fluid level analytical results from thermochemical compatibility methods previously published by Open Compute Project (OCP), as well as results from mechanical reliability testing following immersion of the DDR5 test modules. Our goal is to advance the discussion on industry-wide immersion standards adoption for product performance and compatibility while emphasizing the convergence of technology for mass adoption.

### Immersion Cooling in AI: Data & Insights from a Multi-Year Deployment
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks), Andy Richenderfer (The Lubrizol Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7229/
This session focuses on operational insights from a multi-year scaled deployment of single-phase immersion cooling at Denvr Dataworks, using fluid developed and monitored by The Lubrizol Corporation. The realities of deploying and maintaining a fully-immersed AI data center will be discussed alongside years of experience and collected data. The dataset includes fluid testing, in-line and facility-level telemetry of over 2,000 monitored parameters, and examples of AI workload performance. We will detail what was learned across hardware compatibility, component optimization, fluid aging, and system reliability. The goal is to provide a clear picture of immersion cooling in long-term use, emphasizing the reliability, practicality, and efficiency of the solution.

### Community Panel - Breaking the Barriers: Immersion Cooling Challenges Across the Ecosystem
Speakers: Jessica Gullbrand (Intel Corporation), Samantha Yates (Intel Corporation), Austin Hipes (UNICOM Engineering), Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks), Kris Fletcher (bp)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6858/
Immersion cooling is gaining traction as a transformative approach for data center efficiency, yet the path to adoption is filled with technical, operational, and market-driven challenges. In this interactive panel, OCP Technical Committee Chairs from across disciplines will share their perspectives on the barriers immersion cooling must overcome to scale successfully. The discussion will explore key issues such as IT equipment readiness, facility integration, supply chain maturity, fluid standards, safety, and qualification complexity. This is a unique opportunity to gain cross-domain insight into what’s holding back the industry and how collaborative innovation can unlock the next wave of deployment.

### Community Update - Immersion IT Equipment (ITE) Workstreams Update
Speakers: Austin Hipes (UNICOM Engineering)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6973/
OCP Immersion Cooling updates for the IT Equipment (ITE) Committee. This presentation includes the statuses and updates for Signal Integrity, Advanced Cooling, and Design Guidelines Workstreams.

### Advantage and Challenges for SSD's in Immersion Cooling Environment
Speakers: Hardeep Singh (Solidigm), Karissa Blue (Solidigm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7363/ | materials: https://www.intel.com/content/www/us/en/content-details/787148/open-ip-advanced-cooling-reference-solutions-single-phase-partner-solutions-for-ecosystem-scaling.html
As data centers driven by AI and high-performance computing move to higher rack densities, traditional air cooling is becoming less viable for thermal management. Immersion cooling offers a sustainable and efficient thermal solution for data centers. Solid State Drives (SSD’s) must manage the advantages and challenges of immersion cooling, The presentation will focus on the specific challenges and opportunities for SSDs with immersion cooling fluids. We will showcase extensive tests being conducted on internal SSD components and SSDs in different dielectric liquids, including synthetic oils, gas-to-liquid coolants, and fluoride liquids. As industry faces challenges in providing reliability for SSDs in the way of warranty and wearout when used in immersion cooling solutions, we will explore the impacts due to the multiple fluids in absence of acceleration factors for reliability.

### Abstract High-Speed Interconnect Signal Integrity in Immersion Cooling Technology
Speakers: Terry Little (Foxconn Interconnect Technology), Kai Wang (Intel)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6984/
As data centers evolve to meet the increasing demands of high-performance computing, immersion cooling technology has emerged as an effective solution for thermal management. However, the deployment of high-speed connectors within dielectric cooling fluids presents unique challenges related to signal integrity. The goals for the presentation of this Immersion Technology White Paper is to understand the impact of immersion cooling on high-speed signal transmission, examining factors such as dielectric constant changes, crosstalk, attenuation, and material compatibility. Additionally, it will outline strategies for mitigating signal degradation, including connector design considerations, material selection, and advanced signal conditioning techniques.

### Effective Heat Removal from a Packaged Data Center based on 2-Phase Immersion Cooling: A Case Study from Singapore
Speakers: Manfred Engelhard (Exyte)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7174/
This presentation details the development and deployment of a modular, containerized data center in Singapore utilizing innovative 2-phase immersion cooling technology. Key challenges were posed in relation to the critical heat removal process of massive amount of heat generated in 2-Phase immersion tanks. Instantly generated heat due to sudden CPU/GPU load changes require dedicated engineering solutions to overcome the risk of tank overheating. Lessons learned are included in view of the space-optimized integration of cooling coils into the facility container and the mechanical robustness in connecting the cooling container to the Data Center Container. The presentation is intended to contribute to the broader Advanced Cooling Facility group as part of the Cooling Environment communities, fostering knowledge exchange on emerging practices in liquid cooling integration and mobile infrastructure deployment.

### Updates in Safety Compliance for Immersion Cooling Systems
Speakers: Michael Sakamoto (UL Solutions)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6934/
The presentation will cover a summary of immersion cooling system requirements currently in IEC 62368-1 4th ed, highlighting areas of concern or needing updates that will better align with current immersion technology and application. Content will also cover current OCP workstream efforts that intend to provide input to those items along with supportive technical rationale that can be constructed into a proposal to the IEC standard technical committee from the OCP immersion liaison group. Presentation will also cover new suggested approaches in meeting safety compliance of both immersion hardware and fluids through number of new standards that can simplify compliance for the manufacturer, and then closing always with Call To Action to this effort.

### Community Update: OCP Solutions Technical Committee
Speakers: Amy Short (Denvr Dataworks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7199/
Immersion innovation happens at many levels, from fluid chemistry to facility integration - but scaling adoption requires system-wide planning that is de-risked technically and financially. The Solutions Technical Committee is comprised of five workstreams that address that need. In this session, the Solutions leadership team will share how it supports cohesive development across these workstreams and facilitates product acceptance within the OCP Marketplace. Topics include community highlights, opportunities for new participant engagement, and critical activities roadmapping for the following OCP workstreams: Immersion Requirements Power Distribution Hardware Management Failure Mode & Effect Analysis (FMEA) Total Cost of Ownership (TCO)

### Power Distribution in Immersion Update
Speakers: Oriol Chavanel (Submer), Kevin Gero (Murata)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7011/
The Power Distribution in Immersion workstream is developing a new Base Specification to guide the design of power distribution units for use in single-phase immersion environments. With input from a cross-section of industry contributors—including ITE vendors, fluid experts, and power system designers—the goal is to define safe, efficient, and future-ready power delivery solutions. This session will outline key elements of the new specification and share performance findings from recent OCP community efforts, including data from the OSAI whitepaper that highlights thermal, electrical, and reliability gains when power systems are fully immersed.

### Fluids Committee Update: Best Practices for Fluid Lifecycle Management, Cleaning, and Oxidation Control
Speakers: Kris Fletcher (bp)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6914/
As immersion cooling adoption grows, so does the need for better guidance on fluid performance, safety, and lifecycle impact. The Fluids workstream is leading this charge by coordinating multi-stakeholder efforts across testing methodology, chemical stability, cleaning, and oxidation management. This update will highlight current progress across its workstreams and show how OCP is developing best practices that balance environmental responsibility with engineering durability - ensuring that immersion deployments remain effective and scalable.

### Community - The Final Dip: Cleaning IT Hardware for Peak Immersion Cooling Performance and Operation
Speakers: Romà Suris Valls (Submer), JanElsa Carty (Shell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6893/
Cleaning residual fluid and contaminant debris clinging to IT hardware following immersion cooling deployments is a critical step in maintaining assuring equipment reliability and safeguard optimal performance. Focused on single-phase immersion systems, the Immersion Fluid Cleaning Guidelines workstream outlines solvent selection, equipment requirements, and scalable cleaning methodologies ranging from manual spraying to ultrasonic and vapor cleaning where appropriate. This whitepaper will focus on sustainability, safety, and compliance of various cleaning options, while supporting standardization in practice across small and large-scale deployments. This workstream’s proposals will recommend easily adaptable practices aimed to reduce downtime, extend hardware lifespan, and enable broader adoption of immersion cooling technologies in high-performance data centers.

### Enhancing Two-Phase System Performance through Fluid Purification via Distillation for Solubilized Contaminant Removal
Speakers: Zach Papiez (Magnalytix), Matt Imburgia (Magnalytix)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6735/
Two-phase immersion cooling systems are increasingly utilized for high-efficiency thermal management across diverse industries. The long-term performance and reliability of these systems heavily depend on the purity of the immersion fluids, as solubilized contaminants can degrade both thermal conductivity and dielectric properties. This paper highlights the critical role of fluid purification, particularly through distillation, in maintaining system integrity by removing solubilized contaminants. It explores the sources of contamination, the mechanisms by which these contaminants affect system performance, and the efficacy of distillation in restoring fluid properties. By presenting experimental evidence and practical insights, this paper underscores the value of incorporating purification protocols in two-phase immersion cooling systems to enhance performance, reduce maintenance costs, and extend operational lifespans.

## Data Center Facility (DCF)

### Data Center Facility Project Highlights
Speakers: Saket Karajgikar (NVIDIA), Karl Rabe (WoodenDataCenter)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7329/
The Open Compute Data Center Facility project delivers integrated solutions optimizing mechanical performance and thermal-electrical efficiency alongside purpose-built servers. This comprehensive initiative spans five functional areas: facility power, cooling systems including CDUs, IT space layout, monitoring/control, and operations management. The adaptable framework supports diverse deployments from new construction to existing colocation through Open Rack implementation. CDU integration enables advanced liquid cooling capabilities, enhancing thermal management for high-density computing environments. New Energy Topologies from AC to DC powers are being proposed. By addressing foundational infrastructure while maintaining deployment flexibility, the project demonstrates effective Open Compute technology integration across varied data center environments, delivering improved operational efficiency without requiring complete facility reconstruction.

### OCP Telemetry White Paper - A summary of the work led by hyperscalers on DC Telemetry guidelines (THIRD PARTY INTEGRATION  TELEMETRY APIs)
Speakers: Scott Sharp (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6823/
While data center operators have traditionally maintained tight control over critical infrastructure data, such as that generated by building management systems (BMS) and power monitoring systems (PMS), tenants are seeking more granular visibility. This visibility would allow them to optimize their own operations, make informed decisions about energy efficiency, and proactively address potential issues that could threaten uptime. The absence of a standardized solution for secure third-party access to this data creates friction and limits the potential for collaboration. Implementing an industry-wide standard and a baseline set of critical infrastructure data would transform the relationship between tenants and operators. It would empower tenants with data-driven insights, promote greater efficiency across the facility, and contribute to a more sustainable and resilient data center ecosystem.

### Operating temperatures to support performance, energy efficiency, and heat recovery
Speakers: Mark Steinke (NVIDIA), Wade Vinson (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7017/
Optimizing the fluid temperature in the Data Center is a balance of compute performance, energy efficiency, and other potential reuses. As rack power densities increase, the demands upon keeping a high facility fluid temperature become a challenge. It is critical to optimize that fluid temperature so that the best overall performance can be realized without compromising energy efficiency in a power constrained data center.

### Advanced Datacenter AC/DC Distribution and Conversion Power Architecture
Speakers: Ehsan Nasr (Microsoft), Jing Wang (Google), Krishnanjan Gubba Ravikumar (Google), Ruslan Nagimov (Microsoft), Niannian Cai (Google), Desheng Guo (Texas Instruments)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7523/
Advancements in power and cooling systems are critical to meeting the demands of the new generation of IT rack, and revising existing distribution systems at both rack and facility level. Advanced distribution and conversion from medium voltage AC to low voltage DC systems require alignment for product availability and interface compatibility to accommodate various options for high-density data centers. Emerging technologies such as solid-state transformers (SSTs) and associated components could provide several benefits. For example, SST eliminates the need for large traditional transformers, currently in use, thereby simplifying power systems and reducing space requirements. However, several challenges need to be addressed for widespread adoption in this industry.

### Panel: Data Center Automation with Robotics & Cybersecurity
Speakers: Georgios Papadopoulos (Facebook), Jay Yale (DS AUtomotion LLC), Toby Xu (Microsoft), Sushma Gogula (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7074/
As datacenters continue to grow, ensuring efficient and safe maintenance operations is crucial for minimizing downtime, reducing costs, and preventing accidents. This abstract proposes the utilization of robotics and other advanced technology for inspection, repair, and maintenance (IRM) activities in datacenter facilities. Our focus is on high-frequency activities such as walkabouts, early failure detection, and work at heights. Robotics : Utilizing robots and drones for remote inspection and monitoring, reducing the need for human intervention in hazardous areas. Artificial Intelligence and Machine Learning : Implementing AI-powered predictive maintenance tools to detect potential failures before they occur, minimizing downtime and reducing maintenance costs. Internet of Things (IoT) Sensors : Deploying IoT sensors to monitor environmental conditions, detecting anomalies and alerting maintenance teams to potential issues. Augmented Reality and Virtual Reality

### Rack Emulator Design for Multigenerational IT Racks
Speakers: Pardeep Shahi (NVIDIA), Steve Medeiros (Boyd)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7090/
As AI factories expand, the need for advanced commissioning tools becomes critical. Liquid-cooled load banks are essential for validating CDUs, thermal loads, telemetry, control systems, and electrical infrastructure. However, most available load banks fail to meet the precise hydraulic and thermal conditions required. The rapid evolution of GPU racks also demands new load banks for each generation. To address this, a next-generation load bank has been developed to support multigenerational GPU racks. It delivers accurate hydraulic and thermal performance for seamless commissioning across configurations. The current design supports up to 250 kW in a rack-mountable form, with scalability to 500 kW. This innovation enables robust infrastructure testing, faster deployment, and reduced integration risk—supporting the evolving needs of AI data centers.

### Data Center Sustainability & Decommisioning - The Ugly Truth
Speakers: Allison Boen (Alcatex and Shell Fluid), Joel Chakkalakal (Critical Risk Solution), James Emerson (Americase)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7012/ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Dq_3spWWmNlVRLyGWrqZ20LlzEEmmcXb/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=106651623269308081059&rtpof=true&sd=true
Data centers are growing, but so is the environmental problem of old hardware. Most talks focus on new tech, not what happens to old equipment. Decommissioning creates a huge carbon footprint . Without clear rules, this process harms the environment. It also creates safety issues for workers, wastes packaging, and makes recycling hard. Our goal is to create a sustainable ORV3 decommissioning plan . This plan covers everything from safe equipment removal to recycling. All parts of this plan are already used by big data centers like AWS, Meta, and Microsoft separately. We want to combine these to set a new industry standard.

### Panel: Insights for Cleaner Liquid Cooling – Cleaning & Commissioning the TCS
Speakers: Hussameddine Kabbani (Google), Pardeep Shahi (NVIDIA), John Herboth (Glumac)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7352/ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Jtbf13k_0SxG4fvajwDVm5eLO9Gv4FZ2/edit?usp=sharing&ouid=117101996934129873700&rtpof=true&sd=true
The rise of liquid-cooled IT—driven by AI and high-density workloads—is redefining data center design & delivery for cloud-scale deployments. Traditional custom cooling solutions no longer meet the evolving demands of large-scale deployments exceeding 100 kW per rack and hundreds of megawatts of capacity with dynamic thermal requirements and extreme cleanliness. Panel discussion highlights updates of the OCP Modular TCS @ Cloud Scale white paper, offering practical guidance on the modular design and delivery of water-based Technology Cooling Systems (TCS). Topics include: - adaptable system architecture - Flow control considerations - Thermal performance - Cleanliness & Commissioning - Sustainability

## Digital Twins

### The Digital Twin Revolution: Smarter, Safer, and More Profitable Data Centers
Speakers: Sherman Ikemoto (Cadence)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7358/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TRc0z_qcze0&t=5s | materials: https://www.koomey.com/koomey_blog/our-new-report-on-digital-twins-for-data-center-operations--out-today-/
Today’s data centers—especially the emerging class of AI factories—are complex, mission critical systems. Uptime and cost efficiency are critical. Falling short risks not only performance but the viability of the entire investment. As workloads and infrastructure demands surge, digital twins are gaining traction as a breakthrough solution. These virtual replicas mirror the behavior of real-world facilities, offering real-time insight into a data center’s ability to meet uptime and total cost of ownership goals. They empower business leaders and operators to simulate, predict, and optimize performance—reducing inefficiencies, outages, and wasted capacity. This session explores the challenges data center planners face and how digital twins are reshaping the economics, sustainability, and reliability of modern infrastructure.

### The Value of Digital Twins: Real-World Case Studies from the OCP Community
Speakers: Matthew Käufeler (Cadence), Kourosh Nemati (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7203/
Digital twins are increasingly being applied to real-world challenges in the data center life-cycle. This session, led by the OCP Digital Twin Working Group, presents a set of case studies demonstrating how simulation-based digital twins have been used to reduce risk, improve efficiency, inform decision-making and improve time to market. Examples include airflow and thermal optimisation, hardware refresh planning, and power simulation. The session will focus on practical outcomes, highlighting both the benefits and the implementation challenges faced along the way.

### Using MGX racks and digital twins to convert any data center into an AI Factory
Speakers: Wade Vinson (NVIDIA), Kourosh Nemati (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6783/
In 2024 NVIDIA worked with OCP ORV3 to donate the MGX Accelerated Computing Rack. Learn how these racks with GB200NVL72 today, and GB300, Vera Ruben and more over time can be used to convert even the simplest data centers and even warehouses into AI Factories. NVIDIA has produced full 3D models of Enterprise DGX SuperPODs, GPU rich pods of various sizes and support pods so that even the smallest site with minimal power can own their own cutting-edge inference and reasoning workhorse. The 3D models are all based on the OCP MGX rack with power, networking, plumbing, containment and more. Attendees will learn how to convert a data center formerly thought as a cost center into a revenue producing intelligence factory where every amp of power from the grid can be converted to token as efficiently as possible at any scale.

### Augmenting Existing IT Procurement and Deployment Workflows Through the Integration of a Data Center Digital Twin
Speakers: Matthew Käufeler (Cadence)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7237/
A key feature of any digital twin is an ongoing exchange of information between the digital twin and the physical asset it represents. In data centers, digital twins span and unite traditionally siloed verticals – namely, the compute hardware and the physical infrastructure and support systems provided by the facility. Explore the ongoing integration of a digital twin into an IT procurement and deployment process, improving existing workflows instead of requiring pre-existing practices to be re-imagined, ensuring that the digital twin is updated automatically as the underlying physical asset changes over time. Learn how the use of a physics-based digital twin has augmented traditional decision-making processes, with simulation data providing insight into the impact of changes before they are made and assisting in the evaluation of diverse placement options to mitigate risk and reduce energy use.

## Edge

### Mavenir- Advent of Open Networking in Telcos
Speakers: Kamini Santhanagopalan (Broadcom), Amir Elbaz (BE Networks)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6923/
Mavenir, a world class technology company evangelizing and leading the disaggregation of carrier networks throughout the world, is using open networking and OCP based hardware design to propel its innovation forward and simplify its diverse deployments. Using Broadcom based hardware designs, together with BE Networks automation and orchestration, it increased its customer satisfaction while reducing design and deployments timeframes. Mavenir CTO and Product lead from Broadcom (or BE) will discuss the advances of open networking in carrier networks and the positive impacts on reliability and downtime while reducing maintenance window times and cost.

### AI in Transportation: Global Impact and Understanding of AV Computing Energy
Speakers: Haitam Laarabi (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7554/ | materials: https://eta.lbl.gov/publications/2024-lbnl-data-center-energy-usage-report | pdf: https://ieeexplore.ieee.org/abstract/document/9942310
Autonomous Vehicles are rapidly evolving from prototypes to commercial deployments, transforming into "Data Centers on Wheels" with major computing demands. Studies estimate that 1 billion AVs driving 1 hour daily would consume energy equal to all current data centers, with onboard computing reducing vehicle range by up to 12%. Yet quantifying this energy footprint across onboard computing, data transfer, and datacenter training remains challenging due to varied architectures and limited data. Our analysis examines three tiers: 1) ADAS (Level 2-3): 30-100W onboard compute generating terabytes daily, 2) Current AV (Level 4): modular AI with accelerators like GPUs and custom FPGA/ASICs, 300-700W power draw generating hundreds of terabytes daily, and multiple server GPUs for datacenter training, 3) Future AVs (Level 4+): end-to-end foundational models potentially exceeding 1kW. We investigate societal net energy savings in the transportation sector as these tech see increased adoption.

### Accelerating Inference at the Edge: Unlock Scalable, Secure, and Low-Latency Connectivity for the AI Era with IP over DWDM
Speakers: Yinglin (Frank) Yang (Legrand)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7522/ | materials: https://approvednetworks.com/blog/accelerating-the-ai-revolution-thoughts-from-nvidia-gtc-2025/
As AI inference expands to the edge—powering applications like retail analytics, fraud detection, smart cities, and autonomy—networking must evolve for secure, real-time, high-throughput data exchange. Traditional multi-layer optical transport introduces latency and complexity, limiting scalability. This session explores how IP over DWDM (IPoDWDM) collapses IP and optical layers into a unified, efficient architecture. It delivers lower latency, greater bandwidth efficiency, and reduced TCO. Attendees will learn how IPoDWDM enables dynamic AI workload delivery across edge, metro, and regional nodes—bridging the gap between theoretical potential and practical deployment.

### Deploying Modular 2-Phase Immersion Cooling: A Case Study from Singapore
Speakers: Manfred Engelhard (Exyte)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7171/
This case study presents the development and deployment of a modular data center project in Singapore utilizing a 2-phase immersion cooling system with a compact, containerized architecture. The project involved unique design and integration challenges, particularly around the mechanical integration of roof-mounted cooling systems, fluid management logistics, and component-level customization. Key engineering decisions were driven by the need for flexibility, transportability, and local deployment constraints. This session will focus on lessons learned, including pre-deployment assembly trade-offs, and edge-specific design considerations. The presentation is intended to contribute to the broader Modular Data Center (MDC) as part of Data Center Facilities communities, fostering knowledge exchange on emerging practices in liquid cooling integration and mobile infrastructure deployment.

### Edge-MHS - The foundation of the full spec has arrived - Understanding the Details
Speakers: Dirk Blevins (Intel Corporation), David Heinrich (HPE), Gregory D Sellman (AMD), Xinxin Wang (H3C)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7175/
The team has been working hard over the past year and a half to triangulate on what it would take to take modular hardware solutions to the edge. The time to start to utilize the specification is here, come out and learn the details about the specification and ask all the great questions to some of the key developers of the specification.

## Executive Sessions

### Building the Future of AI Data Centers with Open Systems presented by AMD
Speakers: Krishna Doddapaneni (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7640/
AI is accelerating the evolution of data center design, introducing new demands for performance, flexibility, and efficiency. Meeting these demands requires a robust foundation built on open systems.​ AMD is deeply committed to open standards, contributing across x86, UALink, UEC, and OCP. This session will explore how scale-up architectures powered by UAL enable dense compute within racks, while scale-out approaches using UEC bring RDMA-grade performance to Ethernet—addressing backend challenges in distributed AI environments.​ Together, these open technologies are shaping the next generation of AI-centric infrastructure.

Learn how Arm and its Total Design ecosystem are collaborating within OCP to advance open standards, chiplet integration, and platform-level disaggregation. We’ll showcase how these efforts accelerate innovation, reduce time to market, and support the industry’s move toward more open, sustainable, and workload-optimized infrastructure.

### Back to the Future: Leveraging Classical Compute to Make the Quantum Leap presented by Dell

While fault-tolerant quantum computers may still be in the future, quantum computing is indeed possible today. Tap into its transformative potential with Hybrid Quantum-Classical Computing (HQCC), an approach that can help you significantly mitigate the risks and challenges associated with quantum adoption. Learn the steps to realizing HQCC—including understanding the capabilities of different modalities; identifying appropriate use cases; and leveraging AI—to move from idea to implementation.

### Driving AI at Scale with 1.6T Networking: How Open 100T Switches will Redefine Data Centers in 2026 and Beyond presented by Celestica
Speakers: Tareq Bustami (Celestica), Hasan Siraj (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7649/
This presentation discusses next-generation 1.6Tbps switching platforms, specifically engineered to meet the demanding requirements of modern AI clusters. We will explore how these systems leverage advanced 200G SERDES technology and innovative thermal and mechanical design to deliver high-performance, low-latency networking. In addition, we will introduce the Celestica DS6000 portfolio of switches, developed in close collaboration with Broadcom. These platforms are fully compliant with OCP and open standards like SONiC, ensuring flexibility and seamless integration into hyperscale AI fabrics. This session will provide an in-depth look at how our early bring-up and speed-to-market approach enables our end customers to rapidly deploy the high-bandwidth infrastructure needed for the latest GPUs.

### Redefine Networking. Awaken Cognitive AI with Cisco presented by Cisco
Speakers: Rakesh Chopra (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7656/
The expansion of AI compute clusters is driving increasingly distributed workloads with significant challenges for network infrastructure. Through years of collaborative innovation with the OCP community, Cisco is ushering in a transformative era for AI networking. Cisco will show how advanced silicon and system architectures, backed by real-world deployments and a data-driven approach, enable much higher scale AI clusters to accelerate the journey to cognitive AI. We will present novel technologies for optimizing AI workloads, showcase breakthroughs in power-efficient architectures for scalable AI networks, powered by Cisco Silicon One, and discuss our vision for future-proofing AI networks through continuous innovation and adherence to evolving industry standards for Ethernet.

### The Path to Zero Flap: Reinventing Optical Reliability for Scalable AI Clusters presented by Credo
Speakers: Stephen Manley (Oracle), Bill Brennan (Credo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7637/
As AI clusters scale to millions of GPUs, backend network reliability is vital. Yet commodity pluggable optics remain a top failure point—suffering up to 30,000 FITs. A single unstable optical link can trigger disruptive link flaps, derailing AI training runs. To eliminate unplanned flaps, Credo and Oracle have developed a comprehensive “Zero Flap” optical solution combining host- and line-side telemetry, in-band messaging, event logging, and predictive analytics. This architecture is being contributed to the Open Compute Project (OCP) to launch a new optical reliability workstream. In this presentation, Oracle will outline the challenge optics pose to AI networks. Credo and Oracle will jointly present their solution, its tradeoffs, and initial results highlighting the path to more stable, large-scale AI infrastructure.

### Beyond SSD : SK Hynix AIN Family Redefining Storage as the Core Enabler of AI at Scale presented by SK Hynix
Speakers: Chunsung Kim (SK hynix)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7743/
SK hynix introduces the AIN Family, a new class of NAND-based solutions purpose-built for AI at scale. From ultra-high performance storage for KV-cache and vector databases, to ultra-high bandwidth flash for LLM token throughput, and ultra-high capacity eSSD reaching petabyte scale, AIN solutions extend far beyond conventional SSDs. Leveraging cutting-edge CTF NAND, TSV, and cooling technologies, the AIN Family delivers up to 7× higher IOPS and breakthrough bandwidth efficiency, bridging HBM, DRAM, and NAND into a seamless hierarchy. With AIN, SK hynix redefines storage as a core enabler of AI innovation, setting a new benchmark for the future of memory-centric architectures.

Speakers: Rob Campbell (Flex (Flextronics)), Chris Butler (Flex (Flextronics))

High-density IT racks are pushing data center power consumption and heat generation into uncharted territory. Innovations in power and cooling are rising to the challenge, transforming data center infrastructure. As 1+ MW racks gain adoption in AI-era data centers, electrical rooms and data halls are undergoing a metamorphosis, with collaborative design, engineering and advanced manufacturing leading the way. As computational workloads become tightly intertwined with the infrastructure that powers them, data center operators must embrace next-gen power, cooling, and IT systems to meet rising demands and stay competitive. Discover what this means for the supply chain and next-generation data center architecture —from significant gains in energy efficiency to off-site fabrication and space utilization that simplifies construction and increases compute capacity regardless of current footprint. By 2030, the traditional infrastructure we’re familiar with today will be completely redesigned.

### Accelerating AI Infrastructure Through Modular Building Blocks presented by Supermicro
Speakers: Johnson Eung (Super Micro)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7586/
Revolutionary AI infrastructure demands revolutionary deployment approaches. Through open community collaboration and standardized modular design principles, we've transformed traditional 2-year hyperscale deployments into 122-day realities. This session shares engineering insights from deploying the world's largest liquid-cooled AI supercomputer using modular building blocks—server, cooling, networking, and storage components designed for interoperability. As AI training evolves toward distillation and mixture-of-experts models, diverse environments require flexible, standardized solutions. We'll explore how open design principles enable compute modules adapting to varying datacenter constraints while maintaining performance excellence. Through real-world case studies, discover collaborative engineering strategies that reduce complexity, accelerate deployment, and democratize enterprise-grade AI infrastructure access.

### Building, Extending, & Customizing Foundation Models: FoxBrain’s Journey to Industrial Frontier AI | Presented by Dr. Yung-Hui Li and Dr. Tran Van Nhiem - Foxconn
Speakers: Dr. Yung-Hui Li (Hon Hai Precision), Dr. Tran Van Nhiem (Hon Hai Precision)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7595/
In this talk, we share the story of FoxBrain Taiwan’s first high-performance Reasoning Traditional Chinese LLM—and how it was built with a lean, cost-efficient setup: a small team, limited GPU resources, and focused engineering strategy. Our journey demonstrates how today’s AI can be used to build the next, unlocking domain-specific Foxconn industrial manufacturing platform solutions for the era of AI-driven innovation. We will dive into key lessons learned, including: Designing scalable, cost-efficient data engines Developing synthetic & augmentation data pipelines Scalable and Efficient Model Training Strategies: Pre-training and Post-training Stage Evaluating Quality and the Continuous Improvement Foundation Model Cycle FoxBrain is a 70B-parameter model based on Llama 3.1. Despite our lean approach, it delivers competitive reasoning and language performance, surpassing regional benchmarks and nearing world-class standards at only a fraction of the usual cost and scale.

Paired with Delta’s precision cooling technologies—including liquid cooling and energy-efficient air solutions—this integrated approach delivers optimized energy use, space savings, and scalable performance for high-density environments. ### Empowering AI at Scale with Lenovo Neptune® Liquid Cooling presented by Lenovo

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6801/
As AI adoption accelerates, organizations face mounting challenges in scaling performance, efficiency, and infrastructure to meet the demands of next-generation workloads. ### LITEON GPU Power Solutions presented by LITEON
Speakers: Bob Weng (Liteon)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7741/
The rapid rise in GPU power demands, driven by evolving architectures and large-scale AI clusters, is straining data center infrastructure. LITEON meets these challenges with next-gen power solutions focused on efficiency, stability, and scalability. Using advanced digital control, intelligent current sharing, and optimized AC input strategies, LITEON ensures reliable performance under dynamic GPU loads. This presentation introduces a roadmap covering in-server, in-rack, and sidecar systems, scaling from 18 kW PSUs to 1.2 MW racks. Innovations include upgraded bulk capacitors for better energy smoothing, enhanced flicker performance under EDPP loads, and robust three-phase PFC converters. LITEON leads in powering high-density, energy-efficient AI clusters, driving both performance and sustainability.

### Navigating the Road to Co-Packaged Optics: Challenges and Considerations for Scalable Deployment presented by Marvell
Speakers: Kishore Atreya (Marvell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7684/
As AI and cloud workloads continue to scale, data infrastructure must evolve to deliver higher bandwidth with greater efficiency. Co-packaged optics (CPO) is a promising architectural advancement that integrates optical interconnects directly with silicon to overcome the performance and power limitations of traditional pluggable optics for scale-up. This session will examine the technical and ecosystem challenges that must be addressed to accelerate widespread adoption of CPO — including thermal management, signal integrity, serviceability, reliability and fiber management at the rack and row level. Attendees will gain insight into the current state of the CPO ecosystem, the innovations needed to overcome adoption barriers, and the strategic considerations for network architects and system designers evaluating next-generation optical interconnects

### Not All AI Racks are Built the Same presented by QCT

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7589/
In this session, QCT will share its engineering journey across all their accelerated systems, highlighting how Quanta and QCT collaborate to analyze architectural choices, make design trade-offs, and optimize for performance, reliability, and total cost of ownership. Attendees will gain insights into QCT’s validation infrastructure for ensuring compatibility and interoperability, as well as the unique manufacturing and logistics considerations for liquid-cooled AI systems. The session will conclude with an overview of QCT’s AI system product lines and how they are engineered to meet the demands of the next generation of data centers.

Speakers: Brijesh Warrier (Microsoft), Esha Choukse (Microsoft), Divya Ramakrishnan (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7677/

### OCP and IOWN Global Forum for Open Optical Acceleration presented by IOWN
Speakers: Masahisa Kawashima (NTT Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7746/
While computing platforms are getting more powerful and efficient, networks are not keeping up with the acceleration of computing platforms. Edge computing could be a solution to cope with this acceleration gap. However, edge computing spoils the benefit of cloud computing, i.e., being able to create a big resource pool. IOWN Global Forum is aiming to address this problem by building high-bandwidth, low-latency, energy-efficient networks with the latest optical communication and device technologies. With the forum's new architecture, we will be able to form one accelerated computing domain by connecting multiple sites, e.g., cloud and edges. This will bring new business opportunities to OCP members, enabling unprecedented applications. That said, this talk presents the overview of IOWN Global Forum activities and proposes possible work items for OCP and IOWN Global Forum collaboration.

### Building the Future of AI Wiwynn - Accelerates the Shift to AI-Driven Infrastructure presented by Wiwynn
Speakers: Alfred Chao (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7587/
This talk shows how Wiwynn differentiates with ODM Direct agility, rapid design-to-deploy, and proven global execution. It explains AI-driven shifts in data centers—explosive compute, higher power density, the limits of air cooling, and the rise of high-speed fabrics—and their impact on performance, power, and cooling. We outline the capabilities needed to deploy AI reliably at scale, and how Wiwynn’s system engineering plus rack/facility-aware integration deliver AI-optimized platforms, robust power, and production-ready thermals. We’ll highlight L12 Cluster building blocks, reference fabrics, and service playbooks that cut time-to-value and TCO while meeting performance, reliability, and sustainability targets.

### Enabling AI at Scale with 1.6T and Beyond: Energy-Efficient DSP, LPO/LRO, and CPO Optics presented by Accelink
Speakers: Tiger Ninomiya (Accelink Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6822/
As AI workloads continue to scale, data center interconnects must evolve to deliver higher bandwidth and improved energy efficiency. This session explores the latest optical solutions at 1.6T and beyond, spanning DSP-based pluggables, Linear Receive Optics (LRO), Linear Pluggable Optics (LPO), and Co-Packaged Optics (CPO). We compare these approaches in terms of power consumption, performance, and integration complexity. The discussion includes DR8 optics as well as variants such as 2xVR4, VR8, and 2xFR4. For CPO, we highlight developments in co-packaged modules, external laser sources (ELS), and fiber shuffle solutions that support scalable, low-power architectures. By presenting a broad portfolio optimized for different system needs, this talk aims to help system designers and data center architects build energy-efficient, high-performance infrastructure for AI at scale.

### Interconnect Solutions for Next-Generation High-Density AI Data Centers presented by TE Connectivity
Speakers: Pranav Garg (TE Connectivity), Ganesh Srinivasan (TE Connectivity)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7679/
Executive Session

### Liquid-Cooling SSDs Isn't Easy:  Challenges, Innovations, and the Need for New Standards presented by Solidigm
Speakers: Hardeep Singh (Solidigm), Ace Stryker (Solidigm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7625/
Liquid cooling in AI servers is a hot proposition, but storage has not kept up. Attaching cold plates to SSDs creates several problems, such as losing space (SSDs get hot on both sides, requiring two cold plates per drive) and restricting the ability to hot-swap drives as needed. An added challenge is that current SSD standards don't address critical requirements needed to make cold-plate cooling work well, including SSD enclosure thickness and flatness thresholds to ensure a strong thermal connection. We will discuss developments in the SSD liquid cooling space, innovations that allow hot-swapping and the cooling of a dual-sided SSD with a single cold plate, and opportunities for the community to define specs to pave the way for a future in which every component of an AI server, including storage, is liquid cooled.

### Ultra Ethernet: Escaping the Chaos of Order presented by UEC
Speakers: Keith Underwood (HPE)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7782/
For decades, most networks have assumed packets must arrive in order. That design kept things simple. Today, networks spend enormous effort to reduce ordering in the network but give the appearance of ordering to applications. In many workloads, especially AI, the appearance of ordering adds cost without providing value. The Ultra Ethernet Consortium’s 1.0 specification provides a full-stack solution over Ethernet, but without the need for ordering in the network. This talk will explore how Ultra Ethernet integrates industry proven solutions to deliver scalable communication for the AI era.

### Pioneering with OCP: Jabil’s Building Blocks for the Modern Data Center presented by Jabil

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7612/

Speakers: Sanjoy Maity (AMI)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7691/
The Open Compute Project (OCP) is transforming the data center landscape by promoting open, scalable, and energy-efficient hardware designs. As OCP adoption grows, open-source firmware innovation plays an increasingly critical role in maximizing the security, reliability, and performance of modern compute infrastructure. This Executive Session will explore the impact of innovative firmware development in meeting the challenges of the explosive growth of AI infrastructure with a multi-layered approach that includes open-source firmware initiatives, security advancements, and manageability standardization. Attendees will gain insights into how open firmware solutions are driving efficiency and flexibility in hyperscale environments. Whether you're a data center architect, firmware engineer, or IT decision-maker, this session will provide a roadmap for leveraging open-source firmware innovations to build next-generation, AI-ready computing platforms.

### OCP Solutions with NVIDIA GPUs for New Gen-AI presented by Aivres
Speakers: James Zou (Aivres)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7740/
Aivres offers a comprehensive suite of OCP solutions with NVIDIA GPUs, designed specifically for generative AI applications. Our portfolio includes AI servers, high-bandwidth networking switches, storage systems, and liquid cooling solutions. Additionally, we offer management tools that streamline the management of servers, network infrastructure, and storage systems within data centers. Our AI DevOps tools support multi-platform device management, enabling the efficient allocation and optimization of AI computing resources. From hardware and firmware to management software, platform setup, model development, and deployment, we deliver end-to-end solutions for generative AI in data center environments. ### Powering Next-Generation Switch Architecture for AI Hyperscale Infrastructure through Open Standards Implementation and Performance Optimization presented by HYVE
Speakers: Michael Lane (Hyve Solutions)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7660/
The growth of AI workloads in hyperscale data centers necessitates a new class of network switch hardware capable of meeting optimized performance, scalability, and efficiency requirements for AI/ML and HPC applications. These workloads demand fundamentally different network infrastructure, requiring purpose-built switching solutions that align with OCP principles of disaggregation and standardization. Engineered for the massive east-west traffic of AI-driven hyperscale environments, these purpose-built network switches integrate ultra-high bandwidth ports, support for RDMA over Converged Ethernet (RoCEv2), and address advance congestion management mechanisms. The architecture also incorporates AI-optimized fabric scheduling and deep buffer memory. Benchmarks demonstrate gains in throughput, latency reduction, and job completion time, thus positioning this purpose-built switch hardware as a foundational component for AI infrastructure.

### Scale or Stall: The Case for Purpose-Built Connectivity in Open Ecosystems presented by Astera Labs
Speakers: Ahmad Danesh (Astera Labs)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7651/
AI's scaling demands have broken traditional server architectures. With emerging AI models requiring orders of magnitude more compute – interconnecting 200,000+ GPUs as a unified fabric – we face a fundamental rethink of data center architecture. AI Infrastructure 2.0 has emerged as the new paradigm for data centers – where the rack functions as the new unit of compute. To enable the transition to AI Infrastructure 2.0, leading hyperscalers are turning to purpose-built connectivity solutions developed within open ecosystems. This shift represents a strategic bet on collaborative innovation, driven by the need for vendor choice, supply chain resilience, and interoperability at unprecedented scale. This Executive Session will cover the blueprint for implementation: proven architecture, protocol developments, system-level software integration, and fleet management strategies that demonstrate how industry leaders achieve breakthrough performance and operational efficiency.

Speakers: Hancock Chang (MiTAC), Christian Walter (9elements), Raj Kapoor (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7745/
As sustainability, transparency, control, and security become critical priorities for datacenter operators and cloud providers, firmware is emerging as a strategic foundation for trust and innovation. This session highlights how MiTAC Computing, the Open Source Firmware Foundation (OSFF), ISVs such as 9elements, and the open hardware community are collaborating to deliver open source firmware—such as OpenBMC and Open Platform Firmware—aligned with OCP, DC-SCM, and DC-MHS standards. Through this ecosystem approach, the partnership enables scalable, auditable, and secure infrastructure for hyperscale environments. MiTAC Computing and Open Source FW Foundation would like to share how open firmware enhances platform integrity, accelerates sustainability goals, and replaces proprietary stacks with open, modular, and future-ready solutions.

### Scaling AI: The Case for 100T Switching
Speakers: Matt Roman (Celestica)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7646/
The exponential growth of AI clusters, with thousands of interconnected GPUs, demands a fundamental shift in network architecture. Traditional scale-out designs struggle with the high-bandwidth, low-latency requirements of collective communication. This presentation explores the critical role of 100T scale-up switching in meeting these challenges. We'll analyze how cutting-edge switch ASICs, leveraging high-speed 100G/200G SERDES, enable a single-stage, non-blocking fabric. This approach dramatically increases intra-cluster bandwidth and reduces latency by eliminating intermediate network hops. The talk will provide a technical overview of this architecture, detailing its benefits for memory-semantic communication and coordinated computing. By adopting a scale-up fabric, we can unlock the full potential of AI supercomputers, ensuring future-proof performance for the most demanding workloads.

### Transforming Deployment Challenges into Opportunities with Cisco AI Networking
Speakers: Murali Gandluru (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7659/
AI and HPC networks face mounting challenges, from managing bursty, GPU-synchronized flows to ensuring fairness, observability, and consistent job performance at scale. As clusters expand, networks must evolve beyond packet switching; they must coordinate, adapt, and recover in real time. Cisco’s Nexus platforms, powered by Cisco Silicon One G200 ASICs, drive the Intelligent Packet Flow suite, with policy-based, congestion-aware mechanisms to optimize performance and job completion times. The G200’s flexible, programmable pipeline supports simultaneous load-balancing techniques like WCMP, Flowlet, and Packet Spray, enabling fine-grained traffic management with lossless transport. These capabilities drive better GPU utilization, faster job completion, and improved visibility under load. The high-performance, low-power architecture also supports flatter topologies, aligns with UEC goals for ultra-efficient Ethernet fabrics, and suits OCP-style deployments. Join us to explore how you can transform deployment challenges into opportunities using Cisco’s AI Networking portfolio.

### Extending Copper Interconnects to 448G: Innovations Overcoming Conventional Limitations
Speakers: Andrew Kim (Luxshare-Tech)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7750/
The challenges to extend channel throughput capacity continue to grow exponentially. We continue to see this in the step from 224G to 448G as manifest by the growing adoption of high-power optics and the exploration of coding and modulation to get copper channels to work. But it is not time to give up on copper yet. In this talk we will discuss the SI challenges in the copper interconnect impairing the desired extension of bandwidth. Then, understanding these phenomena, we will discuss innovations in our interconnect solutions, and how they enable the better SI and impedance control at high frequencies needed for 448G. The technologies discussed in this presentation will be complemented by Luxshare-Tech’s expo demos showing the realization of these technologies and their measurable benefits.

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7674/
The technology landscape is rapidly evolving, from static workloads to software-defined systems, isolated setups to converged networks, and rules-based decisions to AI-driven automation. ### Deploying QLC at Scale: Balancing Density, Power, Performance, and Reliability
Speakers: Ben Di Qual (Pure Storage)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7761/
Hyperscale fleets are struggling to accommodate AI data pipelines, analytics, and massive warm data due to the limitations of HDDs and TLC SSDs. QLC flash, in the SNIA EDSFF E2 form factor, offers a compelling path forward by delivering rack/pod-scale density with improved power/TB and $/TB. However, its adoption requires addressing challenges related to endurance, performance consistency, and platform thermals/power. We will demonstrate how the Pure Storage hyperscale architecture enables QLC to operate at scale, showcasing efficient host-based Flash Media Management (FMM) to extend endurance, manage reliability, and optimize memory utilization. We will also explain how the E2 form factor facilitates petabyte-per-node and exabyte-per-row efficiency. The session will conclude with actionable steps you can take to influence open specifications and deployment models, thereby accelerating adoption. You will gain a clear understanding of the current role of QLC + E2, the critical trade-offs, and how to participate in upcoming open standards discussions.

### From Fans to Fluids: The Critical Need for Liquid Cooling in AI Data Centers
Speakers: Bobby Kinstle (HYVE Solutions)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7592/
As AI infrastructure continues to push power densities to 700W+ per GPU and rack power envelopes beyond 80kW, traditional air cooling has reached its limits. This session will explore the data-driven tipping point that has made liquid cooling not just an innovation—but a necessity in line with the Open Compute principles of Technology Cooling Systems (TCS). With a power density envelope above 40KW, the facility just can’t move that much air around anymore. Liquid cooling enables ultra-high compute density for modern AI data centers. It can be required at any power level above 1000W per server but gets interesting above 2000W. Thermal modeling becomes a must as software needs to support both liquid and air in the same model. We will discuss real-time monitoring of flow rates, pressure, and thermal data, as well as API-based management and energy efficiency optimization in AI and HPC environments.

### Heterogeneous Memory System Architecture for Next-gen Agentic AI Server Platform - Montage, H3 Platform and Samsung
Speakers: Jinin So (Samsung Electronics), Geof Findley (Montage Technology), Brian Pan (H3 Platform)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7601/
With the emergence of DeepSeek, the pressure on GPU systems for computing power and HBM bandwidth in LLM training and inference has eased. However, the demand for larger memory capacity continues to rise. To tackle this issue, new memory tiers such as SOCAMM, MRDIMM, and CXL are becoming increasingly essential. This shift, however, brings several challenges—such as increased data traffic between CPUs and GPUs, greater memory capacity requirements, faster CPU memory access, and the need to reduce energy consumption during data transfer. In this session, Samsung, Montage, and H3 Platform will introduce “Lake Tahoe,” a collaborative R&D project aimed at developing a next-generation AI/HPC server built on the AMD EPYC Venice platform. This open initiative presents a forward-looking vision for memory architecture and technologies, designed to deliver high-speed, high-capacity memory infrastructure tailored to the evolving demands of AI platforms.

### When Storage, DTC, and Immersion meet, Data Stays Cool
Speakers: Scott Shadley (Solidigm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7622/
In the world of AI, the need to stay cool continues to be at the forefront of design concerns. Storage being one of the last frontiers of how to truly stay cool. From the need to make it ‘hot swapable’ and still liquid cooled, the innovation required is somewhat more intriguing than that required for CPUs and GPUs. Solidigm created a Direct-to-Chip (DTC) liquid cooling solution in partnership with Nvidia to enable a cold plate product for storage, find out more! However, there is a need for even more options in the market today. DUG will join us on stage to discuss the potential and value of full liquid immersion technologies and coming requirements and the support tools needed to see that technology move forward for storage solutions as well.

### Powering the AI Era: High-Density Racks, Cutting-Edge Systems, and Advanced Cooling from Wiwynn
Speakers: Marcus Hsieh (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7588/
AI is igniting a massive new market for compute, driving explosive growth in processing demand—and with it, unprecedented power density and thermal challenges. This talk begins by examining the rapid rise in chip and module power consumption, why traditional air cooling is reaching its limits, and how thermal constraints are now a gating factor for performance, reliability, and TCO. We then demonstrate Wiwynn’s technology across three layers—rack, system, and components—showcasing next‑generation high‑power IT racks with robust power delivery, cutting‑edge AI servers, and high‑bandwidth switches designed for accelerated workloads, as well as innovative thermal solutions that span enhanced air cooling, liquid cooling options, and hybrid approaches. Attendees will see how these building blocks integrate to unlock higher density, improve energy efficiency, and accelerate time to deployment while maintaining serviceability and sustainability.

This trend has spurred the advancement of large language models, which in turn require highly customized hardware accelerators. As a leader in the fabless semiconductor sector, MediaTek is dedicated to delivering solutions from edge to cloud that maximize computing performance and memory bandwidth while minimizing power consumption. With MediaTek's full technology offerings including customized libraries in the most advanced process nodes, advanced 2.5D/3D packaging, customized memories and interconnect solutions of die-to-die, chip-to-chip, chip-to-system and rack-to-rack, AI Accelerator ASICs and switch/router ASICs for this emerging trend are enabled with minimum time-to-market efforts.

### Leading the Future of AI Networking
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7616/
The relentless demands of AI and high-speed networking are rapidly pushing data centers beyond the capabilities of traditional copper interconnects. As data rates surge to 400Gbps and higher, copper's limitations become a critical bottleneck, hindering performance and scalability. CPO delivers the solution, overcoming these electrical constraints and enabling seamless optical scaling for next-generation infrastructure. Micas Networks is a pioneer in open networking solutions, today announced the volume production and global availability of its 51.2T Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) switch system. Micas’ next-generation switch, developed with Broadcom, is an industry first and a game-changer for AI-driven data centers needing to scale.

Speakers: Logan Tseng (Perstorp)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7598/
As data centers evolve to meet increasing demands for energy efficiency, operational safety, and environmental sustainability, cooling technologies play a pivotal role in enabling this transformation. This presentation explores how synthetic ester coolants offer a versatile and sustainable solution to address the diverse thermal management needs of modern data centers.

### Conceptualizing Next Generation Memory & Storage Optimized for AI Inference
Speakers: Thomas Won Ha Choi (SK hynix)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7755/
With the emergence of various AI inference applications such as large language models (LLM), unprecedented growth of context length is demanding significant amount of data accesses, where weights and Key-Value (KV) cache data are two major components that drive this demand. With such large data accesses from AI inference, general understanding in memory and storage are facing a new paradigm with few major takeaways. First, with tremendous demand for bandwidth and energy efficiency, compute cannot be handled in the conventional memory system architecture anymore, and thus some partial compute needs to be offloaded into or near memory in order to optimize energy efficiency and power consumption of overall systems. Second, with its unique access patterns in AI inference, memory accesses are not as random as we thought they would be, memory workload patterns are a lot more read-skewed than general-purpose workloads, and storage accesses are not as sequential as we thought they would be. With this new paradigm in mind, we will introduce several next-generation memory and storage concepts being developed or planned to be developed. With growing demand on LLM, PIM (Processing-In-Memory) is gaining a lot of attention recently as next-generation memory that can optimize energy efficiency and bandwidth by offloading some compute and reducing excessive data movement. Furthermore, few pathfinding concepts are being developed that can be optimized for AI inference, such as read-skewed high-capacity memory with semi-sequential access behaviors, and high-performance storage with semi-random access behaviors. With the selection of right interfaces and semantics, we are looking forward to continue conceptualizing these memory and storage concepts that can significantly improve energy efficiency, bandwidth, and capacity for AI systems.

### Leading GreenIT for Modern AI Data Centers, Powered by DLC-2 and DCBBS
Speakers: Morris Ruan (Supermicro)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7606/
The growing demand for sustainable AI infrastructure is driving data centers to rethink their cooling and power delivery strategies. Traditional methods are no longer sufficient to meet the thermal and energy requirements of large-scale GPU clusters. This session introduces DLC-2, Supermicro’s latest Direct Liquid Cooling, together with DCBBS, a next-generation modular backbone that improves energy efficiency and enables rapid deployment. These technologies enable greener operations, higher-density deployments, and lower TCO—forming the foundation of liquid-cooled AI Data Centers. Attendees will gain practical insights into architecture choices, performance outcomes, and deployment experiences that make DLC-2 and DCBBS essential for tomorrow’s sustainable data centers.

### Optimizing Data Center Cooling: TCO and Design Comparisons Between Single-Phase and Two-Phase Direct-to-Chip Facilities
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7639/
As AI and high-performance computing workloads push data centers to their thermal limits, advanced liquid cooling technologies are essential for achieving efficiency, scalability, and sustainability. In this session, Lucas Beran, Director of Product Marketing at Accelsius and a leading expert in data center cooling, will present a head-to-head comparison of single-phase (1P) and two-phase (2P) direct-to-chip liquid cooling systems. Drawing from Jacobs Engineering reference designs, the talk will outline key architectural differences, including fluid dynamics, heat transfer efficiency, and integration with server hardware. A detailed Total Cost of Ownership (TCO) analysis will cover capital investments, energy consumption, and operational maintenance, highlighting where 2P refrigerant-based systems may offer advantages for high-density AI deployments. Attendees will leave with practical insights and recommendations to inform their cooling strategies for next-generation infrastructure.

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7645/

This discussion explores the critical connectivity challenges posed by next-gen AI/ML workloads – bandwidth limits, thermal loads, and ESG compliance pressures – and presents Alpha’s modular, open-networking Empower AI platform as the solution. The talk underscores the switch’s ESG-aligned design and future roadmap, inviting technical collaboration to shape resilient, scalable and sustainable networks.

It supports OCP architecture, enabling scalable, thermally stable, and serviceable high-power delivery for next-generation compute infrastructure .

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7593/
Presented by ASRock Rack and Scaleway, this session highlights their collaboration on the 4OU12N platform. ASRock Rack, a leader in server hardware innovation, presents its growing OCP portfolio with a focus on the 4OU12N, a high-density platform for cloud-native environments. Scaleway, a European CSP, offers diverse services built on a complex array of servers and hardware to host a variety of cloud products. To streamline operations and achieve better efficiency, it adopts Open solutions to cut R&D time, optimize CAPEX/OPEX, enhance agility, and simplify integration. Committed to open standards such as OpenBMC, DC-MHS, and Open Rack, Scaleway began its OCP journey with ORv3-based storage and bare metal solutions, co-developed with strategic partners. A key example is its collaboration with ASRock Rack on the 4OU12N, where Scaleway contributed detailed specifications and focused on OpenBMC readiness via collaborative input, and new feature proposals for integration enhancement.

### Axiado's Hardware-rooted AI Agents Reimagine Security and System Management in the Datacenter - Presented by Axiado
Speakers: Benjamin Levine, Ph.D. (Axiado Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7628/
Today's data centers face escalating complexity and evolving security threats. Axiado is meeting this challenge by embedding intelligent AI agents in hardware directly into the system control layer. In this talk we'll introduce Axiado's AI-driven approach to autonomous security and system management, powered by our purpose-built TCU hardware platform. We’ll show how our AI agents deliver real-time threat detection, thermal and power management, and automated response – reducing downtime, stopping insider threats, and simplifying operations. This talk will highlight use cases, integration options, and how our technology fits into your existing architecture. If you’re building or securing systems at scale, this is a glimpse into the future: hardware-enabled, AI-first.

### "How will copper drive high-speed interconnect solutions fueling next generation AI data centres?".
Speakers: Hendrik Coldenstrodt (BizLink Technology, Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7619/
"With the next generations of AI data centres pushing the limits of every component in high-performance computing, copper interconnect solutions must keep up, to meet the growing performance requirements. In this talk we will cover various options for increasing data rates to 448G, such as bandwidth extension and changed coding, as well as the implications on cable characterization. We will also present solutions for small cables down to 39 AWG for CPC applications. Next generations of (AI-) data centres might also come with additional requirements on interconnects, such as materials compliance and enabling deployment in environments such as space. We will conclude the presentation by showing ways to address these challenges and present a space ready high-speed cable."
