### Modularizing the Datacenter: A Layered Approach from HV to Compute and Cooling
Speakers: JP Buzzell (Eaton)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7642/
As datacenter demands evolve toward greater scalability, efficiency, and sustainability, modularization offers a compelling architectural strategy. This talk explores a holistic framework for decomposing the datacenter into interoperable modules, each optimized for performance, deployment flexibility, and energy efficiency. We also address low-performance compute modules , which offer cost-effective capacity for latency-tolerant workloads. Thermal management is treated as a first-class module, encompassing cooling and heat rejection strategies that align with the compute and power layers. Finally, we explore the software orchestration layer , which binds these modules into a cohesive, intelligent system—enabling dynamic provisioning, fault isolation, and energy-aware workload placement.

### Simplifying the Deployment of Agentic AI at Scale
Speakers: Tim Zhou (Accton Technology)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7596/
Over the last decade, a fundamental shift in computing approaches has occurred which demands foundational change to the structures and architectures that have been the de facto design center for more than 25 years. The success of digital infrastructure hinges on the ability to deliver Al value across all business units at scale. We'll break down the technologies associated with the composable compute and connectivity required to deliver autonomous agentic Al applications, aligning the advancements in merchant silicon and industry standards to the insights drawn from our installed base of large Enterprises, hyperscalers, and service providers.

### A Peek Into the Future: Results from PCIe Gen 6 SSD Simulations
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7672/
Every new generation of PCIe has been met with a new generation of SSDs and a greatly improved level of performance. PCIe Gen 5 has been no exception to this. Companies have taken some time to adjust hardware and software architectures to accommodate a new level of performance. Can future generations of SSDs maintain this breakneck speed? This talk will go over recent simulations on what we believe PCIe Gen 6 SSDs will look like. In addition, it will cover some of the new technologies being developed to help system builders be better able to make use of the new levels of performance. These include: Faster networking CPU offload technologies New NVMe commands

### Methodology and Observation of Congestion Control Impact on MoE Training Job Completion Time
Speakers: Alex Bortok (Keysight)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7675/
This presentation will focus on methodology and emulation technique to quantify the performance and effectiveness of AI network fabric that interconnects GPU clusters for running Mixture-of-Experts training workload. Impact of congestion control and load balance schemas deployed in AI data center will be observed and compared. By aligning theoretical insights with practical implementations, we hope to provide actionable insights for advancing research and applications in AI network infrastructure.

### Quantum-Resilient Security for Datacenters: A QRNG-RoT FPGA Framework with Live Demonstration
Speakers: Mamta Gupta (Lattice Semiconductor), Carlos Abellan (Quside)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7758/
Enabling Secure Boot, PQC Readiness, and Trusted Communications with Verifiable Hardware Randomness for High-Assurance Infrastructure As datacenters evolve to support post-quantum cryptography (PQC), zero-trust architectures, and secure multi-tenant operations, the need for high-quality entropy and hardware-rooted trust becomes critical. This presentation introduces a practical framework that integrates Quantum Random Number Generators (QRNG) with Root of Trust (RoT) enabled FPGAs, delivering scalable, quantum-resilient, upgradable security tailored for modern datacenter infrastructure. The talk will explore how verifiable quantum randomness—generated independently of deterministic processes—can enhance trust in cryptographic operations such as key generation, secure boot, and attestation. While traditional TRNGs remain widely used, QRNGs offer a provable and physics-based source of entropy, making them a compelling complement for high-assurance environments without discrediting existing TRNG implementations. Key Topics Covered - Secure firmware updates for server blades and BMCs - Cryptographic provisioning for hardware identity and attestation - Entropy-hardened key generation for TLS, SPDM, and MACsec flows - PQC readiness for CNSA 2.0 compliance and beyond - Modular, low-power, integration-friendly design for hyperscale and edge datacenter deployments Live Demo A working prototype will be showcased, demonstrating secure data exchange and device authentication using quantum-grade entropy in a datacenter-relevant scenario. Target Audience Security architects, datacenter infrastructure engineers, FPGA developers, PQC strategists, and anyone involved in trusted compute and cryptographic provisioning.

Speakers: Calvin Nicholson (Legrand), Swen Anderson (Legrand)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7603/
This discussion will focus on recent advancements in power shelf, busbar, and cabinet designs, detailing how new features developed through close customer collaboration are improving usability and system intelligence. ### MangoBoost Full-Stack AI Infrastructure Solutions: MLPerf Inference, Training, Storage Case Studies
Speakers: Eriko Nurvitadhi (MangoBoost)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7633/
Advances in AI/LLMs has led to large-scale AI systems with many GPUs, high-speed NICs, and storage devices. While modern GPUs offer massive compute capabilities, a key challenge lies in efficiently deploying the AI LLMs to multiple GPUs, and orchestrating data movement across GPUs, storage, and the network. This talk presents MangoBoost’s holistic AI infrastructure. MangoBoost LLMBoost software offers a full-stack solution for inference, training, RAG, and management. MangoBoost DPU hardware fully accelerates GPU-GPU communication (GPUBoost) and GPU-storage access (StorageBoost), at line rate over standard ethernet protocols (RoCEv2, NVME-of, UEC). The talk reports MangoBoost's top-ranked results for MLPerf Inference, Training, and Storage, using MangoBoost solutions on modern GPUs (e.g., AMD MI300X). The talk highlights MangoBoost DPU cards delivering 400 Gbps network. Finally, the talk introduces DPU-enabled JBOF & JBOD headless storage solutions designed for AI systems.

### Megmeet 800V 1MW Sidecar Power System for AI Data Centers
Speakers: Roya Movahedi (Megmeet), William Zhang (Megmeet), Yuan Meng (Megmeet)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7632/
This paper presents the DC architecture and key design features of Megmeet’s 800V 1MW sidecar power system, tailored for next-generation AI data centers such as NVIDIA’s Kyber platform. The sidecar includes high-density AC/DC shelves, battery backup units (BBUs), and capacitor-based compensation modules, delivering scalable, efficient, and fault-tolerant DC power for AI workloads.

### Standards-Based Computing for the Modern Datacenter
Speakers: Chris Andrada (MSI)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7695/
Open standards created and published by the Open Compute Project make excellent server designs. Find out how MSI implements OCP standards such as the Datacenter Modular Hardware System (DC-MHS), Datacenter Secure Control Module (DC-SCM), and Open Rack V3 (ORv3) to provide world class server platforms for the modern datacenter.

### Molex Genesis SFF-TA-1040 - Enabling the next generation of PCIe internal cabling standards
Speakers: Adonis Nesser (Molex)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7630/
The Molex Genesis SFF-TA-1040 connector system plays a pivotal role in enabling the next generation of PCIe® internal cabling standards for OCP server architectures. Designed to support PCIe 7.0 performance levels, Genesis offers a super low-profile envelope, direct-to-contact twinax termination, and shielded interface with superior signal integrity and low crosstalk—meeting the increasing bandwidth and thermal requirements of modern data centers and enterprise systems. Its scalable architecture (available today in x8 and x16 configurations), mechanical robustness, and compliance with industry-standard SFF-TA-1040 specifications make it an ideal solution for applications demanding faster, more reliable data transfer. Come learn about Molex’s innovation in this space and how we ensure a future-ready, interoperable cable solution for high-performance computing and storage environments.

### Powering Progress: Murata’s Role in Advancing OCP Innovation
Speakers: Kevin Gero (Murata)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7611/
As Open Compute Project (OCP) architectures evolve, so does the demand for power solutions that are more efficient, scalable, and future-ready. In this session, Murata will explore its contributions to OCP innovation – highlighting key developments across ORV2, ORV3, and the High-Power Rack (HPR) initiatives. We’ll outline the progression from 12V and 50V power delivery in ORV2 to the standardized 50V architecture in ORV3 and examine how Murata is engineering solutions that align with OCP’s aggressive performance and efficiency standards. The session will also touch on power delivery in immersion-cooled environments, including foundational insights into how OCP is shaping power strategies for high-density compute applications. Join us to learn how Murata continues to support and accelerate the future of open hardware innovation .

### Empowering Scale-Up and Scale-Out Networking  with Samtec CPX Architecture (CPC/CPO)
Speakers: Jignesh Shah (Samtec)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7685/
As Next-Gen Datacenter and AI/ML workloads grow in complexity, the demand for high-performance interconnects has intensified. Co-Packaged Copper (CPC) extends 224 Gbps signaling by eliminating BGA packaging losses. This implementation allows for ~1.5m DAC reach preserving the viability of copper interconnects while supporting GPU clustering. Additionally, emerging Co-Packaged Optics (CPO) solutions enable high-bandwidth, low-latency optical links that can span multiple racks. In this Sponsor Session presentation, technical experts from Samtec will detail next-gen CPC/CPO solutions using the same Co-Packaged CPX connector. This new CPX architecture breaks through existing performance barriers allowing seamless interconnection of multiple GPUs and enabling AI supercomputing fabrics.

### From Concept to Capability: Innovating the Future of OCP Reference Designs
Speakers: Gregory Ratcliff (Vertiv)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6778/
Get a behind-the-scenes look at how Vertiv's innovation transforms ideas into impactful prototypes and how this process can drive the evolution of open infrastructure solutions. As compute demands accelerate and sustainability becomes mission-critical, how do we design data centers that are ready for what’s next? This session explores how forward-looking reference designs are enabling the next generation of infrastructure—starting with modular, scalable architectures that evolve with emerging workloads. We’ll look at how AI-assisted planning tools and stakeholder collaboration are reshaping the design process, making it faster, smarter, and more adaptive. You’ll also learn how integrating Open Compute Project (OCP) products supports open, energy-efficient, and vendor-neutral systems built for tomorrow’s challenges. Whether you're building from the ground up or modernizing an existing facility, this session offers a roadmap to smarter, energy-efficient, and future-ready data centers.

## FTI Workshop: Short Reach Optical Interconnects

### Copper Interconnect In 200G Scale-Up Networks
Speakers: Raymond Law (Lotes Tech)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7829/
With its lower cost and high reliability, copper interconnects have major role in enabling the 200G scale-up networks. This presentation provides an overview of the scale-up network and its physical length requirements. Furthermore, various interconnect topologies including the use of active repeaters are discussed. We will also touch on where copper/electrical transition to optical could occur, and we will close with some other high-speed interconnect needs.

### Optical Opportunities for the AI Scale-up Fabric
Speakers: David Piehler (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7830/
As AI demands increase various factors will drive copper-based interconnects in the AI scale-up fabric to the optical domain. This presentation reviews modern copper technology as well as various evolution paths to an optical future considering such factors as bandwidth density, power consumption, and ecosystem maturity.

### Ultra Low Power MicroLED Based Interconnects for AI Scale Up Networks
Speakers: Christoph Pfistner (Avicena Tech)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7831/
Avicena will give an overview of its ultra-low power microLED based interconnects and how they offer an essential solution to the power and connectivity challenges in current AI Scale Up networks. Avicena is the first company to use microLEDs for high-speed communications. Unlike laser-based alternatives, Avicena’s µLED interconnects enable AI compute clusters to scale to thousands of GPUs. These interconnects offer class-leading bandwidth density and energy efficiency optimized for Scale Up with a 10 meter reach. Avicena is a startup located in Sunnyvale, CA with a subsidiary in Edinburgh, Scotland.

### Energy Efficient Photonic Interconnects for AI scale-up
Speakers: JEFFREY HUTCHINS (Ranovus)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7835/
The rapid evolution of Artificial Intelligence (AI) is driving larger and highly interconnected clusters along with the need to shuffle huge quantities of data between compute elements. The next generation of larger AI systems require cost effective, low latency, dense, and energy efficient optical interconnections between local compute elements with sufficient radix to facilitate these data transfers in these new systems. Hyperscalers have provided key metrics for the next generation of scale-up links and are exploring the alternatives. This presentation reviews the benefits, considerations, and challenges of a variety of approaches for the entire end-to-end link.

### Resolving Operational Barriers to AI Scale-Up with Co-Packaged Copper/Optics Sockets
Speakers: Peter Winzer (Nubis Communications)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7836/
AI scale-up remains constrained by the limited reach of passive copper interconnects at data rates of 200 Gbps per lane and higher. A promising approach involves leveraging existing co-packaged copper (CPC) sockets to support plug-in compatible passive and active copper as well as co-packaged optics modules supporting a variety of optical transmission hardware. The resulting flexible, co-packaged mixed-media (CPX) interconnect architecture eliminates traditional reach limitations and allows operators to extend the AI scale-up domain across the data center while maintaining operational simplicity and benefiting from a multi-vendor, pluggable CPX ecosystem.

### Short-reach Optical Interface Scale Connectivity for Optimized Data-movement Through Memory for AI/ML Applications
Speakers: Siamak Tavallaei (Samsung)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7837/
As data-intensive applications continue to scale, throughput-computing workloads demand efficient data-movers. High-speed interconnects such as UCIe are trending to become standard for die-to-die interconnect; while photonics will play a significant role for high-speed chip-to-chip interconnect. Within memory-optimized architectures, capacity and bandwidth considerations have their own places within an at-scale system; however, for all-to-all data-exchange, we will also need efficient, unimpeded connectivity between peer devices while maintaining physical serviceability. In this presentation, we explore how photonics interconnect may help building large AI/ML Super Clusters.

### Infinite HBD: Scale-up Outside the Box
Speakers: Bobby Lu (Lightelligence)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7838/
As AI pushes the boundaries of traditional computer systems, the industry is increasingly adopting scale-up networks for solutions. However, existing scale-up network standards rely on high-radix switches to connect more xPUs for greater computing power. This talk introduces a new concept in scale-up network topology, InfinitHBD, which uses disaggregated switches to build up the high-bandwidth domain network. InfinitHBD offers significant advantages in cost, fault resilience, and other key metrics.

## FTS: Data Center Sustainability

### Biochar in Data Center Decarbonization
Speakers: Seth Kane (University of California Davis)

Incorporating biochar in data center concrete can reduce data center embodied carbon by 30% at lower cost than purchasing biochar carbon credits.

Speakers: Andrew Chien (U Chicago)

### Towards Data Gravity Centric AI: Integrating PNM, PIM and Fabric-level Computing Paradigms presented by Mayank Saxena, Senior Director of Memory Solutions Lab, Samsung
Speakers: Mayank Saxena (Samsung)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7718/
Data gravity centric computing reshapes high-performance architectures with Processing-Near-Memory (PNM) and Processing-In-Memory (PIM) technologies, reducing data bottlenecks. However, as datasets and model sizes continue their exponential growth, ultra-high-bandwidth semantic communication protocols and intelligent software integration marks a decisive era, where boundaries between processing and memory are blurred — promising unprecedented efficiency and scalability for future data-centric digital infrastructure. Crucially, to fully realize the potential of data gravity centric computing, capabilities must be elevated to the fabric level. The fabric-centric programming approach enables scalable, flexible resource orchestration and democratizes access for future AI infrastructure. Embrace this fast-moving technology trend and collaborate with Samsung to deliver industry-useable full-stack memory solutions for Data Gravity Centric AI.

## FTS: Quantum (Data Center Design)

### Quantum at OCP
Speakers: Yogendran Boniface
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7766/

### Quantum Market Overview
Speakers: Noah El Alami (IDTechEx)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7767/
In 2025, the quantum computing industry is at an inflection point, where multiple vendors have deployed their first products in a HPC or data center environment but scaling up to systems capable of real commercial advantage sits tantalizingly close on the horizon. This presentation aims to give a broad overview of the quantum technology market, focusing specifically on the landscape of the quantum computing industry and the drivers for their adoption into data centers. Using a benchmarking scheme, IDTechEx has assessed the progress of commercial quantum computing from an end user perspective and forecast a timeline for future milestones, attempting to answer when commercial quantum advantage might be achieved and the potential impacts on the data center industry.

### Quantum Secure Networks Partnership Update
Speakers: Inaki Barettini (ICFO)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7780/

### Bridging Product and Facility Requirements for Quantum Computing Architectures and Datacentre Environments
Speakers: Jonathan Burnett (National quantum computing centre)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7769/
This presentation will cover the integration quantum computers within datacentre environments. The aim is to be hardware modality agnostic and to inform both product design on the quantum computing vendor side, as well as facility design on the datacentre side. Through the talk a state-of-play will be given to describe the recent history, challenges and frontiers of quantum computing integrations with cloud, HPC, networking and datacentres.

### A Vision for the Future of Quantum Computing and Networking
Speakers: Reza Nejabati (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7778/
Quantum networking can accelerate practical quantum computing from decades to 5-10 years away. Quantum networking technology serves as foundational infrastructure for quantum data centers, enabling scalable quantum computing that can tackle complex problems beyond classical systems. This talk discusses challenges and solution for the complete software and hardware stack that orchestrates entanglement distribution, manages quantum processors from different vendors, and coordinates distributed quantum operations. Further, I will discuss how the quantum networking technology can deliver immediate value today for classical applications and use cases across sectors such as fintech and cybersecurity. It enables capabilities such as high-precision time synchronization, high-speed decision coordination, and quantum-enhanced secure communications that are not possible with current classical networking technologies.

### Fault Tolerance for Quantum Computing as-a-service

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7770/

## Hardware Management

### Hardware Management Project Overview and Workstreams
Speakers: Hemal Shah (Broadcom), Jeff Hilland (Hewlett Packard Enterprise)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7004/
The hardware management project has expanded its scope via work streams. The work stream model enables rapid development of specifications and solutions. In this talk, we will provide an overview of hardware management project and its works streams of system GPU, RAS API, fleet memory fault management, manageability profiles, scalable cloud infrastructure management (CSIM), and auxiliary management controller (AMC), debug and diagnostics, and open boot management framework.

### Panel: Hardware Fault Management Progress Towards Reliability at Scale
Speakers: Yogesh Varma (OCP Project Lead), Anil Agrawal (Meta), Shubhada Pugaonkar (Intel), Vilas Sridharan (AMD), Carlos Vallin (Microsoft), Drew Walton (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6714/
Discover how industry leaders are advancing server reliability at the OCP Global Summit! This session presents the latest on Hardware Fault Management (HWFM), Fleet Memory Fault Management (FMFM), and the RAS API, outlining a unified approach to error handling and reporting for AI-ready data centers. Attendees will gain a practical framework for standardizing DDR5 error diagnosis, leveraging cross-vendor tools for DRAM fault analytics, and adopting consistent RAS API action queues for improved in-band and out-of-band fault response. Take away actionable best practices and insights on developing scalable, interoperable reliability solutions for tomorrow’s high-performance computing environments, with opportunities to contribute to OCP’s ongoing collaborative workgroups shaping the future of silicon and data center resilience.

### OCP Silent Data Corruption Workgroup Update
Speakers: Harish Dixit (Meta), Cyril Meurillon (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7190/
Impact of Silent Data Corruption (SDC) can be significant at scale, and becoming increasingly relevant as data centers and clusters expand to handle growing AI workloads. OCP SDC workgroup drives solutions and best practices that prevent and detect SDCs. Since its inception the workgroup has partnered & engaged with the academic community to actively address growing SDC challenges. In the first phase the workgroup contributed an OCP specification with information exchange format definition and a common test development framework for different chipset architectures. The presentation will highlight how working group continues industry-academia SDC partnership via funded research. It will also discuss recently contributed technical-paper titled: “SDC in AI” about how SDC propagate through an AI stack to affect model behavior requires a holistic view that combines traditional architectural vulnerability modeling with AI workload-specific sensitivity analysis.

### Inside the OBMF Workstream: Latest Spec Developments and Roadmap
Speakers: Bharat Pillili (Microsoft), Janusz Jurski (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7155/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/ocp-obmf/wiki
Open Boot and Management Framework (OBMF) Workstream was launched earlier this year under the OCP Hardware Management Project. OBMF aims to define a unified interface and protocol for communication between the Baseboard Management Controller (BMC) and devices on the HPM such as CPUs from multiple vendors. The goal is to standardize boot and management flows to improve interoperability, reduce integration complexity, and enable scalable, vendor-agnostic deployments. In this session, we will: - Outline the current interoperability challenges in modular platform architectures - Present the OBMF vision and its architectural principles - Provide key updates on the specification content and direction - Share the planned release schedule for the OBMF specification This session offers a detailed update on the progress of the OBMF Workstream and its role in enabling a more unified, open, and scalable approach to platform boot and management.

### Introduction to OCP Diagnostics and Debug Workstream
Speakers: Marko Bartscherer (Intel), Enrico Carrieri (Intel)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7002/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-HM-Hardware_Management/wiki/Wiki-%E2%80%90-Datacenter-Diag-and-Debug
With the rise of large AI systems, nodes and components are increasing in size, diversity and complexity. This increasing complexity requires the ability to securely diagnose and debug beyond the component level to the system levels. The goal of the Workstream is to fill the gap and develop a standardized methodology to extract data and perform debug operations across the data center. We will create specifications that define methods for how debug data is securely exchanged between all components across the datacenter and the debug tooling. The scope includes: Define the use of common connectivity, transports, and protocols. Define a common communication “pipe” for debug data where data payloads stay vendor specific. Use of existing specifications/standards/transports. Allow flexibility but provide interoperability by making debug capabilities discoverable and self-describing. Provide scalability across the development life cycle from manufacturing to the at-scale, in-field deployment.

### Creating Coherent, Scalable, and Interoperable Telemetry Solutions
Speakers: Jim Harford (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7129/
As data centers grow in size and complexity, it becomes even more important to manage their hardware components in an effective, efficient, and timely manner; such that data center service levels and financial goals can be realized. Competing telemetry protocols, scalability hindrances within those protocols, and lack of agreement on metric names can cause difficulties managing the data center. The OCP SCIM workstream endeavors to solve these problems… First, we are creating a high-level, protocol-agnostic architecture that will specify the semantics needed in a data center management system to achieve coherent and scalable telemetry solutions. Next, we will map that architecture to various protocols that are currently deployed or proposed for data center telemetry, including offering suggestions for specific improvements. In addition, we aim to standardize the names of metrics that data center operators are interested in observing, to complete the quest for interoperability.

### Establishing common Redfish requirements with a baseline profile
Speakers: Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies), John Leung (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7240/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/HWMgmt-OCP-Profiles
Redfish is the standard management interface for many classes of devices in the data center ranging from individual servers or power distribution units to rack managers. These different types of devices have unique modeling requirements to manage their respective hardware designs. However, there are common management requirements that apply universally to all types of equipment, which are captured in the OCP Baseline Redfish Service Profile.

### Platform Management Communication Infrastructure Enhancements for Advanced OCP Use Cases
Speakers: Hemal Shah (Broadcom), Patrick Caporale (Lenovo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7014/
OCP has been leveraging DMTF MCTP, PLDM, and NC-SI specifications for platform management communications. MCTP specifications have been extended to cover new physical media bindings and host interfaces. PLDM specifications recently added support for FRU, file transfer, and AI accelerator management. In this talk, we will describe the latest enhancements to MCTP, PLDM, and NC-SI specifications, and how they can be leveraged for advanced OCP use cases for diagnostics and operational management of OCP platform components.

### Panel - Standardizing GPU Management: Redfish, Telemetry, and Firmware Update Protocols
Speakers: Justin York (Google), Akkiah (Choudary) Maddukuri (Microsoft), Qian Wang (NVIDIA), Krishna Sugumaran (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7434/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/ocp-gpu-accelerator-management-interfaces-v1-pdf
The rapid need to integrate GPUs to power AI and high-performance computing workloads reinforces the need for standardized, scalable management and observability solutions. Over the past year, AMD, Google, Meta, Microsoft, and NVIDIA made significant strides in addressing this challenge. This presentation will detail our progress, including the successful publication and future application of a DMTF Message Registry and a Redfish Interoperability Profile for GPU management. Also, we will detail our recent work to standardize a GPU telemetry interface for accessing time-series data, detailed crash dumps, and debug logs. A key focus of this effort is to support low-latency and time-sensitive data streams, complete with service level objectives (SLOs) to ease integration and testability. All of this work establishes a foundational layer for interoperability, providing hyperscalers with consistent management of multi-vendor GPU deployments while defragmenting requests to GPU suppliers.

### Advancing Hyperscale AI Fleet Quality Through Standardized Debug, Diagnostics, and RAS
Speakers: Rama Bhimanadhuni (Microsoft), Jicksen Joy (Google), Taniya Siddiqua (Advanced Micro Devices, Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7094/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/hyperscale-cpu-ras-and-debug-requirements-specification-v0-5-2025-05-22-pdf
As hyperscale AI infrastructure continues to expand and diversify across multiple hardware platforms and suppliers, maintaining consistent fleet quality becomes increasingly complex. This challenge is further compounded by fragmented tooling and proprietary implementations of Debug, Diagnostics, and RAS (Reliability, Availability, and Serviceability) across CPU and GPU architectures. This session introduces the industry’s first collaborative initiative to standardize Debug, Diagnostics, and RAS requirements for hyperscale environments. We will present the latest developments, including contributions - GPU RAS v1.7 and CPU Debug and RAS v0.7, and explore how unified, vendor-agnostic standards streamline onboarding, reduce engineering overhead, and accelerate deployment. Both hardware suppliers and infrastructure operators will gain insights into how these standards enhance fleet quality at scale.

### OpenRMC - Increasing the Role of the Rack Manager in Data Center Management
Speakers: Brian Vandecoevering (American Megatrends (AMI)), Han Wang (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6921/
As artificial intelligence continues to evolve, the demand for efficient rack management has become a critical component of AI infrastructure. A Rack Manager plays a pivotal role in overseeing hardware operations, maintaining system reliability and optimizing training time for massive models/jobs. This presentation explores the need for rack managers to move beyond just a proxy and take on additional intelligence and control by analyzing logs, finding bottlenecks, applying predictive failure detection and better modeling the workloads. With disaggregated hardware, node level management does not have visibility of the other devices impacting it. In larger datacenters, management software cannot respond fast enough with all the telemetry it is receiving. Therefore, decisions need to be made closer to the node. Ultimately, the future of rack management will depend on the integration of AI and automation, enabling more robust and scalable solutions for next-gen computing environments.

### A Rack Management/Monitoring application implementation based on OpenRMC-DM
Speakers: Avinash Natarajan (Celestica), Damodharan Sreenivasagaperumal (Celestica)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7163/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/HWMgmt-DeviceMgr-DeviceManager
The focus of the talk is a Rack Management/Monitoring (RM/M) application we’ve built, leveraging OpenRMC-DM (DM) implementation. This is part of a unified Rack Orchestration solution that includes Network Management besides RM/M over Redfish. The focus of the session would be the latter, but with some data on how it fits into the rest of the orchestration system to facilitate context aware telemetry. Extensions have been made to the DM implementation to fit our needs, but could have generic relevance. DM acts as a proxy to various racks, collecting southbound data from the BMC end points within Racks, and providing northbound rack level redfish abstractions to our RM/M function. Solution is built for a Rack/Multi-Rack GPU cluster. Our specific implementation runs on a remote bare metal server, but other application models are viable. The talk would cover details of the interfaces across various layers, our changes to the base DM, and end user consumable aspects.

### Manage and Secure your PCIe Infrastructure using eSFI and PCIe-MI
Speakers: Paul Kaler (HPE), Austin Bolen (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7033/
This session covers the new hardware management capabilities, key benefits, and use cases enabled by the newly standardized PCI-SIG Enhanced System Firmware Intermediary (eSFI) and PCI Express Management Interface (PCIe-MI) ECNs. These ECNs define the hardware infrastructure that enables a Baseboard Management Controller (BMC) to manage PCIe components independent of the operating system running on the host CPU(s) as well as an extensible protocol that a BMC uses to communicate with a PCIe component.

### Managing Ultra Ethernet Using OCP Standards
Speakers: John Leung (Intel Corporation), Hemal Shah (Broadcom), Jeff Hilland (Hewlett Packard Enterprise)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7351/ | materials: https://ultraethernet.org/
Abstract - The Ultra Ethernet Consortium has been working with the OCP Networking Project on its horizontal layers. Manageability is one of UEC's vertical groups spanning the horizontal layers. UEC is leveraging OCP SAI and OpenConfig YANG for managing the switch and OCP Redfish profiles for managing the servers. The OCP Hardware Mgmt Project is working with the OCP NIC 3.0 subproject to lay the foundations for managing Ultra Ethernet server endpoints with the OCP Profile for the EthernetNIC. In this talk, we will describe how UEC is leveraging OCP standards for managing Ultra Ethernet.

### Management and Control of UALink Scale-Up Pods
Speakers: Justin King (AMD), Arun Satyanarayana (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7225/
Ultra Accelerator Link (UALink) is an open, high-bandwidth, low-latency network designed to interconnect AI accelerators into scale-up Pods. This presentation will explore the configuration and control of a UALink Pod via standards-based interfaces to a centralized Pod Controller and distributed Management Agents. By standardizing these interfaces, along with the GPU management interfaces being standardized in OCP’s GPU management interfaces workgroup, we promote interoperability and open choice for the over 100 accelerator, switch, and platform vendors and hyperscalers who make up the UALink Consortium. Attendees will gain valuable insights into the architecture, limitations, and security considerations of UALink Pods, equipping them with the knowledge to optimize AI infrastructure.

## IPEC Workshop

### Learnings from 25 Years of Market History and a Forecast Update
Speakers: Vlad Kozlov (LightCounting)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7880/
This presentation provides a comprehensive overview of Ethernet optics market trends, illustrating past cycles and future trajectories. It examines how AI-driven growth is reshaping market dynamics, outlines realistic forecast scenarios, and highlights evolving technologies in optical interconnects. The talk positions these insights within a broader context of long-term innovation, investment behavior, and supply chain evolution shaping the next decade of optical networking

### Opportunities and Challenges of Optics in Booming AI/ML Systems
Speakers: Cedric Lam (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7881/
This talk shares a hyperscaler’s perspective on operating datacenter optics, highlighting their role in scaling AI/ML systems. It will cover both the opportunities optics unlock and the challenges that remain in deployment and operation.

### Optics for AI Networks- Scaling for Large Clusters
Speakers: Tzu Hao Chow (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7883/
As AI cluster sizes grow larger in size with higher escape bandwidth, reliable optical connectivity becomes more critical. For scale-out and front-end networks, these have been served with pluggable optics. Co-package optics (CPO) provide higher density and low power optical connectivity, and recent performance data showed good link stability. This paves the way forward for deploying this technology for scale-up networks. Copper currently dominates AI scale-up network connectivity. Optical interconnects on the other hand offer simplification and reach but are currently limited by power and cost competitiveness compared to copper. Two optical technologies offer good performance metrics needed for scale-up links. VCSEL-based near packaged optics (NPO) offers superior energy efficiency and cost, while silicon photonics CPO provides high escape bandwidth density, extended reach and good link stability. Both NPO and CPO technologies will see increased deployment in scale-up networks.

### 400G Optics: Choices, Challenges, and Opportunities
Speakers: Jianying Zhou (Hisense)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7884/
We review 400G Optics in the aspect of Choices, Challenges, and Opportunities. We show the demonstration of 400G/lane with a high bandwidth and efficient thin-film lithium niobate Mach-Zehnder modulator. High performance is achieved using the modulator and practical equalizations, confirming the feasibility of 400G/lane applications.

### Cost-effective and Power-efficient AI / ML Networks
Speakers: Matt Sysak (Lumentum)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7885/
This presentation showcases Lumentum’s technological leadership in building cost-effective and power-efficient optical networks for AI and ML data centers. It highlights innovations that dramatically reduce network power consumption while enabling large-scale GPU deployments. The talk outlines how co-packaged optics and high-volume interconnects are redefining scalability, reliability, and energy efficiency for next-generation AI infrastructure

### Enabling the AI Revolution Through Energy-Efficient Interconnect Technologies
Speakers: Bilal Riaz (Ciena)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7886/
Explores energy-efficient interconnects for AI systems, advancing from 224G to 448G per lane to boost bandwidth while reducing power. Compares electrical and optical options including LPO, NPO, and CPO. Highlights liquid-cooling innovations and 3.2 Tb/s modules enabling next-generation AI networks.

### IPEC Standards Projects: Enabling the Next-Generation AI Datacenter
Speakers: Eric Bernier (IPEC/Huawei)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7887/
IPEC is driving open, collaborative standards that enable the next generation of AI datacenters through innovations in optics, reliability, and liquid cooling. Together, we’re defining the technologies that will power higher bandwidth, efficiency, and scalability for years to come.

## Keynotes

### Agile AI Architectures: The Fungible Data Center for the AI Era
Speakers: Partha Ranganathan (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7728/
We are in the middle of an intelligence revolution, with impactful new AI innovations across various sectors from consumer products to enterprise cloud offerings, and even fundamental scientific research. These amazing advances have been made possible by equally impressive progress in computing infrastructure. But as we look at continued exponential growth, the growth, volatility, and diversity of the rapid pace of innovation demands unprecedented adaptability across the infrastructure stack. We need new agile and fungible data center architectures with modular, interoperable, and standards-based designs cross-cutting all aspects of the systems stack, delivering dramatic improvements in compute efficiency, reliability, and sustainability. Collaborative efforts and industry-wide alignment are essential to realize this exciting future.

### Shaping the Future of Open Infrastructure for AI
Speakers: Ian Buck (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7723/
NVIDIA’s commitment to open, rack-scale architecture enables fast, seamless upgrades for AI Factories at scale. This keynote highlights how the ecosystem is enabling data center operators to build reliable infrastructure on current and future generations of NVIDIA rack architectures for the most demanding AI workloads. Join us as we highlight the enduring value of open hardware and software platforms, ecosystem collaboration, and rack-scale reliability in shaping the future of accelerated computing and open infrastructure for AI.

### Building Zettascale AI Clusters with OCI: Scaling Together with Open Ecosystem for the Next-Gen AI
Speakers: Pradeep Vincent (Oracle)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7724/
As AI workloads explode in scale, infrastructure must evolve to accommodate trillions of parameters, exabytes of data, and unprecedented compute demands. This session explores how Oracle Cloud Infrastructure (OCI) is architecting and deploying next-generation zettascale AI clusters purpose-built for large-scale training and inference. We’ll walk through the design principles, deployment challenges, and lessons learned along with the critical role that open ecosystems and industry standards play in accelerating innovation.

### A Fully Open and Collaborative AI Ecosystem
Speakers: Mark Papermaster (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7732/
As AI reshapes data center design from racks to entire regions, open collaboration is more critical than ever. In this keynote, AMD CTO and EVP Mark Papermaster will highlight how the Open Compute Project’s blend of open hardware, software, and ecosystem partnerships is enabling friction-free adoption of AMD EPYC™ processors, Instinct™ GPU accelerators and Pensando NIC. He will share the significant progress on key OCP workstreams—from platform enablement and security to networking, RAS, and power/cooling—and unveil the momentum on Rack infrastructure, Open Software, and interconnect standards. Attendees will leave with a clear call to action for advancing open, energy-efficient AI infrastructure at scale.

### Infrastructure for a New Era: Powering AI at Frontier Scale
Speakers: Saurabh Dighe (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7725/

### What AI Wants: New Silicon, New Systems, and a New Era for the Data Center
Speakers: Mohamed Awad (Arm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7731/
AI is transforming every layer of the compute stack—and with it, the architecture of modern data centers. In this keynote, Arm’s SVP of Infrastructure, Mohamed Awad, will outline a vision for the next era of cloud and AI infrastructure: one built on energy- efficient, workload-optimized compute and enabled by open collaboration. As hyperscale cloud operators and the broader industry shift toward disaggregated systems and heterogeneous silicon, chiplet-based design is emerging as a key enabler of flexibility and innovation. Arm will share how its Arm Total Design ecosystem—including leaders from across the semiconductor supply chain—are working together to meet this moment, engaging within OCP initiatives to champion shared standards, emerging marketplaces, and co-designed solutions. The industry has an opportunity to build infrastructure that’s not only faster, but smarter, more open, and ready for the AI era.

### Networking for AI Scaling
Speakers: Ram Velaga (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7726/
As AI models grow in complexity and scale, building ultra-large AI clusters demands a reimagining of compute, memory, and I/O architectures to support fast, flexible, and recursive reasoning. This evolution is no longer confined to a single rack or even a single data center—it spans across entire geographies, interconnecting hundreds of thousands to millions of XPUs. Meeting these unprecedented demands requires a next-generation networking fabric that can: · Scale up within and across a few racks, seamlessly connecting thousands of XPUs; · Scale out across entire data centers in a flat, high-performance topology, supporting tens to hundreds of thousands of XPUs; · Scale across geographically distributed data centers, creating globally unified AI fabrics of million-plus XPUs. In this keynote, Broadcom will showcase: · Key innovations that unlock this scale—powered by open, standards-based Ethernet; · The latest advancements in the Scale-Up Ethernet (SUE) architecture, including product roadmap highlights for in-rack, inter-rack, and inter-DC connectivity.

### Scaling Data Center Infrastructure
Speakers: Ihab Tarazi (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7733/
We are building the future of AI on a foundation of open collaboration. For over a decade, Dell Technologies has been a driving force within the Open Compute Project, advancing open standards and scalable infrastructure that power the modern data center. Today, we’re applying that same commitment to openness to the world’s most ambitious AI platforms—helping customers unlock innovation at unprecedented scale. At Dell, we believe technology should drive human progress—and with AI reshaping industries and society, that mission has never been more urgent. We’ll explore how our open infrastructure approach is enabling AI to be deployed responsibly, securely, and for the benefit of all.

### Scaling AI at the Speed of Openness: From Silicon to Systems
Speakers: Sachin Katti (Intel Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7736/
AI is rapidly moving from “train once, deploy forever” driven by centralized clusters to real-world deployment that run continuously with agents reasoning, planning, and acting in real time, across AI factories, enterprise data centers, and the edge. Scaling inference at this pace demands more than powerful chips. It requires hardware innovation with memory and logic scaling. And manufacturing capabilities where supply chain will be a key differentiation. For agentic AI sustained throughput, low latency, memory orchestration, and system-level efficiency matter more than peak compute. And, where modular systems co-designed for performance, efficiency, flexibility, and developer continuity is addressed through deployable, rack-scale systems for agentic and inference-first AI. In this keynote, Sachin Katti will explore how shared innovation, openness, hardware diversity and system-level design are required to accelerate AI adoption, all in collaboration with its partners and the OCP community to purposefully deliver intelligence.

## Manageability Workshop

### Opening Remarks & gRPC vs REST Benchmarks using Redfish
Speakers: Jeff Hilland (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Bailey Hollis (TAMU)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7813/
The manageability world is wrestling with gRPC vs REST when it comes to the protocol used for transferring management information. While there are some benchmarks comparing the two, they neglect key comparisons. Those published often measure REST on HTTP 1.1 but Redfish supports both HTTP 1.1 and HTTP 2. And they don’t include full API transfer time, including marshalling and unmarshalling of the data. And what is the effect of CPU utilization, latency and bandwidth? Prior benchmarks neglect this information. This session reveals the results of a benchmark designed to indicate exactly where the benefits are for manageability workloads in a head to head comparison so that the manageability industry can assess the benefits.

### Aggregating the Redfish Model for Expansion Modules
Speakers: Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies), Hari Ramachandran (Microsoft Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7814/
As a system is created by combining complex compute modules from multiple sources, the Redfish client would value consistent behavior of the management model. DMTF has published the “Redfish Aggregation Guidance for Compute Expansion Modules” whitepaper. The whitepaper provides guidance to system integrators on the behavior of the Redfish data model when Redfish-enabled modules are added, such as a Universal Baseboard (UBB) containing OCP Accelerator Modules (OAM).

### Enabling Data Center Management with DMTF Redfish: Message Registry Opportunities and Technical Challenges
Speakers: Hari Ramachandran (Microsoft Corporation), Chitkala Sethuraman (Microsoft Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7815/
The rapid evolution and increasing complexity of modern data centers demand standardized, scalable, and interoperable management solutions. The Distributed Management Task Force (DMTF) Redfish specification has emerged as a next-generation protocol designed to replace legacy interfaces like IPMI, offering a RESTful interface, JSON data format, and OData modeling for secure and efficient hardware management. Central to Redfish’s alerting and event capabilities is the Message Registry—a standardized framework for defining, communicating, and interpreting management messages. This paper explores the transformational potential of Redfish in data centers, focusing on the practical adoption of the Message Registry, its role in automation and interoperability, and the technical challenges. We examine both the opportunities and limitations , providing insights into the challenges of integrating Redfish into existing infrastructure and the considerations necessary for future scalability.

### Streaming Telemetry Proposal Update
Speakers: Jeff Autor (Vertiv)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7816/
This session will provide an update on the streaming telemetry proposal first presented at last year’s workshop. Feedback has been incorporated to prioritize telemetry integration with popular time series database clients. The updated approach focuses on an improved payload design, interoperable methods to create time-label-value (TLV) records, and an easier transition for existing MetricReport implementations.

### Introduction to DMTF Authorization Specification
Speakers: Scott Phuong (Microsoft), Raghu Krishnamurthy (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7817/
This presentation introduces the DMTF SPDM Authorization specification (DSP0289), a crucial component for enhancing the security of platform firmware. We will explore how this specification extends the existing SPDM protocol to enable robust, policy-driven access control for device management. Attendees will gain an understanding of the core concepts, including credentials and policies, and features that are planned for incorporation into the CXL specification. The presentation also covers enhancements to the Secure Message specification (DSP0277) that allow more efficient transmission of Authorized messages. Through practical examples and use cases, we will demonstrate how SPDM Authorization facilitates secure and flexible management of platform resources, addressing critical security challenges in modern data centers and edge devices. This session is ideal for engineers and security professionals interested in firmware security, platform management, and supply chain integrity.

### Enhancements to PLDM Firmware Update
Speakers: Patrick Caporale (Lenovo), Yuval Itkin (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7818/
The PLDM Firmware Update standard (DSP0267) has enabled a consistent and common method for updating code on a wide variety of devices. Through the years, the standard has added additional features which provide even more flexibility, error handling, and support for downstream devices. This session will discuss the recent additional and what is available in the latest 1.3 spec version while also giving a preview of some major enhancements coming for 1.4 in the near future.

### Advancing PLDM: Structured Sensors, Scalable Modeling, and the Server Host SoC PLDM Specification
Speakers: Justin King (AMD), Samer El-Haj-Mahmoud (Arm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7819/
As server platforms grow in complexity—with more cores, memory controllers, accelerators, and sensors—existing PLDM mechanisms face limitations in how systems are described and managed. This session presents early DMTF work to extend PLDM with scalable and structured modeling capabilities, including Structured PLDM Sensors, Scalable Entity Association PDRs, and Clone PDRs. We will also introduce a new DMTF informational specification for modeling Server Host SoCs, which builds on these extensions to define sensors and effecters across SoC subsystems including cores, memory, PCIe, and networking. These innovations aim to improve interoperability, enable structured data representation, and enhance manageability for complex and modular systems. The session shares current work-in-progress from the DMTF PMCI working group and invites engagement from the OCP community to align future server design and firmware implementations.

### New Tests to Verify Redfish Service Conformance; libspdm: Past, Present, and Future; PMCI Tools Task Force Update and Demonstration
Speakers: Mike Raineri (Dell Technologies), Steven Bellock (NVIDIA), Patrick Schoeller (Intel)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7820/
This presentation, as part of the broader DMTF Tools Panel Discussion, will introduce the PMCI Tools TF GitHub Repository with an expected release schedule for the updated DSP0280 PMCI Test Tools Interface and Design Specification. A review of the component architecture will be presented: PMCI Protocol Validator, Test Service Library, and Control Plane. Key Features of the PMCI Tools TF deliverables: - Universal (open source with expected 80-95% compiled compatibility) - Statically linked or a stand-alone DLL style library -Allows for diversity in BMC (Control Plane) integrations; Designed to allow BMC operations to co-exist with the Test Service Library PMCI transactions. -PMCI-Protocol-Validator provides consistent stimulus & verification of PMCI WG protocols on a device, using the Control Plane as a pass-through support agent.

Speakers: Rami Radi (American Megatrends (AMI))

Enterprises adopting OCP infrastructure often face significant operational and integration challenges, particularly when managing diverse hardware, firmware and monitoring tools. This session shares real-world scenarios from enterprise customers, demonstrating how they successfully scaled OCP adoption by leveraging vendor-agnostic management solutions that take full advantage of open standards. We will explore some of the key challenges they encountered, including unifying server management and ensuring firmware compatibility and flexibility across vendors. We will also highlight how these capabilities improve operational efficiency and reliability. In addition, the session will touch on the role of AIOps in simplifying infrastructure workflows and reducing administrative overhead. Attendees will leave with practical strategies for addressing common enterprise barriers to OCP adoption and aligning with OCP’s principles of openness, efficiency, and sustainability.

### Liquid Cooling: Test, Validate & Deploy at Scale, a Panel Discussion with Hyperscalers
Speakers: Cheng Chen (Meta), Feini Zhang (Google), Jason Adrian, Sean Sivapalan (NVIDIA), Andy Regimbal (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7374/
As hyperscalers drive the transition from server-level to rack-scale liquid cooling, manufacturing these complex systems at scale presents unprecedented challenges in production processes, quality assurance, and deployment strategies. The choice between wet shipping (pre-filled systems) versus dry shipping (field-filled systems) represents more than logistics, it fundamentally reshapes manufacturing workflows, testing protocols, supply chain operations, and quality control at hyperscale volumes. This panel brings together senior executives from major hyperscalers, moderated by Hyve Solutions, a leading hyperscale ODM, to discuss the strategic realities of rack-level liquid cooling deployment. The discussion will explore executive-level decisions around wet versus dry shipping strategies, rack-level testing and validation approaches, and the critical need for industry standardization to streamline and enable liquid cooling at global scale.

### When the Chips are Down: Assessment of Vulnerability to Disruptions to the Supply of Critical Semiconductors
Speakers: Alex Newkirk (Berkeley National Lab)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7048/ | materials: https://fas.org/publication/strategic-microelectronics-reserve/
Modern life depends on semiconductors, yet we lack visibility into the consequences of losing access to specific devices and fabs. To address this, we combine expert elicitation spanning critical sectors with detailed microelectronics origin assessments of key products to map supply risk. We find that memory, high-performance logic, and commodity ICs pose the greatest risk, though nearly every chip category proved catastrophic to lose. Existing infrastructure weathers disruptions well, but all new production grinds to a halt. Experts warned that engineering talent shifts wholesale from innovation to firefighting. Each substitution approach requires 18-36 months, creating a window of vulnerability. Strategies to improve resilience include modular architectures, open standards for foundational devices, and design-for-portability, approaches aligned with the open-compute hardware ethos. While yesterday's chips keep today running, tomorrow's innovations hang by a silicon thread.

Speakers: Marcos Schwarz (RNP)

The innovations presented in a new generation of mobile communications networks are based on the use cases and scenarios envisioned by countries that invest in technology development, but do not always take into account the demands and requirements of other nations. Therefore, continuous action is needed in the design and standardization process of mobile networks to ensure that national needs are met by future generations and avoid a technology gap within the country. Check out how Brazil is moving forward and paving the infrastructure for the next generation of mobile networks!

### The Immediate Impact of Open Networking – The Rakuten Use Case
Speakers: Amir Elbaz (BE Networks), Brijesh Yadav (Rakuten), Shinsaku Shimizu (Rakuten Mobile)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7180/
Rakuten is a Japanese technology conglomerate based in Tokyo founded in 1997. It has several divisions focusing on online retail, financial services, digital content and communications services, carrier technology platform (Rakuten Symphony) and Japan's fourth largest mobile carrier. Rakuten has more than 28,000 employees worldwide, operating in 30 countries and regions. With its scale and increased demand for agility, Rakuten looked for Open Networking to improve its business resiliency and lessen vendor dependency, go deeper on its network virtualization; and lastly reduce its CapEx and Opex to deliver greater shareholder value. With its partnership with BE Networks and Broadcom, Rakuten deployed OCP based whiteboxes with multiple SONiC NOS distributions and BE’s Verity. Both deployments were deemed very successful and demonstrated great value. In this presentation we will share the outcomes and lessons learned.

### Deploying OCP Inside National Critical Infrastructure
Speakers: Arjun Hemrajani (Excloud)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6722/
National Critical Infrastructure (NCI) includes essential systems such as power grids, banking networks, stock exchanges, and their data centers—assets that are fundamental to a nation’s economy, security, and sovereignty. Governments provide these infrastructures with heightened protection, both physically and digitally. In this talk, we share how we deployed Open Compute Project (OCP) racks at a finance facility, which operates key financial platforms for the India. By adopting open, disaggregated hardware and 48V power delivery, we improved density, energy efficiency, and operational control—while aligning with national objectives for trust, resilience, and vendor independence.

### Taiwanese AI Supply Chain: In Preparation for the AI Infrastructure Boom
Speakers: Eric Huang (DIGITIMES Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7134/
The global surge in AI workloads has driven substantial investment in AI data centers. With the rise of sovereign AI initiatives, AI is set to become the next major infrastructure investment after the internet, 4G, and 5G. Taiwan’s semiconductor and computing hardware supply chain works closely with major AI accelerator vendors and hyperscalers to enable next-generation AI development. As a long-standing technology intelligence provider in Asia, DIGITIMES has observed strategic developments within Taiwan’s supply chain in response to technological and geopolitical challenges. This session will present insights on how leading Taiwanese ODM/EMS providers, such as Foxconn, and semiconductor companies, including TSMC, are adapting their global presence to support sovereign AI goals and modular infrastructure demands. It will also examine how Taiwan’s supply chain aligns with OCP-style hardware design and contributes to the scalability of open infrastructure.

Speakers: Noel Chakkalakal (Fish And Richardson), James Davies (Amazon Web Services), Jeremy Hopwood (5 Iron Cyber)

Join us to learn why strong digital protection is crucial for OCP's security, growth, and future.

There has been a significant shift in the interest of Europe into Open Source and Sovereign systems, How can OCP play a key role in that? OCP has earned it stripes in Europe in the AI/ML learning, and has taken water cooling along. Although we are having more conversations on how ORv3 will benefit data centers and server operators, adoption is still slow. ScaleUp is in the process of developing its Experience center 2.0 to showcase the new OCP technologies. However, the supply chain is starting to fall behind. Although there are requests for open hardware, getting the parts & Systems on time, at a competitive cost is proving to be the crucial next step in the adoption plan. The community involvement of standards such as the OAI, OAI-OAM, and OAI-UBB enables design reuse and faster product development of the entire server.

### Enabling an Open Ecosystem for 100% AI Infrastructure Testing
Speakers: Hani Daou (MultiLane)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7245/
The rack topologies necessary for AI workloads introduce unprecedented sensitivity, high-density computational infrastructure, which poses unique challenges for a full open ecosystem. Such stringent requirements have created a 100% testing mandate to ensure flawless interconnection between the countless components in an AI rack. Maintaining an open ecosystem in line with this mandate means creating solutions to test at scale while meeting cost expectations. This is an area of core competency for MultiLane, who have pioneered ultra-high-density testing solutions to meet the challenges of the AI era. In this presentation, MultiLane will highlight specific usecases of comprehensive, high-volume, 100% testing, demonstrating how the company is enabling OCP interconnects to stay open, inclusive, and scalable; answering the call of a mutual market need and helping to lay the foundations for a resilient digital infrastructure from the physical layer upwards.

### Federal Energy Research At the Nexus of High-Density, Large-Scale Compute and the Grid
Speakers: Cosimo Pecchioli (Castrol (BP)), Arman Shehabi (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory), Sarah Smith (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory), Dale Sartor (Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7849/
Come help shape the federal data center energy research agenda and work at the U.S. Department of Energy’s Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory (LBNL). To inform that work, LBNL and industry partners are soliciting industry inputs for: Prioritizingthe most challenging technical barriers in powering and cooling high-density compute. Providing expert insight into industry-wide practices and trends in the selection of IT equipment across data center types and workloads, for use in calibrating industry-wide models of U.S. data center energy use . Federal energy researchers are working to support large-scale data centers and high-density compute while ensuring stability of the U.S. grid. LBNL and partners are conducting a listening session to help shape the federal research agenda and ongoing activities. Data Center Initiatives - Government initiatives will be introduced, and representatives will be available for discussion. What roles should the national labs play in addressing the technical challenges facing the data center industry? Advanced Cooling and Power Challenges - The federal government is funding research, development and testing of advanced IT and infrastructure systems (e.g., ARPA-E’s COOLERCHIPS program), as well as strategies for demand flexibility. We will ask for expert input on identifying and tackling strategic technological challenges associated with serving high-density computing loads (e.g., high-/medium-voltage direct current power distribution, hybrid cold-plate/immersion cooling systems, demand flexibility enabling technologies). Forecasting Data Center Energy Use - DOE is launching an update to LBNL’s 2024 United States Data Center Energy Usage Report to Congress. Lab researchers would like to solicit your feedback on industry trends and averages. We are particularly interested in expert views on how servers and storage vary across space types and how network equipment varies across use types.

## Networking

### OCP Optical Circuit Switching Subproject Update
Speakers: Brian O'Connor (Google), Richard Yu (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7331/
The Optical Circuit Switching (OCS) subproject, a new initiative within the OCP Networking project, aims to improve data center and AI/ML networking. Formed in June, our working group has been actively developing open software standards and a fully open software stack for OCS. We're addressing the urgent need for high bandwidth, low latency, and energy-efficient solutions driven by the exponential growth of AI, machine learning, and high-performance computing. Our mission is to standardize and advance OCS as an open, scalable, and efficient solution, empowering users and manufacturers through collaboration, interoperability, and innovation. We are committed to the OCP vision of openness, scalability, efficiency, and community-driven innovation, accelerating the adoption of OCS in next-generation network architectures.

### Enabling Scalable Infrastructure: Alibaba’s High-Performance 102.4T Ethernet Switch
Speakers: Zhiping Yao (Alibaba Group)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7153/
In this talk, we’ll unveil Alibaba’s next-generation high-performance 102.4Tbps Ethernet switch—built from the ground up for our forthcoming high-performance network infrastructure. We’ll walk through: Design philosophy : key considerations that drove our hardware and software choices System architecture : a hyperscale, multi-tier, multi-plane topology supporting both 800 GbE and 1600 GbE variants Electrical and power challenges : balancing density, signal integrity and efficiency, integrating both air- and liquid-cooling solutions for optimal heat management Optical integration : overcoming signal loss, reach and interoperability hurdles By maximizing component and software reuse across product families, this architecture delivers the scalability, flexibility and performance today’s hyperscale environments demand.
