### Next generation co-packaged optics systems
Speakers: Jason Wildt (Jabil), Kishore Atreya (Marvell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7013/
Marvell and Jabil will discuss a planned contribution of a co-packaged optics switch system as an open design. In this talk we will provide an overview of the system design including liquid cooling, mid-plane fiber management and attach to light engines, and co-packaged substrates and their impact on system design. The system design is meant to serve two purposes, first, as a desktop evaluation platform and second as a rack mountable ORV3 or ORV4 compliant system. The system is a liquid cooled design with 16 ELSFPs mounted in the front and 64 SN-MT high density optical connectors which enable this switch to fit in a small 1OU form factor. With over 1024 fibers to the front panel for data, and 128 fibers for light source, serving as a proof point for CPO addressing rack scale connectivity in scale up or out. The optical and electrical design of the system is 224G ready taking advantage of Marvell's advanced 6.4T Light Engine supporting 32 channels of 224G linear signaling.

### Evolution of Ethernet based switch platforms and fabrics to meet Meta's AI training clusters' scale-out AI fabric demands
Speakers: Parvez Shaikh (Meta), Lingjun Wu (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6811/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ccHcLvHZit4 | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI
At OCP 2024, we showcased how we evolved SAI and FBOSS to tackle challenges with building reliable non-blocking cell-based fabrics for interconnecting clusters of up to 4K GPUs using disaggregated deep-buffered switch platforms. Evolution of Generative AI training workload demands significantly larger non-blocking clusters. In this talk, we will share our journey evolving disaggregated shallow-buffer based Ethernet platforms for building larger fabrics and GPU clusters. We will discuss switching and routing platforms and features that have enabled us to build significantly larger non-blocking fabrics and some unique challenges we encountered while scaling them. Additionally, we will highlight the SAI and FBOSS enhancements that were instrumental in adapting FBOSS to support the new features.

### AI - Packet Trimming
Speakers: Prince Sunny (Microsoft), Kumaresh Perumal (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7025/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI/blob/master/doc/SAI-Proposal-Packet-Trimming.md
In the world of AI network, when the lossy queue exceeds a buffer threshold, it drops packets without any notification to the destination host.When a packet is lost, it can be recovered through fast retransmission or by using timeouts. Retransmission triggered by timeouts typically incurs significant latency. In order to help the host recover data more quickly and accurately, packet trimming feature is utilized in such a way that upon a failed packet admission to a shared buffer, will trim a packet to a configured size, and try sending it on a different queue to deliver a packet drop notification to an end host. Based on OCP SAI - https://github.com/opencomputeproject/SAI/blob/master/doc/SAI-Proposal-Packet-Trimming.md

### eCore: Architecture and Whitebox Evolution of Alibaba’s Service-oriented DCI network
Speakers: Dennis Cai (Alibaba), Manish Mukherjee (Cisco)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7064/
eCore is Alibaba’s cutting-edge Data Center Interconnect (DCI) network, engineered to redefine scalability and efficiency through a modular, whitebox-based architecture and service-aware traffic forwarding powered by SRv6. This talk, presented jointly by Alibaba and Cisco, delves into its design philosophy, deployment challenges, and measurable impact—alongside Alibaba’s custom whitebox solutions and roadmap for the future.

### In Network Collective acceleration for AI Fabrics
Speakers: Surendra Anubolu (Broadcom Inc), Nikhil Shetty (Oracle)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7349/
For AI/ML (and HPC) workloads, collectives such as All Reduce, ReduceScatter and AllGather are a critical portion of the job completion time. GPU based collectives are more limited by communication bandwidth than the computation. Network switches offer a perfect place to offload as the switches offer a high radix and throughput they can sink. Performing the reduction operator in the switch-fabric (network), can reduce network bandwidth by half compared to what is achievable with GPU based reductions. This additionally enables higher MFU and lower memory footprint at the GPU end points. We will present an INC offload solution for Ethernet networks implemented in a high-performance low latency ethernet switch. We will also share performance measurements and application benefits from the network offload of collective operations. In addition, we will provide an update on the standards activity happening in relevant communities such as UEC (Ultra Ethernet Consortium) and OCP SAI.

### Accelerating AI Hardware NPI: Cluster-less Validation of GPUs and Networking
Speakers: Matt Bergeron (Meta), Ashutosh Kumar (Keysight Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7360/
The rapid pace of AI hardware, notably GPUs and AI accelerators, creates challenges in validating these systems. Traditionally, a single system has been validated standalone; but this has proven insufficient for complex AI systems. Based on system or rack validation, a large physical cluster is typically constructed, wherein collectives and compute are evaluated at the AI model level. Unfortunately, numerous hardware and software issues are discovered at this stage; things missed at system validation time. This results in a very long NPI process, negatively impacting TTP (time-to-production.) To address these challenges, a validation methodology utilizing collective and network emulation is proposed. By emulating the network interfaces, a single GPU-under-test can perceive itself as communicating within a cluster of thousands of simulated peers. This enhances single-system test coverage and provides a mechanism to reduce the overall time required to bring new hardware to production.

### SAI Specifications for newer technologies introduced by Ultra Ethernet Consortium
Speakers: Jai Kumar (Broadcom), Ravindranath Coimbatore Kanakarajan (Marvell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6780/
Ultra Ethernet Consortium is working on bringing next-gen technologies for AI/ML Fabrics. In this session, we will talk about the set of technologies that need corresponding support in SAI. We will go over Layer 2 enhancements done to Ethernet like link layer retry, Credit Based Flow Control, as well as talk about the new network telemetry data needed for various congestion control algorithms.

### SRv6 for AI Backend
Speakers: Changrong Wu (Microsoft), Abhishek Dosi (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7344/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ZBnn_YOGhp0 | materials: https://www.segment-routing.net/conferences/2025-ocp-emea-microsoft-srv6-ai-backend/
In Ethernet-based AI backend networks, routing has become a significant challenge because traditional BGP+ECMP schemes can no longer meet the unprecedented and stringent communication requirements of AI training jobs. We innovatively use Segment Routing over IPv6 (SRv6), originally designed for traffic engineering across wide-area network, in our AI backend networks to provide fine-grained network path control, maximizing network utilization and delivering excellent fabric resiliency. In this presentation, we aim to showcase our methodology for using SRv6 in AI backend networks, focusing on how we can use SRv6 to enable continental-scale GPU AI clusters.

### Accurate Cable Length Measurement for Optimized Data Center Networking
Speakers: Jasmeet Bagga (Facebook), Mehak Mahajan (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7365/
Precise cable length measurement plays a critical role in optimizing network design, performance, and operational efficiency in modern data center environments. One effective approach is to infer cable length through link delay measurement. Silicon driven link delay measurement delivers high precision and simplified deployment. In this session, Meta will showcase a real-world deployment of this capability, highlighting its use cases and the operational benefits observed through accurate cable length and performance monitoring. Broadcom will provide a technical deep dive into the delay measurement methodology, including relevant SAI (Switch Abstraction Interface) attributes and APIs that support the solution.

### Alert Fatigue in Hyperscale Environments: A Metrics-Based Approach to Signal Tuning in Open Networks
Speakers: Pooja Gupte (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7424/
As hyperscale infrastructures expand, alert fatigue has become a growing challenge—especially in physical network environments where failures span multiple layers, from server-to-ToR to packet loss above ToR. At Microsoft, we use a metrics-driven approach to refine and prioritize alert signals in networks built with OCP-recognized hardware. This session shares how key metrics like Time to Detect (TTD), Time to Mitigate (TTM), and Black Box to White Box Alert Ratios help reduce noise, improve correlation, and surface actionable alerts. We’ll walk through real-world tuning strategies for alert thresholds, deduplication techniques, and intelligent alert routing that enable teams to focus on what matters most. We’ll also explore how open telemetry from multi-vendor OCP hardware powers scalable alerting across hyperscale networks. Attendees will gain a practical framework for reducing alert fatigue and improving reliability across disaggregated, open infrastructure.

### OpenBMC and FBOSS on Network Switch
Speakers: Dean Kalla (Arista Networks), Mike Choi (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7057/ | materials: https://github.com/facebook/fboss/
This presentation details integrating open source FBOSS and OpenBMC on network switches, leveraging BMC-ready designs and their respective open-source stacks for comprehensive control plane and data plane management. A key focus is the common hardware designs with BMC-Lite architecture, allowing BMC to perform key out of band features, but offloads the switch management to the host CPU. A core initiative involves migrating critical hardware management from the traditional BMC to the host CPU, streamlining operations. The presentation will delve into the OpenBMC stack, the latest advancements in FBOSS OSS, and config-driven hardware platform descriptions, enabling extensible FBOSS support across diverse network switches.

### Deterministic ISSU Qualification
Speakers: Ryan Garthwaite (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7061/ | materials: https://github.com/sonic-net/sonic-mgmt
ISSU is a tricky and delicate process for a data center switch and requires multiple entities (SW and HW) to sync and converge from an old state to a new state (after upgrade). The convergence in software requires reconciliation of multiple DBs and services. The convergence in hardware requires SAI/SDK data migration from old version to new. All this is done without causing a single packet drop! The challenge in qualification is to ensure that reconciliation, convergence and migration are transparent and deterministic (to know what really has/has not changed after ISSU upgrade).The uber goal for ISSU is to have 1) state (SW and HW) which is same as cold rebooted state. 2) Deterministic and fully vetted differences in the SW and HW state of the device before and after upgrade. These strict criteria is enforced in SONiC by an extensive qualification to determine, assess and vet the system across the upgrade.

### AVS for vendor sim and topologies (with Broadcom, Arista)
Speakers: Sonika Jindal (Google), Runip Gopisetty (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7101/
Alpine Virtual Switch, upcoming open source SONiC VS, allows 3P virtual ASIC integration for software testing. We cover key achievements through partnership w/ Broadcom & Arista Networks BCMSIM integration: BCMSIM Device Models are high fidelity virtual datapath models of Broadcom XGS devices available for over 20 years. vBridge provides alternative connectivity to the BCMSIM framework to the ALPINE platform, giving high speed packet & link level connectivity of latest Bcm XGS Device Models to AVS. BCMSIM along with vBridge run in a container within the AVS Pod, connecting to the container's veth for data path & listening on TCP port for control path connections from SAI/SDK. Independent thread monitors netlink events for link state. SONiC testing w/AVS: We will cover the deployment of a standalone DUT, configuration & SONiC topologies with AVS. It is crucial for enabling a wide array of community-driven use cases with large scale deployment of virtual networks using Aktive.

### The Immediate Impact of Open Networking ‚ The Rakuten Use Case
Speakers: Amir Elbaz (BE Networks), Brijesh Yadav (Rakuten)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7890/
Rakuten is a Japanese technology conglomerate based in Tokyo founded in 1997. It has several divisions focusing on online retail- financial services- digital content and communications services- carrier technology platform (Rakuten Symphony) and Japan's fourth largest mobile carrier. Rakuten has more than 28-000 employees worldwide- operating in 30 countries and regions.¬†\nWith its scale and increased demand for agility- Rakuten looked for Open Networking to improve its business resiliency and lessen vendor dependency- go deeper on its network virtualization; and lastly reduce its CapEx and Opex to deliver greater shareholder value. With its partnership with BE Networks and Broadcom- Rakuten deployed OCP based whiteboxes with multiple SONiC NOS distributions and BE‚Äôs Verity. Both deployments were deemed very successful and demonstrated great value. In this presentation we will share the outcomes and lessons learned.

## Open Platform Firmware (OPF)

### Open Platform Firmware (OPF) project update, OCP projects’ firmware scope delineation
Speakers: Murugasamy Nachimuthu (Independent), Raj Kapoor (AMD), Jean-Marie Verdun (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Dong Wei (ARM)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7425/
As the platform continues to evolve to support modern technologies, continuous improvements in the area of platform firmware are needed to enable the eco-system to deliver these new technologies. The Open Platform Firmware (OPF) project is making significant progress to address these challenges. This session will provide updates on the OPF project accomplishments, milestones and the additional initiatives under consideration. The session will also outline the firmware related scope delineation with other OCP projects.

### From Vendor-Specific to Vendor-Neutral: Advancing Silicon Firmware Abstraction with openSFI
Speakers: Allison Goodman (Intel), Raj Kapoor (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7204/
As AI workloads reshape data center infrastructure, hyperscalers, CSPs, and platform integrators demand more modular, agile, and interoperable firmware. The Open Platform Firmware (OPF) project is incubating openSFI, an open, architecture-neutral interface standardizing how host firmware interacts with silicon init and runtime logic across vendors. This session will showcase progress since its OCP Global 2024 launch: v0.3 spec milestones, AMD/Intel proof-of-concept work, and steps toward ISA neutrality. We’ll highlight what’s coming in v0.5: lifecycle-aware and stateless init/runtime APIs, integration with the Unified Platform Configuration Interface (UPCI), and community-driven abstraction layers enabling plug-and-play firmware design. Join us to see how openSFI is building a path toward sustainable, multivendor firmware for AI-era platforms through open collaboration.

### Defining a Unified Platform Configuration Interface
Speakers: Felix Polyudov (AMI), Tim Lewis (Insyde Software)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7312/
As firmware architectures grow more modular and ecosystem-driven, the need for a standardized, interpretable format for configuration data exchange becomes critical. Today, each silicon vendor relies on proprietary formats to receive configuration data from the host firmware. This model lacks cross-vendor compatibility and adaptability, hampering efforts to unify platform firmware stacks. The Unified Platform Configuration Interface (UPCI) addresses this challenge by introducing a compact, and schema-driven format for host firmware to silicon initialization firmware data exchange designed to be vendor-neutral and framework-agnostic. This talk will outline the motivations behind UPCI, walk through key design choices such as encoding formats and memory tradeoffs, and provide a forward-looking roadmap for how UPCI will underpin multi-vendor adoption of the openSFI specification.

### Firmware update and reconfiguration: a path forward
Speakers: Jean-Marie Verdun (HPE), Gilles Lasnier (OVHcloud)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6992/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-OPF-Backend
Firmware updates and system reconfigurations are critical functions in modern servers. These operations are often slow to execute due to the lack of standards and unified tooling ecosystem which expose servers to complex threats and increase DC interventions. From real examples at OVHcloud, we will describe these issues and propose enhancements to the firmware lifecycle management, to ease automation, and speed up systems restart following firmware updates. These enhancements will be based on common work performed with HPE, under the OCP umbrella to deploy flashless systems and decoupling firmware load operations from its payload. We will demonstrate how we could improve boot time ,reduce platform complexity, and provide a common firmware update interface to enhance host systems provisioning and wipe out operations. Our goal is to drastically reduce system downtime during firmware updates and provide a fully open-source solution based on industry standard interconnect: ethernet.

### Streaming and flash-less boot system- a detailed methodology is proposed for secure and authenticated Firmware update
Speakers: Munir Ahmad (Lattice Semiconductor), Ching-Yu (Lawrence) Lo (Intel)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7290/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/Caliptra/issues/213 | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=h2DdkbJQARs&t=244s
Although the OCP flash-less boot initiative aims to eliminate or reduce reliance on local flash memory for system boot—particularly in devices such as CPUs—a detailed implementation strategy has not yet been defined. Key aspects remain unclear, including how and where the primary and main firmware are downloaded and stored, how this firmware is secured and authenticated, and how it is ultimately delivered to the CPU. During our efforts to implement a flash-less boot system in a next-generation server platform, we encountered several significant challenges. In this paper, we present the obstacles we faced. To address these challenges, we developed and evaluated several methodologies, including, a dual-stage firmware delivery model where the BMC streams the primary firmware into a secure memory region, followed by authenticated transfer of the main firmware. Fallback and recovery mechanisms is also discussed to handle firmware delivery failures or authentication mismatches

### Bridging the Gap: Bringing HPE Gen11 into the OpenBMC Mainline
Speakers: Jean-Marie Verdun (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Christian Walter (9elements)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6799/
This talk outlines the collaborative effort between HPE and 9elements to bring upstream OpenBMC support to HPE’s DL360 Gen11. Starting from a 3-year-old downstream fork, we modernized the firmware stack and overcame challenges related to secure boot, image signing, and remote validation. Learn how the osfci.tech platform enabled continuous hardware testing during development and how HPE’s ownership process affects firmware agility. We’ll also introduce our new managed OpenBMC support offering for HPE platforms, designed to make enterprise open firmware a practical choice.

### BMC and AMC Open Platform Firmware Development on Arm Servers: From Simulation to Hardware
Speakers: Samer El-Haj-Mahmoud (Arm), Dwarka Partani (ASPEED Technology Inc)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6875/ | materials: https://community.arm.com/arm-community-blogs/b/architectures-and-processors-blog/posts/advancing-server-manageability-arm-css-openbmc
This presentation showcases an open development environment for platform firmware on Arm-based servers, built through collaboration between Arm and ASPEED. The focus is enabling open platform firmware development for the BMC and the SoC’s on-chip Auxiliary Management Controller (AMC), using both simulation and hardware. We demonstrate platforms built using Arm’s Neoverse Fixed Virtual Platform (FVP) to simulate a server SoC, including a Management Control Processor (MCP) acting as the AMC, and ASPEED’s AST 2600 EVK board as physical BMC targets. As an additional hardware option, the AST1030 serves as an AMC running OpenBIC firmware. These platforms support rapid prototyping and bring-up of opensource firmware, including OpenBMC and OpenBIC. The session highlights how simulation and low-cost hardware accelerate development, expand test coverage, and promote collaborative innovation. A live demo will show end-to-end platform management using these open technologies.

### Building Open Firmware for Open Platforms: openSIL & The Road to Full Silicon Transparency
Speakers: Raj Kapoor (AMD), Srini Narayana (American Megatrends (AMI))
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7511/
As open platforms become central to datacenter innovation, the demand for robust, scalable, and production-grade open-source firmware is growing across enterprise and cloud service providers. Project Mu, an open-source UEFI implementation, is designed with scalability and maintainability at its core. AMD’s openSIL is an emerging framework for silicon initialization designed to align with this trend, enabling modular, host-agnostic firmware integration. This session will explore how Tianocore’s EDKII, Microsoft’s Project Mu, and AMD’s openSIL are being used together to build flexible, transparent firmware solutions for next-generation datacenter platforms. While openSIL is still evolving toward full silicon coverage, platforms like AMD Venice are pioneering its integration. We’ll also highlight AMD’s proposed open-source governance model for openSIL and how it sets the stage for long-term ecosystem sustainability and community collaboration.

### Hyperscale CPU Impactless Firmware Updates
Speakers: Giri Mudusuru (Microsoft), Justin King (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7361/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/hyperscale-cpu-impactless-firmware-updates-requirements-specification-v0-5-2025-05-22-pdf
Microsoft, Google & AMD are excited to announce the release of a specification for Hyperscale CPU Impactless Firmware Updates. This specification represents a significant leap forward in enhancing the operational efficiency, availability, and interoperability of hyperscale data centers. By standardizing the capabilities for impactless updates of microcode and silicon vendor firmware in CPUs, it addresses the critical need for delivering new firmware while maintaining system uptime in modern data centers. The specification includes mechanisms for both in-band and out-of-band (OOB) updates, ensuring streamlined communication and updates across different channels. It also covers notification mechanisms to inform the control plane of update completions, package formats for secure updates, and optional customer co-signing for added protection. This specification is set to propel the industry forward, offering mutual benefits to CPU suppliers, hyperscalers, and the broader OCP community.

### Transforming Firmware in the Age of AI: Insights, Challenges, and Opportunities
Speakers: Ranjeet Kumar (AMD), Allison Goodman (Intel), Phillip Leech (HPE), DJ Whang (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7437/
Join a discussion amongst industry firmware experts exploring the transformative impact of artificial intelligence on our work and the broader ecosystem. AI is reshaping the way firmware is written, validated, and maintained. While we learn and gain insights to generative AI applications within our companies, we can also look at how genAI will aid and accelerate the secure integration and interoperability of firmware across the open-source ecosystem. The discussion will share key learnings from integrating genAI into firmware processes, identify areas where it has proven invaluable and where challenges still persist. The panelists will also highlight where genAI could apply and accelerate the OPF workstreams and open-source unification and interoperability goals.

## OpenRAN (Special Focus)

### OpenRAN at OCP
Speakers: Brad Stimpson (Bell Canada)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7708/
OpenRAN at OCP

### Open RAN Acceleration with Optical Transport Capillarization and Softwarization
Speakers: Masahisa Kawashima (NTT Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7791/
While open RAN enables the deployment of RAN functions on a cloud computing platform, today’s RAN depends on static and manual fiber cabling. This makes RAN hardly benefit from the cloud-based architecture. IOWN Global Forum has been working to address this issue with a disaggregated DWDM network called Open All-Photonic Network (Open APN.). Combining Open APN with O-RAN will enable RAN deployment with full-stack virtualization, i.e., from optical transport up to RAN functions. That said, this talk will present the overview of IOWN Global Forum’s activities and propose possible work items for OCP-IOWN Global Forum collaboration.

### The OpenRAN @ Brasil Program
Speakers: Lucas Bondan (RNP)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7333/ | materials: https://www.rnp.br/projetos/brasil6g/#
The OpenRAN@Brasil Program has been conceived as the largest research, development, and innovation initiative to promote open and disaggregated mobile networks across Brazil. In this presentation, the main objectives and progress achieved by the program will be described, with emphasis on the expansion of the testbed infrastructure and the engagement of new participants throughout the country. Building upon the foundations established in Phases 1 and 2, where the initial testbeds were implemented in Campinas and Rio de Janeiro through the integration of multiple components provided by different hardware and software manufacturers, the third phase has been designed to extend the initiative to all regions of Brazil. Through this phase, additional sites are being deployed in the North, Northeast, Midwest, and South, enabling geographically diverse experimentation and broader access to open infrastructure resources. In parallel, open calls have been launched to encourage research groups, startups, and companies to propose innovative 5G Open RAN applications in strategic verticals such as smart cities, agriculture, digital health, and immersive education. Selected projects are being supported with funding, mentorship, and access to the national testbed, allowing their solutions to be validated in realistic operational environments. During the talk, the testbed architecture, deployment processes, and collaboration mechanisms adopted by the program will be presented. The lessons learned, challenges faced, and strategies adopted to ensure scalability and sustainability will also be discussed. It is expected that the experiences shared from OpenRAN@Brasil will contribute to global efforts in building open, interoperable, and inclusive mobile network ecosystems.

### Open Radio Hardware with a Radio Abstraction Interface
Speakers: Laawanya Kishor (Cloudy.io), Rexy Geevarghese (Bell Canada)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7773/
Open Radio Hardware with a Radio Abstraction Interface

### The Role of Open Networks in Shaping 6G: Enabling Intelligent, Open, and Adaptive Networking
Speakers: Femi Adeyemi (1Finity)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7821/
The evolution towards 6G networks demands a paradigm shift in how networks are designed, deployed, and operated. Open Networking is emerging as a foundational enabler in this transformation journey, offering open interfaces, flexibility, disaggregation, observability and AI intelligence necessary to meet the unprecedented performance and service demands of 6G. This talk explores the pivotal role of Open RAN in shaping 6G by fostering vendor interoperability, software-defined control, and AI-native architectures that support real-time adaptability and context-aware decision-making. We will examine how Open networking facilitates a disaggregated and virtualized network architecture, paving the way for energy-efficient, low-latency, and scalable infrastructures. Additionally, we address the integration of advanced technologies - such as machine learning at the edge, massive MIMO, and new spectrum in the THz space - within the Open RAN framework to unlock the full potential of 6G use cases, including immersive XR, ubiquitous IoT, and autonomous systems. The talk will conclude by outlining the challenges and research directions in standardization, security, and orchestration, underscoring Open RAN’s transformative role in enabling intelligent, open, and adaptive 6G networks.

### 5G Small Cell: The Next Wave of Ubiquitous Connectivity for Smart and Secure Cities
Speakers: Hossam Fattah (Lattice Semiconductor)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7253/
5G ORAN small cell now supports Non-Terrestrial Networks (NTN) (a.k.a satellite ORAN) starting from 3GPP Release 17 where a small cell is incumbent in the satellite system. The addition of satellite connectivity is a significant step for smart cities, as satellites acting as the ORAN (Open Radio Access Network) have a larger coverage area than traditional cell towers. Not only does it cover ground devices and equipment, but it also provides connectivity to airborne devices such as airplanes and drones, rural towns, islands, vessels at sea, deserts, and for providing internet access to places affected by disasters or for global communication across diverse terrains. In this presentation, we demonstrate and delve into critical aspects of designing and implementing a 5G NTN-powered small cell.

### Autonomous Radio Units: Au-RUs
Speakers: Brad Deforge (Dell)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7823/
Due to the immense amount of engineering that goes into commercial cellular radios and radios in general, to meet specifications demanded by each MNO, RUs are often highly customized and simultaneously optimized for maximum efficiency, size, weight, and power – why? Due to the extreme amount of energy consumed by the mobile communications infrastructure – RU vendors develop very high complexity products for a low profitability market leading to few competitors and very high barriers to new entrants and competition. The market seems to demand commodity RUs. No one will be willing to lessen the efficiency, while MNO’s maintain a demanding stance on innovation and CapEx. This “lowest cost at all cost” market place results in limiting innovation investment for RU development at all but the largest suppliers. Long-term result is driving limited profitability for RU vendors and market exists for new entrants. This Au-RU concept, in its ideal form, would render the CapEx cost for RU’s as one of the least important factors, and enables operators to deploy RUs that can be monetized through different means, such as through data. It further opens up an eco-system for application of that data both within RUs themselves (NES – Network Energy Savings, and so on), and beyond as in a network deployment for such aspects including predictive maintenance and digital twins.

### OpenRAN Pave the Way to AI RAN
Speakers: Yaming Wang (Supermicro)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7847/
OpenRAN Pave the Way to AI RAN

### Panel: Accelerating Private Wireless and the Role of Open RU
Speakers: Roy Chua (AvidThink), Stéphane Téral (Teral Research), Brad Deforge (Dell), Femi Adeyemi (1Finity), Jonathan Polly (Cal Poly)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7710/
Private wireless provides a viable and valuable connectivity option to enterprises worldwide. Uptake outside of China has been more measured than expected. In this panel, we'll discuss the current challenges in deployment, relevant and critical use cases that drive the need for private wireless, and the role that an Open RU and OpenRAN could play in accelerating adoption.

## P4 Workshop

### Welcome & Keynote - High-Level and Target-Agnostic Transport Programs
Speakers: Fernando Ramos (University of Lisbon), Mina Tahmasbi Arashloo (University of Waterloo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7804/
Welcome and Awards by P4 Workshop General Chair, Fernando Ramos Keynote by Mina Tahmasbi Arashloo Over the past two decades, programming abstractions for packet processing have gained widespread adoption. These abstractions enable network operators to specify packet processing logic in high-level, domain-specific languages that are independent of the underlying hardware architecture of packet processing nodes. This approach has unlocked numerous benefits, including compiler-driven generation of efficient low-level implementations, portability across diverse execution environments, and automated testing and verification. Most existing abstractions, however, primarily focus on L2/L3 packet processing. In this talk, we highlight the need for new programming abstractions that capture the complexities of network mechanisms essential for quality of service, specifically transport protocols. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### DVaaS Detective: The Case of the Failing Tests
Speakers: Victor Rios (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7805/
At Google, the P4-Based Automated Reasoning (P4-BAR) team’s DVaaS (Dataplane Validation as a Service) utility validates switch forwarding behavior by automatically generating packet test vectors (input packets and expected output packets) using P4 as a machine-readable specification language to describe switch forwarding pipelines. We introduce DVaaS Detective, a macro-level bug analysis tool for switch validation that automatically identifies working and broken forwarding features from the result of DVaaS testing by automatically finding patterns among failures and successes, significantly reducing the cost of triaging test failures. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### Cisco Silicon One: Unifying Network Forwarding with P4 Program
Speakers: Anand Sridharan (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7806/
Cisco Silicon One offers a unified architecture with a P4 programmable Network Processing Unit, enabling consistent packet forwarding across service provider, enterprise, data center, and hyperscaler devices. This strategic adoption of P4 allows for deep data plane programmability, driving innovative features and unlocking new opportunities for network agility, performance in high-performance networks and the ability to personalize the forwarding pipeline based on market needs. I will share my experiences leading the P4 development team for Cisco Silicon One. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### Unifying P4 with eBPF and DPDK via XDP2
Speakers: Tom Herbert (XDPnet)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7807/
We discuss XDP2 and the unified for programming model. XDP2 takes the best from P4, XDP/eBPF, and DPDK to create a unified model that allos the user to program in the language of their choice, and to run their code without modification on a variety of targets. This solves a fundamantal problem being faced by many infrastructure developers and companies. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### New Dawn of P4
Speakers: Debashis Chatterjee (Intel)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7808/
As a high-level, domain-specific language, P4 is designed for controlling packet forwarding planes on networking devices, and its core objectives—Protocol Independence, Target Independence, and Reconfigurability—has created a powerful abstraction that decouples network logic from the underlying hardware. But the fundamental nature of network traffic is undergoing a transformation, under the proliferation of AI and HPC workloads – moving from unpredictable, bursty, North-South traffic to steady, high-volume East-West traffic. In this new era, instead of losing its relevance, P4 is actually going to play a very significant role, and that’s the new dawn of P4. In the immediate horizon, P4-based INT can help in the operation and administration of large, complex networks by providing aggregated telemetry information on device identifiers, timestamps, and link latencies, directly into the packet header as it traverses the network. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### XASM: A Foundation to Program the X2 With P4
Speakers: Fabian Ruffy (XSight Labs), Vladimir Gurevich (XSight Labs)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7809/
In this talk, we begin by introducing the X2 architecture, contrasting it with the traditional pipeline model exemplified by Tofino. We then demonstrate how XASM is used to program the X2, with concrete examples illustrating how P4 constructs can be mapped to it. Finally, we explore limitations in the current P4 language that make it challenging to express the full capabilities of the X2, and we outline potential paths for evolving P4 to support X2-style programming. In addition to the live P4 Workshop talks, we have a wide range of exciting pre-recorded talks you can check out on the P4 website (recordings to be released October 13, 2025).

### Keynote - Using P4 NICs for Resilient Scale-out GPU Interconnect
Speakers: Krishna Doddapaneni (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7810/
AI transports demand hardware-based solution for low latency, high throughput GPU interconnects. P4 seems a perfect match for demanding datapath transport enabled on programmable NIC for scale-out Ethernet fabrics. The industry is standardizing packet processing, memory transfers, message processing, reliability, multipathing, etc. over the last few years. And we are at the beginning of building and standardizing these solutions. As network components multiplies — from cables and NICs to switches and transceivers, failures become an inevitability. Chasing a myth of perfect reliability, a more practical approach is to design a programmable network that anticipates and handles these failures gracefully. This talk discusses how AMD leverages the P4 to build a robust solution that overcomes network failures, implementing a multi-plane architecture and advanced failure-handling mechanisms. How P4 capabilities demonstrate network data plane programmability is a foundational requirement in today’s demanding and unforgiving AI environments.

## Photonics (Special Focus)

### Evaluation of CPO Performance and Pluggable Optics Health for Reliable AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Siamak Amiralizadeh (Meta), VIRAL LOWALEKAR (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6813/
With escalating AI demands, the efficiency of data center power and network infrastructure is paramount. While contemporary optical transceivers are undergoing continuous improvement, co-packaged optics (CPO) present an opportunity for enhanced power and cost optimization through the integration of optics directly onto the ASIC. Nevertheless, CPO technology introduces considerations regarding reliability, availability and serviceability. This presentation outlines our comprehensive evaluation of CPO to address these concerns. Meta's data centers currently also utilize a diverse range of optical transceivers to support extensive AI/ML connectivity, necessitating robust link failure detection and long-term optical reliability. This discussion will detail our multi-month reliability testing methodologies for both current and next-generation optical technologies, including CPO, thereby ensuring scalable and dependable high-speed data center networks for AI/ML applications.

### Welcome Address
Speakers: Andy Bechtolsheim (Arista)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7698/
Welcome and Overview of the Photonics track

### High Bandwidth XSR Driven Optical Links for CPO
Speakers: Nikhil Kumar (Lightmatter)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7460/
AI demands transformative interconnects for bandwidth & power. We present an industry-first validation of a commercially available, power-efficient extra-short reach (XSR) SerDes interfaced with a microring-based 8λ x 50Gbps NRZ WDM optical link through 100 meters of SM fiber. Modulator drivers & transimpedance amplifiers (TIAs) are monolithically integrated with photonics. Hardware stabilization/wavelength tracking algorithms, demonstrate robust operation and polarization handling over temperature. This demonstration also showcases Lightmatter’s multi-wavelength muxed laser source which provides stabilized wavelength and power accuracy. Lightmatter's electro-optical chip extends the XSR SerDes reach from centimeters to hundreds of meters. Additionally, It enables stacking advanced CMOS electronics onto silicon photonics for 3D CPO, with integrated hardware-based control to realize extreme bandwidths and high energy efficiencies in a single package.

### Revolutionizing Networking for the Era of Agentic AI
Speakers: Gilad Shainer (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6886/ | materials: https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gtc25-s71190/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=kS8r7UcexJU
NVIDIA Co-packaged optics (CPO) switches with integrated silicon photonics are the world’s most advanced networking solution for the era of agentic AI. They integrate optics innovations, including the first 1.6T CPO chip based on new micro ring modulators, 3-D-Stacked silicon photonics engines, high power lasers, and detachable fiber connectors. The CPO switches deliver 3.5x more power efficiency, 63x greater signal integrity, 10x better network resiliency at scale, and 1.3x faster deployment compared with traditional methods. NVIDIA co-invented the switch photonics with an ecosystem of partners, and designed it to fit into the OCP infrastructure and be managed by the Sonic operating system. Contributions to Sonic are being made to adjust it to the new CPO model. NVIDIA CPO-based networks simplify manageability and design and enable more power for compute infrastructure. These benefits are critical to delivering the scale needed to enter the future of million-GPU AI Factories.

### Scale-Out Networks and Scale-Up Architectures with CPO
Speakers: Rajiv Pancholy (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7489/ | materials: https://www.broadcom.com/blog/broadcom-cpo-from-concept-to-core-competency
Provisioning more compute is limited by power and rack density, and more complex architectures are no longer possible without growing the single node scale-up domain sizes. Single-node scale-up domain sizes are limited by the bandwidth density, weight, and reach of copper interconnects. Co-Packaged Optics enables scale-up domains > 512 while addressing the bandwidth, power, and latency constraints required to support growing model parameter sizes. To bring CPO to the scale-up domain where link flaps and operational issues have a severe impact, CPO must first deploy at scale for backend scale-out networks using the operational experience from these deployments to ensure more stringent scale-up interconnect requirements can be met. The talk will highlight specific examples from moving Broadcom’s 51.2 Tbps TH5-Bailly CPO from proto-type to production through the optimization of fiber routing, thermals, test yields, and box design; then focusing on how to bring CPO to scale-up domains.

### Development Challenges and Next-Generation Opportunities for Linear Drive Pluggable Optics (LPO) and Linear Receive Optics (LRO)
Speakers: Nan Ge (Meta Platforms), Amin Yekani (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7421/
The rapid growth of AI workloads poses challenges for data center network technologies, particularly in optical interconnects, which affect the speed and scalability of training large models. Meta addresses this with 200G/L optical transceivers, enhancing both front-end and back-end networks. This presentation reviews Meta's optical interconnect architecture, compares photonics technologies in 200G/L transceivers, and discusses desired diagnostic features for large-scale AI model training. Additionally, Meta explores Linear Drive Pluggable Optics (LPO) and Linear Receive Optics (LRO) to reduce power consumption and costs. Insights from LPO development emphasize overcoming technical challenges and ensuring system compatibility. We will share development experiences, lessons learned, and feasibility studies on LPO and LRO, focusing on technical and practical considerations for next-generation optical technologies.

### Linear Optics for AI Infrastructure: Power-Efficient LPO and LRO Solutions at 112G and 224G per Lane
Speakers: Tiger Ninomiya (Accelink Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6975/
The rapid growth of AI workloads is pushing data centers to seek optical solutions that deliver high bandwidth with lower power consumption. This session focuses on Linear Pluggable Optics (LPO) and Linear Receive Optics (LRO) as efficient alternatives to traditional DSP-based modules for 800G (112G/lane) and 1.6T (224G/lane) connectivity. We’ll explore how linear optics support key optical types such as DR8 and 2xFR4, and highlight the potential system-level benefits in terms of energy savings and scalability. The session will offer insight into the latest developments in LPO and LRO technologies and their role in building more power-optimized AI infrastructure.

### Optical Technology Considerations for AI xPU Scale Up
Speakers: Rang-Chen (Ryan) Yu (TeraHop)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7477/
AI xPU scale up beyond rack scale is of considerable interests to optimize economic value of large size AI compute clusters. Optical connectivity are required for scaling up xPUs beyond single rack. A range of low power optical technologies had been proposed including TRO, LPO, NPO, CPO, as well as "slow and wide" technologies like massive number of channels of uLED modulated at low speed to achieve low pj/b. We will present pros and cons of these technologies and discuss top candidates for next generation multi-rack xPU scale up applications.

Speakers: Richard Xiao (Linktel Technologies), Gilad Shainer (NVIDIA), Nathan Tracy (TE Connectivity, OIF, Member of the Board), Andy Bechtolsheim (Arista)

### ORW Frame Specification Proposal

page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6969/

We will discuss some of the challenges surrounding the AI architectures and why this large format rack is required to address them. The specification will cover rack geometry including critical dimensions such as equipment engagement interfaces, power interfaces, as well as the environmental, mechanical testing, electrical & grounding, and regulatory requirements. Finally the presentation will go over some of the technical challenges and considerations of creating an IT rack of this form, from design and development, through to DC infrastructure and deployment

### High Power Rack V3 (HPRv3) 50V Side Power Rack Design
Speakers: Jingjing Sun (Meta), Mohammed Alisa (Meta), Bo Wen (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7563/
The High Power Rack (HPR) V3 side power rack is an innovative 50VDC-based power solution designed to meet the high-density and high-power demands of AI workloads, advantageous in creating sufficient space for stacking more power HWs. This presentation will delve into the detailed design specifications and the following sections will be covered. 1. Overview and Key HW Components: The HPRv3 is a side power rack solution integrating essential components such as HPRv1 or HPRv2 PSU/BBU shelves, a modified HPR rack, vertical and horizontal busbars, and optional supercapacitor trays. 2. Mechanical design: Insight of into mechanical design of the HPRv3 side power rack and busbars will be illustrated. 3. Power system operation and busbar voltage behavior: An exploration of the power system operation and communication strategy among power shelves will be introduced, and the impact of the dual rack power distribution path to the busbar voltage behavior will be highlighted.

### Diablo 400 Project, HVDC Industry Standardization Effort
Speakers: Jorge Rodriguez (Google), Laurentiu Olariu (Microsoft), David Sun (Meta Platforms)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7350/
Defining Contributors [Google, Meta and Microsoft] have collaborated and submitted Rev 0.5.2 for OCP standard consideration the base specification for a +/-400Vdc disaggregated power rack, including how such a sidecar can attach to an IT rack that is +800Vdc. Effort aims to drive economies of scale that will hopefully lead to supply chain commonality, leveraging mainstream EV HVDC power conversion and battery infrastructure. The goal here is to enable use of high density/power HVDC IT racks in legacy and immediate launch data centers. Using Turn Key Approach (Purchase Sidecar as a Unit), specification aims for interchangeability at the Rack level. Focusing on standardizing rack Input and Output interfaces, along with certification and testing aspects. Spec allows for user specific differences to better support the needs of the user.

### Power Solutions of CBU & BBU
Speakers: Takuya Tsutsumi (Panasonic Corporation), Hank Tsuda (Panasonic Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7466/
As data centers expand and GPU power increases, power systems must handle load fluctuations and high-power demand. This session introduces two key solutions: the Capacitor Bank Unit (CBU) and the Battery Backup Unit (BBU), designed to improve efficiency, save space, and respond quickly. We will highlight recent progress in Li-ion cells and Li-ion capacitors, and how their future improvements can support next-generation GPUs. For BBUs installed in server racks, safety is critical. We will present our labyrinth structure, compliant with UL9540A, which helps prevent fire. The CBU delivers high power in a compact form and shares the same battery-cell can design with the BBU, enabling common parts and stable supply. Finally, we will share our roadmap, including liquid cooling to manage heat, and low-resistance capacitor to boost CBU output. These technologies aim to meet the growing needs of future data centers with safe, compact, and high-performance energy systems.

### Comprehensive Solutions for ORv3 Ecosystem: Ship-loadable Packaging and Essential Accessories
Speakers: Richard Banyard (Rittal)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6971/
This presentation offers a comprehensive overview of key accessories in the ORv3 ecosystem, highlighting two main areas: the 19” adapter and cabling & airflow management solutions. We will introduce essential components, including the 19” adapter rail, cable management arm, blanking panels, side skirts, and side expanders, detailing their design and benefits for the community. Additionally, the session will explore the extensive testing conducted on these accessories. These solutions are crucial for modern data centres, offering flexible, efficient, and organized approaches to infrastructure management.

### Next gen ORv3 HPR power levels and sidecar architectures with liquid cooled busbars
Speakers: Hal Loket (TE Connectivity), Dmitriy Shapiro (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7394/
As OCP and industry increase power levels, busbars are a key component to accommodate the latest AI/ML workloads. Further, incorporating liquid cooling to busbars enables a significant increase in current carrying capacity and overall rack power. When incorporated into horizontal busbars, this technology improves rack-to-rack connectivity in 48V ORv3 HPR sidecar architectures, reducing number of connections and copper for improved sustainability.

### 800Vdc MGX Acclerated Computing Rack & Energy Storage for Improved GPU Power Utilization
Speakers: Jared Huntington (NVIDIA), Mike Tu (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7116/
As larger synchronized GPU clusters are built synchronous AC input current swings present challenges for datacenter infrastructure. By adding energy storage through electrolytic capacitors, supercapacitors, or batteries these swings can be mitigated to better utilize available power. This presentation will give an overview of GPU energy storage requirements and approaches to better utilize datacenter available power as well as some practical techniques to improve rack input current profiles that should be widely understood by the OCP community.

## SONiC Workshop

### SONiC at Scale - Engineering the Future of Open Network OS
Speakers: Yanzhao Zhang (Microsoft), Delna Sholapurwalla (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7387/
The SONiC (Software for Open Networking in the Cloud) community continues to accelerate innovation across hyperscale, enterprise, and AI-driven networks. In this session, we’ll present a comprehensive update on SONiC’s progress, community growth, and strategic roadmap. We will explore highlights from the past year, upcoming releases, new workgroups driving innovation, and SONiC’s growing adoption in AI networking backends and new architectures. We will also cover community governance improvements, global outreach events, and collaborative engineering efforts that have shaped SONiC’s evolution.

### Driving Innovation in Network Infrastructure: Key Advances from the Sonic Routing Workgroup
Speakers: Patrice Brissette (Cisco Systems Inc.), Eddie Ruan (Alibaba)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7786/
Over the past year, the Sonic Routing workgroup has made significant strides in advancing network infrastructure. This presentation will highlight our primary initiatives, including the ongoing development of SRv6 and the Phoenixwing initiative, progress on VxLAN Multi-Homing (MH), the upgrade of Free Range Routing (FRR) to version 10.x, and the introduction of the RIB/FIB separation architecture. Additionally, we will discuss the impact of our newly added Wednesday workgroup meetings. Attendees will gain insights into the current status, achievements, and next steps for each of these key projects.

### SONiC-VPP – A Platform for DataCenter Network testing Roles
Speakers: Murali Venkat (Cisco Systems Inc.), Yue Gao (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7787/
This talk will follow up on key SONiC-VPP improvements, highlighting how we've ensured greater community usability since its initial release. We have enhanced SONiC-VPP with crucial features to enable an IP/BGP network ensuring its integration and validation against the SonicMgmt test frameworks, particularly for critical T0/T1 roles. We will share enhancements made to the core infrastructure, new features supported with improved dataplane and control plane capabilities. Furthermore, we will showcase the end-to-end packaging and build functionalities, empowering anyone to host and deploy SONiC-VPP within diverse cloud environments. Looking ahead we will also present a rich roadmap, detailing our development plan to drive broader adoption and unlock other compelling use cases for SONiC-VPP.

### Introducing SONiC Community Hardware Testing Lab
Speakers: Brian O'Connor (Google), Guohan Lu (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7322/
This year, the SONiC project has funded a new community hardware testing lab, hosted by the University of New Hampshire (UNH) Interoperability Lab. This testbed will enable SONiC images to be tested on a variety of switches provided by the community, and SONiC’s verification pipeline will be extended to run new code contributions on hardware switches in the lab. The goal of this effort is to improve SONiC image quality, understand new feature readiness for various different hardware platforms, and provide a space to troubleshoot SONiC image issues with the community. Vendors are invited to provide hardware to this new lab, and members of the community are encouraged to add new tests that can be run on hardware switches in the lab.

### Disaggregated Leaf-Spine Architecture for Next-gen Azure T2 Network
Speakers: Kamini Santhanagopalan (Broadcom), Rita Hui (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7442/
With SONiC Disaggregated Chassis, it helps break down traditional monolithic chassis into separate hardware and software components. This gives us increased flexibility and scaling, and very clear hardware abstractions, without being limited by the physical constraints of a single chassis. Recently, Microsoft and Broadcom has collaborated on a new fully disaggregated solution using xgs and dnx chipsets for data center spine layer routing using purely pizza boxes. In this presentation we will go over the details of the design using TH5-512 in the lower-disagg-T2 and Q3D in the upper-disagg-T2, the advantages of such a model. In future, the lower-disagg-T2 could progress to a even higher speed Asic's for further operational efficiency and scalability.

### Device Local Diagnosis
Speakers: Shashank Neelam (Google), Greg Boudreau (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7372/
The proliferation of disaggregated networking and the adoption of open-source network operating systems like SONiC have introduced unprecedented flexibility and innovation. However, managing diverse hardware from multiple vendors presents unique challenges for maintaining consistent device health and swift problem detection and resolution. This session will present device local diagnosis, a new feature that includes a local configurable service to detect and publish faults, if immediate non-traffic impacting reparative actions fail to recover, are exported over Openconfig/gNMI and handled remotely. The presentation covers how the standardized interfaces and open architecture of this feature can be leveraged by vendors and network operators. Vendors can build devices capable of diagnosing and exporting faults/actions allowing network operators to enable consistent diagnostics and repair automation regardless of the hardware, reducing operational complexity and integration overhead.

### Enabling Technologies for Next Generation Large Scale AI Backend Networking
Speakers: Guohan Lu (Microsoft), Mehak Mahajan (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7366/
The rapid acceleration of AI has transformed networking infrastructure, driving the need for increasingly large backend clusters. Over the past year alone, these clusters have grown from tens of thousands to hundreds of thousands of GPUs. This scale introduces a distinct set of requirements compared to traditional data centers, with greater emphasis on low latency, high throughput, and proactive fault detection. Consequently, advanced capabilities in traffic engineering and telemetry have become key focus areas. In this talk, we present Microsoft’s next-generation backend AI network architecture. We will highlight key features deployed in the data centers, including Segment Routing over IPv6 (SRv6), High-Frequency Streaming Telemetry (HFST), and trimming—discussing their implementation and the driving factors behind them. Additionally, we will explore the readiness of SAI and SONiC for supporting the deployment of hyper-scale AI backend networks.

### SONiC for AI Networking: Looking Back and Ahead
Speakers: Xin Liu (Microsoft), Wei Bai (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7788/
As AI workloads rapidly evolve, they are reshaping the demands on datacenter networking infrastructure. In this session, we will revisit key AI networking considerations shared in last year’s discussion and reflect on lessons learned. Building on that foundation, we will explore emerging challenges, evolving requirements, and the innovations needed to advance AI networking—highlighting opportunities for the SONiC and open networking community to lead in this space.

### SONiC's Scale up AI Cluster approach
Speakers: Jinzhou (Riff) Jiang (Microsoft), Eddie Ruan (Alibaba)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7104/ | materials: https://lists.sonicfoundation.dev/g/SONiC-Scale-Up-WG
There is increasing demand for building scale-up clusters, which are the systems that tightly couple large numbers of GPUs to minimize communication overhead for both training and inference workloads. Currently, there are several approaches to building such scale-up GPU clusters. Broadly, they fall into three major categories: using proprietary interconnect protocols, leveraging standard Ethernet, or deploying UAL fabrics. Among these, the SONiC community is focusing on how to enable and optimize Ethernet-based scale-up AI clusters, which offer openness, flexibility, reliability and ecosystem compatibility. This talk will present insights gathered from leading hyperscalers regarding their technical requirements. It will also highlight some key areas of ongoing community discussion and explain how SONiC collaborates with the Ultra Ethernet Consortium (UEC) to address these emerging needs. We also encourge more community members to join this journey to build this cluster together.

### RSR: Rapid Hitful S/W Upgrade
Speakers: Akarsh Gupta (Google), Jason Bos (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7338/ | materials: https://github.com/sonic-net/SONiC/blob/master/doc/express-reboot/Cisco_8000_Express_Reboot_HLD.md
Network switch software upgrades can be hitless with NSF and zero traffic loss, or hitful with possible traffic disruption. While NSF ensures uninterrupted dataplane traffic, it imposes significant complexity in terms of backward compatibility, phased rollouts, and extended qualification cycles. In contrast, hitful upgrades, particularly in the presence of breaking changes (e.g. ASIC-level P4 program change), offer greater simplicity and agility, enabling faster release cadences and version skipping. To minimize service impact during such upgrades, rapid reboot—supported by various vendors and continuously evolving—can reduce downtime to a few hundred milliseconds. This talk focuses on enhancing hitful rapid reboot support in SONiC, including a new reboot method, integration with the reboot back-end, improved shutdown orchestration, and robust state monitoring. It aims to standardize hitful upgrades in SONiC while ensuring reliability, observability, and vendor compatibility.

### SONiC Orchagent Event-based Retry
Speakers: Yijiao Qin (Alibaba), kang jiang (Alibaba)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6849/
This abstract introduces the redesigned event-based retry logic of orchagent program in swss module of SONiC system. The original orchagent performs a brute-force retry strategy for all the previously failed tasks in its cache, which has the risk of blocking the normal workflow when the accumulated failures are scaled. After observed the delay of normal event handling by previously accumulated failed tasks, we dived into the retry strategy and came to several conclusions. Firstly, not all failures deserve an immediate retry, since its constraint may have not been resolved yet. Secondly, it's worth analyzing the failure reason, and record it together with the failed task, as its constraint. Thirdly, we need a in-time notification mechanism to detect when the constraint is resolved and notify the related tasks to trigger their retry. Lastly, we should make sure the retry stage would not starve the normal event workflow, it can't last too long.

### INC Manager for AI Fabrics
Speakers: Mahesh Subramaniam (Juniper Networks), Vlad Olteanu (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7789/
In-Network Collective (INC) technology offers significant performance benefit by offloading GPU collectives such as all_reduce- reduce_scatter work to the fabric that is capable of INC acceleration for collectives such as reduction and multicast. INC manager is a central system management component to enable in-network collective acceleration. Its primary purpose is to orchestrate and manage the switch-based collective acceleration such as reduction and multicast capabilities provided by switch hardware in AI fabric infrastructure.\nINC manager talks to the INC_agent in SONiC NOS over the control or management plane. While the switch hardware is becoming available for INC- it is essential to build an eco system to deploy- manage and debug the fabric with INC capabilities. We will share a proposed architecture and software interfaces on SONiC and SAI needed to enable INC deployment.

## Security

### Caught in the Net: What the Cyber Resilience Act Means for Open-Source Software
Speakers: Christian Walter (9elements)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6837/ | materials: https://eur-lex.europa.eu/legal-content/DE/TXT/?uri=CELEX:32024R2847
The European Cyber Resilience Act (CRA) introduces mandatory cybersecurity requirements for all digital products sold in the EU — and for the first time, open-source software is explicitly in the spotlight. But what exactly is expected from maintainers, stewards, and contributors when code is published or used commercially? This talk explores the CRA’s impact on the open-source ecosystem: who is in scope, what obligations apply, and how open-source projects and vendors can prepare. We'll unpack key requirements like vulnerability management, SBOMs, security policies, and how “commercial activity” is defined. We’ll also highlight the role of open-source stewards — a new category introduced by the CRA — and how they can balance lightweight compliance with community-driven development.

### Device to Cloud Supply Chain Provenance
Speakers: Darpana Munjal Loodu (Microsoft), Prabhu Jayanna (AMD)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6902/
In the complex world of hardware supply chains, the security of hardware components has become a critical concern. Ensuring the integrity and trustworthiness of hardware components requires robust mechanisms for traceability, provenance, secure provisioning, and unclonable identities. Traceability enables manufacturers and consumers to track components throughout their lifecycle, from fabrication to deployment, helping detect tampering or unauthorized substitutions. Provenance ensures that each component's origin and manufacturing history are verifiable, reducing the risk of maliciously altered hardware. Secure provisioning safeguards the initialization and configuration of devices, preventing unauthorized access or manipulation during deployment. Unclonable cryptographic identities aid in the unique identification of devices, enabling strong attestations. Combined these elements can form the foundation of a resilient and trustworthy hardware supply chain.

### From Flashless to Fearless: A Rust Firmware Journey into Caliptra Streaming Boot
Speakers: Vishal Soni (Microsoft), Xiling Sun (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7464/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/caliptra-mcu-sw
As platform security threats escalate, the ability to securely and efficiently provision Root-of-Trust (RoT) components at scale becomes critical. Caliptra’s streaming boot model addresses this by enabling dynamic, host-driven RoT initialization, eliminating the need for persistent firmware storage and reducing attack surfaces. This session provides a deep dive into how this architecture is enabled by the modular, Rust-based MCU firmware stack with TockOS. We’ll examine the full stack from user-space application and image loading API to PLDM/MCTP protocol layers, I3C transport driver and Tock kernel integration. We’ll show how integrators can extend the SDK to implement custom boot logic while ensuring compliance with the Caliptra streaming model. Finally, we’ll draw directly from our own development journeys, sharing hard-earned lessons, real-world challenges, and practical strategies for adopting memory-safe language Rust and secure RTOS architecture in embedded firmware development.

### Device Ownership Transfer and Cryptographic Binding for Devices without Secure Storage
Speakers: Chris Hillier (NVIDIA), James Zhang (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7052/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hgDTxRbWzBQ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1Zo1gw82fqN-1oyobfdlmXnlVvdR-2TzSS4Ec_JNLZDs/edit?usp=sharing
This presentation is a follow-on to a presentation from OCP Global Summit 2023, where we introduced NVIDIA Device Ownership Transfer (DOT). DOT is essential for ensuring that only authorized entities can control and manage device firmware and configuration while still allowing circular economy principles to be applied. In modern computing environments, devices are often manufactured by one party and operated by another, necessitating a robust mechanism to securely transfer ownership from one entity to another during device resale, decommissioning, or repair. This presentation provides an update on DOT standardization at OCP by introducing the concept of a cryptographic binding method to be used with Mutable Locking DOT on devices that do not have secure storage. This method will be used by Caliptra Subsystem for devices without secure storage. This method is in the DOT specification being submitted to OCP - to be ratified as a standard way to approach Device Ownership Transfer.

### OCP S.A.F.E. Updates
Speakers: Eric Eilertson (Microsoft), Nick Hummel (Google), jasper van woudenberg (Keysight Technologies), ilja van Sprundel (IOActive)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6723/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-Security-SAFE
OCP S.A.F.E. standardizes security audits of server component firmware source code. This improves quality by learning from each other and reduces cost by sharing audits between companies. In this presentation we will quickly summarize the program and then discuss recent changes as well as future plans.

### OCP S.A.F.E.:  Enabling IBV Firmware Supply Chain Security for a Heterogeneous Datacenter Infrastructure
Speakers: Stefano Righi (AMI)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6915/ | materials: https://www.ami.com/blog/2024/10/15/ami-achieves-ocp-s-a-f-e-certification-with-open-compute-project/
Security at the datacenter starts in the supply chain. Today most datacenters with heterogeneous infrastructures are challenged to ensure security across multiple source trees, including firmware. To help overcome these challenges, OCP offers the Security Appraisal Framework & Enablement (S.A.F.E.) certification. This session demonstrates how an Independent BIOS Vendor (IBV) leverages the OCP S.A.F.E. framework to improve firmware security in the supply chain, including third party security reviews of ROM and Firmware images. Attendees will gain insight into the requirements applicable to firmware as source code, the short form report output published by the IBV, and the advantages for device manufacturers that adopt an OCP S.A.F.E. reviewed source tree.

### Caliptra Update: Roadmap, Integration, and Assurance
Speakers: Andres Lagar-Cavilla (Google), Bryan Kelly (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6876/ | materials: https://github.com/chipsalliance/Caliptra
The Caliptra project, an open-source Root of Trust (RoT) for modern silicon, has entered its second generation, continuing its mission to democratize silicon security. This presentation will provide a roadmap update, highlighting critical milestones and recent progress. We’ll revisit the core Caliptra profiles and dive into enhancements focused on ease of integration and performance optimization. As the community prepares for potential PSIRTs, we’ll share our assurance model and how it supports transparency and rapid response. Additionally, we will discuss the proposed trademark process and work-back plan for integrators - including a nod toward potential dev cost sharing models. Whether you're building with Caliptra today or evaluating it for future platforms, this session offers a concise overview of what’s next and how to stay involved.

### OCP LOCK with Caliptra 2.1
Speakers: Jeff Andersen (Google), Emre Karabulut (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7415/
An overview of OCP LOCK with the recent launch of Caliptra 2.1.

### SPDM Update
Speakers: Jeff Hilland (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Brett Henning (Broadcom Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6862/ | materials: https://www.dmtf.org/standards/spdm
DMTF’s SPDM WG has been working on a number of deliverables this year, including PQC support, new bindings, an Authorization specification and updates to the suite of SPDM specs. This session will cover the details of those updates, provide information on where to dig deeper and give an update on expectations from SPDM for the coming year.

### An Update on OpenPRoT
Speakers: Louis Ferraro (AMD), Miguel Osorio (Google), Ravi Sahita (Rivos)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6938/
This presentation highlights significant strides made in the OpenPRoT initiative, emphasizing advancements in architecture specification and code development since its introduction. As an open-source/open-standard Platform Root of Trust firmware stack, a central theme of our progress is the reusability of components, ensuring modularity and interoperability across various platforms, thus accelerating industry adoption while lowering costs. Furthermore, we will explore collaborative efforts with other OCP projects, notably code sharing, reuse, and interoperability with Caliptra, which underscores a unified approach in security standards. This collaboration enhances both projects' capabilities, fostering innovation and cohesion within the OCP community. We also cover how OpenPRoT can be used to reduce the complexity of attestation flows by providing standard interfaces, using OpenTitan hardware as an example. Join us to learn how OpenPRoT continues to drive transparency, security, and collaboration in the computing ecosystem.

### Caliptra Subsystem Manufacturing & Production Debug Flows
Speakers: John Thomson (lowRISC), Miguel Osorio (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7367/ | materials: https://opentitan.org/
We present a detailed overview of the Caliptra Subsystem's Lifecycle Controller (LCC) and Fuse Controller (FC), originally derived from OpenTitan RTL but enhanced to securely manage device lifecycle states and enable robust debugging capabilities. The updated architecture uniquely supports secure transitions across states such as Manufacturing and Production Debug modes, incorporating a structured TAP interface for secure token injection and debug access management. The design emphasizes secure debug policy enforcement, integrating multi-level debug capabilities and flexible control mechanisms tailored for advanced SoC integration scenarios. We demonstrate practical implementation approaches and comprehensive security assessments, showcasing our contributions toward secure lifecycle management in future open-source silicon Root-of-Trust systems.

### Cryptography in Caliptra
Speakers: Christopher Swenson (Microsoft), Jeff Andersen (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7445/ | materials: https://caliptra.io
Caliptra uses a variety of cryptographic methods to provide an open-source hardware identity and root of trust (RoT) and to provide certain services to its parent security subsystem. In this talk, we'll discuss the details how we derive and wield our hardware identity with systems like DICE and DPE, attestation, secure boot, and generic cryptographic services through mailbox commands that power concepts like ownership and device encryption, and how we are adapting to post-quantum requirement with the Adams Bridge hardware.

### Use of eSFI and PCIe-MI for Security Quarantine
Speakers: Brett Henning (Broadcom Inc.), Austin Bolen (Dell Technologies)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7402/
A challenge in server implementation is managing devices before trust is established. When a server starts, all the devices typically start as well. In a secure environment, a system owner may prefer to verify the integrity of all the devices before enabling them. In current implementations, servers may use a variety of proprietary mechanisms to delay the start of devices until they can be verified. Using eSFI and PCIe-MI, a server can now use industry standard mechanisms to isolate all devices at power-on and then manage enabling them once they satisfy the system policy. This discussion will layout the high-level approach to achieving security isolation and discuss where the OCP Security Project can take this technology in the future.

### OCP Attestation Updates
Speakers: Fabrizio Damato (AMD), Jeff Andersen (Google), Roksana Golizadeh Mojarad (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6821/ | materials: https://www.dmtf.org/sites/default/files/standards/documents/DSP0274_1.4.0.pdf
This presentation provides an overview of the latest OCP Attestation updates, designed to enhance security and interoperability in trusted computing environments. Key updates include the introduction of a new Remote Attestation Procedures (RATS) Entity Attestation Token (EAT) profile, which standardizes the attestation process for verifying device integrity and authenticity across platforms.
