### Low Carbon Emission Chassis Solution with Innovative Patterned Design
Speakers: Garrett Liu (Wiwynn Corporation)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6941/ | materials: https://www.wiwynn.com/whitepapers/the-lightweight-patterned-reinforced-chassis-in-server-product
As technological advancements and innovation continue to reshape server product design, Wiwynn has introduced a reinforced chassis featuring a novel embossed pattern aimed at reducing material usage and carbon footprint. This paper outlines the development process—from pattern optimization through Finite Element Analysis (FEA) to validation via static and dynamic mechanical testing.Through this approach, Wiwynn successfully developed an embossed pattern, enabling the replacement of the original heavy chassis with a thinner and lighter design. This lightweight, cost-effective, and sustainable chassis design reinforces Wiwynn’s commitment to sustainable server solutions and offers potential for further development.

### Sustainable Software Design: Addressing Bloat and Frameworks with Resource-Proportional Design
Speakers: Arun Mahendran (Lenovo), Manish Baing (Lenovo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6784/ | pdf: https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3600046.3600053
Sustainable (carbon-aware)software design is crucial for reducing the environmental impact of software. Challenges like software bloat & the use of frameworks can hinder resource optimization & energy efficiency. Resource-proportional design addresses these issues by tailoring resource usage to the application's specific needs, thereby reducing environmental impact. Sharing & reusing processed data and models minimizes redundant computations & storage, further lowering carbon production. The ACM paper "From Roots to Fruits: Exploring Lineage for Dataset Recommendations" https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3600046.3600053 promotes efficient data sharing through dataset lineage, optimizing resource usage and reducing energy consumption in processing pipelines. In this session, we will cover sustainable software design, addressing software bloat & framework inefficiencies with resource-proportional design & discuss data reuse to minimize redundant computations and reduce carbon production.

### Unlocking the Value of Waste Heat Through Liquid Cooling
Speakers: David Martinez (Sandia National Laboratory), Gabriel Lazar (Submer), David Gardiner (David Gardiner and Associates)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7219/ | materials: https://www.osti.gov/biblio/2516869
As AI workloads drive a rise in energy consumption and heat generation, this presents both a challenge and an opportunity. Sandia National Laboratories has proactively tested a variety of advanced cooling and heat rejection technologies, publishing a whitepaper that evaluates the performance of each system. This study demonstrates that immersion cooling not only enables greater use of free cooling across a variety of climates due to its higher operating temperatures, but also offers strong potential for efficient heat reuse. The presentation will serve as a call to action for the industry, highlighting specific areas where this case study can serve as a blueprint: Supporting early adopter collaboration across industries to validate scalable use cases Developing practical integration models for liquid-to-liquid heat reuse loops Highlighting design protocols and best practices for energy recovery for a variety of data center environments

### Powering IT, Wisely: Measuring Efficiency at Scale
Speakers: Sadas Shankar (SLAC), Scott Chamberlin (NeuralWatt), Tushar Krishna (Georgia Institute of Technology)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6957/ | materials: https://www.iea-4e.org/wp-content/uploads/2025/01/Data-Centre-Energy-Use-Critical-Review-of-Models-and-Results.pdf
Electricity in datacenters is increasingly delivered to the IT equipment with PUEs among hyperscalers already at 1.20 and among worldwide co-locators reaching below 1.4. Today, energy efficiency in datacenters is all about how well we use the electricity delivered to IT equipment. We have been talking about quantifying IT energy efficiency for years now but we are still missing basic metrics and methodologies for quantifying IT energy efficiency. What are the metrics and methodologies to quantify energy efficiency IT? What are the technologies needed to measure these metrics? What are the first key milestones to reach to power IT wisely? This panel brings together a group of experts to tackle questions about how bad IT energy efficiency is, and what the key hurdles are in measuring it, and how to tackle these key hurdles.

### Heat Reuse Subproject update
Speakers: Bharath Ramakrishnan (Microsoft), David Gardiner (David Gardiner and Associates)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6987/
Subproject update (heat reuse economics workstream has its own presentation, because they will present, finally, the Economics tool): Outreach workstream: update on policies, heat exchange platform (work together with EUDCA, Euro Heat and Power, NZIH) Ref designs white paper revisions 1MW heat reuse, impact on metrics, different climates and data centers ...

### Climate-Based Optimization of Cooling Strategies for Sustainable Data Centers
Speakers: Bahareh Eslami (NVIDIA), Raha Kalantarpour (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7259/
Cooling strategies are central to the sustainability and resilience of data centers, particularly as AI workloads drive rising energy demands. This study introduces an hourly, climate-responsive model to evaluate a range of external heat rejection systems (air/water-cooled chillers, dry and adiabatic coolers, evaporative systems, and hybrid cooling) across dedicated and shared loop designs. Results show that higher liquid cooling setpoints improve chiller performance and expand free cooling opportunities. Evaporative systems provide higher cooling efficiency but are water-intensive, limiting their viability in arid regions. Dry and adiabatic systems emerge as more water-conscious alternatives with acceptable performance levels. By linking system design, operational parameters, and climate data, this work offers insights for developing low-carbon, resource-efficient data centers and explores heat reuse as part of long-term sustainability strategies.

### Standardizing Embodied Carbon Disclosure - OCP Base Specification
Speakers: Lalit Joshi (Microsoft), Andrea Desimone (Schneider Electric), Howard Hsu (Meta), Miranda Gardiner (iMasons Climate Accord)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6978/ | materials: https://docs.google.com/document/d/1isyhViM7J0rjeAvJXawtDsUYCN7F1Khj/edit
The urgency of climate action demands consistent and credible carbon data throughout the data center value chain. To address the lack of standardization and transparency in embodied carbon reporting, the OCP/iMasons Climate Accord along with partner companies Schnieder Electric, Google, Microsoft and Meta have published* the " Base Specification for Embodied Carbon Disclosure" . This industry-first specification provides a standardized, methodology-agnostic guidance to report embodied carbon emission for ICT, MEP equipment, and construction materials. The Base Specification simplifies supplier disclosures and reduces redundancy of carbon data across diverse procurement channels by defining a common set of data fields, including footprint values, methodologies, emission factors, and verification status. It allows data center operators to make informed low-carbon design and purchasing decisions, while preserving flexibility for suppliers in how carbon is measured.

### Transforming Data Center Equipment Management: Reconext’s Shift to a Circular Technical Ecosystem"
Speakers: Shahriyar Rahmati (Reconext)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7133/
Reconext has transitioned from a horizontal to a vertical technical ecosystem approach in managing secondary life equipment for data centers. This shift enables a true circular economy , ensuring that hard disk drives (HDDs), solid-state drives (SSDs), and other data center components are securely erased, refurbished, and reused rather than discarded.

### Clean standby power solutions - powering the net zero transition beyond back-up
Speakers: Priya Chhiba (Google), Todd Noe (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7228/ | materials: https://www.netzerodatacenters.com/rfi-backup-solutions
The (IEA) forecast that data center consumption will grow from 47 GW in 2024 to 108 GWby 2030. Clean back-up power systems could provide flexibility, stabilize the grid and reduce the time to grid connection. NZIH and OCP are collaborating to identify, evaluate and validate alternative solutions for clean back-up power solutions At the 2025 OCP EMEA Summit in Dublin the status was presented in the breakout session 6519. This panel will discuss: Update on the collaboration with the OCP Clean Backup Power workstream Roadmap of clean back-up solutions based on the RFI inputs and evaluation Shortlisted technologies and validation plan Economics and technical evaluation parameters Clean fuels availability AI loads profile for back-up validation protocols Back-up as transition solution before power grid access Back-up contribution to flexibility and grid interaction

### Water Energy Nexus in Data Center Design
Speakers: Michael Anderson (SPX Cooling Tech, LLC), Rishab Vardhan Harikrishnan (Oracle)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7342/
As resource constraints continue to be the main limiting factor in data center development it is important to evaluate the most effective way to use said resources at a specific location. From a technology vendor standpoint, we are working to assist designers and engineers in how to best use water resources available to their site and balance with power and space constraints. WUE+ is a next-generation water efficiency metric for DC that expands on traditional WUE by accounting for embodied water from power generation- local water stress- and water reuse efficiency. By integrating these factors- WUE+ offers a holistic view of a data center’s water footprint‚ highlighting not just direct consumption- but sustainability across the energy and cooling lifecycle. It empowers operators to make data-driven decisions that prioritize water conservation- especially in regions facing high water stress. For those seeking simplicity- the Water Usage Optimization Score (WUOS) provides a location-aware alternative that adjusts traditional WUE by a local stress index. Together- these metrics encourage the adoption of closed-loop cooling- renewable energy sources- and strategic siting to build resilient and sustainable data center infrastructure.

### Empowering Sustainable Data Center Design with Microsoft’s Open Source Cloud LCA Tool
Speakers: Husam Alissa (Microsoft), Teresa Nick (Microsoft), Mukunth Natarajan (WSP USA Inc)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6931/ | materials: https://www.nature.com/articles/s41586-025-08832-3
As the datacenter industry increasingly focuses on sustainability, understanding the environmental trade-offs of siting, design, and operational decisions is more important than ever. In this session, we will explore the Cloud LCA Tool, an open-source life cycle assessment platform published by Microsoft and WSP in Nature. This tool enables stakeholders to evaluate both embodied and operational carbon impacts as well as water consumption across diverse infrastructure configurations. We will showcase the Cloud LCA Tool's capability to model and compare sustainability outcomes for the OCP community. During the presentation, we will demonstrate how the LCA tool can be used to support decision making related to datacenter development and operations by evaluating four scenarios.

### From component to fleet: an open-sourced methodology for IT carbon accounting at scale
Speakers: Mischa Weiss-lijn (Google), Lisa Rivalin (Meta), Arash Saboori (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7223/ | materials: https://sustainability.atmeta.com/blog/2024/09/10/estimating-embodied-carbon-in-data-center-hardware-down-to-the-individual-screws/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=rTfPdI31VIE
LCA is crucial to getting the accurate, granular and actionable scope 3 carbon emissions data needed to drive decarbonization across the IT hardware supply chain. Applying the best available LCA data where it matters, across complex and dynamic portfolios is impossible without automation. To this end, Meta and Google are launching an industry collaboration for a methodology to scale LCA data across the fleet, improve data quality, transparency and supplier engagement. This methodology and planned suite of open sourced software will enable the industry to standardize and cost effectively implement LCA at scale. It combines (1) a data taxonomy for hardware emissions computation, (2) large language models for information extraction and component categorization, (3) strategies for combining carbon footprint data of varying levels of quality, (4) hierarchical aggregations leveraging bills of materials and unit volumes and (5) emissions reduction modelling based on disaggregated LCA models.

### Hardware Design for Sustainability
Speakers: Sanjay Gupta (Meta), Emre Tepedelenlioglu (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6828/
Sustainable hardware design requires collaboration between designers, engineers, and sustainability experts to create products that meet customer needs while limiting impact on the environment. At Meta, Design for Sustainability (DFS) specifically focusses on the design of racks that power our data centers offering alternatives which can help rack designers make sustainable choices early in the product development lifecycle. We optimize designs for the lowest possible emissions, extending as much useful life as possible, harvesting components from decommissioned equipment and using lower carbon materials. We also collaborate with suppliers to optimize their manufacturing processes for carbon reduction. During this presentation, we will share key metrics we monitor and an example of a new product design where following DFS principles led to a 23% reduction in emissions while ensuring that sustainable choices are also economically viable.

### Panel: Standardizing Carbon Assessment Across the Industry
Speakers: Ines Sousa (Microsoft), Stephan Benecke (Google), Lisa Rivalin (Meta), Karsten Schischke (Fraunhofer Research Institute)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7497/
As cloud services expand, so does the environmental impact of datacenters. Measuring and managing carbon emissions is critical to advancing sustainability goals—but current approaches vary across the industry. This panel features sustainability leaders from Google, Meta, and Microsoft discussing how the cloud sector can align on Product Category Rules (PCRs) and Lifecycle Assessment (LCA) standards. PCRs guide LCAs, the method used to quantify the carbon footprint of devices, infrastructure, and services. Standardized frameworks make assessments more credible, comparable, and actionable—enabling smarter decisions in design, procurement, and operations. Panelists will share insights on the current state of carbon accounting, challenges with today’s fragmented practices, and collaborative efforts to establish cross-industry standards. Join us for a forward-looking discussion on how unified carbon assessment can help drive collective impact and accelerate progress toward net-zero goals.

### Data Center Efficiency Metrics (DCEM) workstream update and need for unified performance per energy
Speakers: Murugasamy Nachimuthu (Independent), Sadas Shankar (SLAC)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7450/
As the data center (DC) consumption continues to increase, the data centers infrastructure continue to increase resources to support additional demand. Sustainability of these additional resources requires a mechanism to measure and report various data points for continuous improvements in a sustainable way. The Data Center Efficiency Metrics (DCEM) workstream continues to make improvements in the DC infrastructure metrics. This presentation will highlight the DCEM accomplishments (Power and Telemetry, IUE), milestones, challenges/opportunities and new initiatives under consideration such as need for unified performance per energy metrics.

### DCF-Sustainability Sub-Working Group Update
Speakers: Aaron Wemhoff (other), Kially M. Ruiz (Cibao Cloud Technologies, Inc.), Julius Kusuma (Meta), Priya Chhiba (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7187/
Panel discussion will address the current state of the DCF-Sustainability project sub-working group. We will discuss ongoing workstreams, progress and contributions that have been made to the community: Construction Materials Reduction and Green Concrete Paper Learning Modules New Hyperscaler work Clean Backup OCP Technology Roadmap progress Net Zero Innovation Hub Collaboration NREL collaboration on common set of specifications for clean backup Water/Energy/Heat paper update, results and publication Metrics and Next Steps How will metrics need to be adapted for AI The community will be provided an opportunity to learn about the various work streams, understand how to participate and contribute to the various work streams, and most importantly learn actionable steps the OCP community is taking to support data center sustainability.

### Leveraging Digital Infrastructure in Manufacturing for Performance Optimization
Speakers: Brandon Weilbacher (Flex), Malcolm Hegeman (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7042/
Achieving ambitious sustainability goals and enhancing operational efficiency in hardware manufacturing demands a new approach to data. This presentation, supporting the Carbon Disclosure Standardization workstream, dives into the deployment of advanced digital infrastructure within manufacturing environments, designed to collect and contextualize streams of real-time operational/information technology (OT/IT) data. We will demonstrate how this data foundation directly enables two key performance improvement use cases: (1) pinpointing energy consumption through direct measurement, giving clear insight into reduction opportunities and clean energy strategies, and (2) advancing life cycle assessment (LCA) through the generation of ILCD-conformant life cycle inventories (LCIs), enhancing data quality, representativeness, and interoperability. Attendees will learn how this approach transforms manufacturing data into a powerful tool for understanding and reducing energy and carbon impact.

### OCP-Aligned Seawater Cooling and Heat Reuse: Enabling Circular Infrastructure for High-Density Compute
Speakers: Gemma Reeves (Alfa Laval)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7502/
This presentation introduces a Florida-based proof-of-concept site designed for integration into the OCP V2-certified Start Campus. It showcases a novel thermohydraulic architecture for sustainable, high-density compute environments. The facility uses a closed-loop, direct-to-chip liquid cooling system with seawater as the primary heat rejection medium. Supporting next-gen GPU and ASIC hardware, the system captures low-grade waste heat from the cooling loop and repurposes it to drive a membrane distillation module. This process transforms the warmed seawater into potable water, demonstrating an innovative approach to thermal energy reuse. By converting waste heat into a productive output, the design minimizes environmental impact and enhances overall system efficiency. The project explores the viability of coastal data centers as dual-purpose infrastructure—advancing both computational performance and environmental sustainability through integrated energy and water system design.

## Test & Validation

### Open-Source SSD Validation in Practice: Updates from Meta’s Qualification Framework
Speakers: Vineet Parekh (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7454/
Last year, Meta introduced an open-source SSD qualification framework through the Open Compute Project, aiming to standardize and streamline SSD validation at scale. Since that launch, we’ve continued to evolve the framework based on real-world deployment feedback and internal usage across diverse workloads. In this talk, we’ll provide an update on the project's current state, share practical lessons learned from integrating the framework into our qualification pipeline, and discuss the impact it's had on our collaboration with drive vendors. We’ll also touch on some of the challenges we’ve encountered and outline the areas we’re focusing on next as we continue to improve reliability, coverage, and community engagement.

### Beyond the Drive: The Strategic Value of System-Level NVMe SSD Testing in OCP and Cloud Infrastructures
Speakers: Chandra Nelogal (Wiwynn Corporation), Nick Kriczky (Teledyne Lecroy)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6901/
As hyperscale data centers and cloud infrastructures evolve, the performance and reliability of NVMe SSDs is mission-critical. But testing these drives in isolation is no longer sufficient. In this session, we’ll explore why system-level validation of NVMe SSDs—within the context of Open Compute Project architectures and cloud-native environments—is essential for ensuring real-world performance, interoperability, and resilience. We’ll discuss how NVMe SSD behavior can vary depending on host configurations, firmware interactions, thermal conditions, and workload profiles. By integrating SSD testing into the full system stack—CPU, memory, network, Operating Systems and software layers—organizations can uncover hidden bottlenecks, prevent data integrity issues, and optimize for power efficiency and latency at scale. Join us to understand how holistic SSD testing is not just a best practice—it’s a strategic imperative for building robust, scalable, and future-ready cloud infrastructure.

### CPACT: Cloud Processor Accessibility Compliance Tool
Speakers: Rajat Madhusudan (Microsoft), Alex Akesson (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7412/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/hyperscale-cpu-ras-and-debug-requirements-specification-v0-5-2025-05-22-pdf
The Cloud Processor Accessibility Compliance Tool (CPACT) is a key contribution to the Open Compute Project’s Test & Validation (OCP T&V) initiative. It is aimed at streamlining CPU compliance verification across hyperscalers and suppliers. CPACT empowers diagnostic developers and executors to validate processor accessibility against standardized specifications through domain-specific compliance scenarios, including dedicated RAS & Debug and Impactless Updates domains. Supporting both in-band and out-of-band access methods, CPACT ensures consistent result generation and comprehensive log collection for detailed analysis. Built on OCP T&V’s pytest-based framework, it fosters interoperability and confidence in supplier diagnostics. CPACT bridges hyperscalers and suppliers, accelerating onboarding through a unified, open-source compliance platform. Once specifications are finalized, CPACT will be publicly available on GitHub, promoting transparency and ecosystem collaboration.

### INSPECT:  Data analysis tool for Proactive Link Failure Detection on Meta's AI Infrastructure
Speakers: Granthana Rangaswamy (Meta), Arushi Sharma (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7250/ | materials: https://www.usenix.org/system/files/nsdi24-zu.pdf
As Meta scales up to 100K clusters and beyond for Gen AI applications, ensuring the hardware reliability is critical to prevent unplanned downtime and job restarts. With thousands of high-speed links and cables in the system, identifying correct interconnect issues promptly is essential. To address this challenge, we propose INSPECT - a software daemon that collects and analyzes SerDes parameters to predict link failures. Leveraging machine learning algorithms, INSPECT detects anomalies with pass/fail criteria and can be used for new system provisioning in DC, training job deployment, and continuous monitoring. By standardizing the collection and analysis approach across various SerDes vendors, INSPECT can be used on multiple Meta AI systems which helps us enable vendor heterogeneity. Our solution takes advantage of common SerDes building blocks, and maps them to Meta's predefined SerDes schema. With this INSPECT can be trained and deployed for heterogenous AI systems.

### FAST-Verify (Flexible Automated System Tester) A Modular Platform Testing Framework for Automated Validation in OCP Environments
Speakers: Scott Paulsen (Cisco Systems Inc.), Wei-jen Huang (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7217/
FAST-Verify is a modular, lightweight testing framework designed for high-level platform validation, currently used on OCP-inspired Cisco 8501 products . Using YAML/JSON input test vectors , it defines test parameters such as programs to run, expected return codes, expected outputs for pass/fail cases, and irrelevant outputs to ignore. Fast-Verify produces output test vectors summarizing results, including pass/fail founds and failure reasons, and generates an output tarball for seamless integration into workflows. The framework is portable requiring minimal configurations changes to support new systems, and is customizable, allowing test expansion via libraries or external scripts. Unlike traditional OCP diagnostics, FAST-Verify focuses on quick, high-level validation to ensure platform health before running details tests (e.g. memory, SSD or networking). Planned to be open-sourced by September 2025, FAST-Verify aims to foster innovation and collaboration within the OCP community.

### Testing Systems-in-Package built with Chiplets
Speakers: Rajesh Pendurkar (Cadence)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6721/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/documents/d2d-submission-v8-docx-pdf
Semiconductors in HPC, Data Centres, Automotive, and AI require a range of compute power needs with domain-specific architectures. Bringing those products to market more quickly, chiplet-based integration has advantages over monolithic-based designs, plus potential yield advantages, but finding all the faults in the final product becomes more challenging, which means test strategies become more important. A clear test flow is needed as chiplet-based designs are influenced by some of the key factors below. · The product may use Chiplets from different vendors. · Test coverage of the individual Chiplets, DPPM of individual die to product level. · Individual Chiplets Test Data to be available to the product company to ensure overall System performance. · Substrate influence on overall System performance. · The above factors influence the need for SLT This talk will outline a test strategy flow, based on a Known Good Die, to address the above whilst minimising test time and cost.

### AI assist Unit and Integration test framework for OpenBMC
Speakers: Manish Baing (Lenovo), Arun Mahendran (Lenovo)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6898/
The complexity involved in simulating and injecting hardware failures e.g PSU, DIMM, CPU errors, HDD failures, poses significant challenges for OpenBMC validation. Limited availability of faulty hardware & the high cost of physical test setups, including specific hardware, worsen these issues. Additionally, module-based code development increases the risk of missing existing or domain-specific functional scenarios during new code development. To address these challenges, we propose a unit & integration test framework that reduces the need for actual hardware. This framework uses stubs to simulate firmware code, converts C++ code to Python modules using the SWIG framework & writes function test cases for each module by simulating hardware faults. Beyond traditional testing, we will use AI for code coverage analysis, calculate Cyclomatic Complexity, perform automated patch analysis, vulnerability classification, and fault prediction using historical data and machine learning models.

### Fight fire with fire: AI-assisted test/debug flow and log analysis for AI GPU systems
Speakers: Wenxi Yan (Microsoft), Anna Mary Mathew (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7416/
This talk presents AI-powered test and debug workflows for modern AI GPU datacenters. We capture exhaustive logs during test cycles, decode mixed formats, and apply AI-driven pre-analysis (sanitization, deduplication, enrichment) to isolate error signatures. By integrating project docs, bug databases, and fleet-wide repositories via retrieval-augmented generation, we enable contextual matching, whitelisting, and deep post-analysis using domain specs, historical bugs, and occurrence metrics. Enriched signatures feed large language models for precise root cause analysis, while stop-on-fail triggers forward key alerts and diagnostics to team channels. Case studies—GPU link integrity failures, transient power inrush PGOOD anomalies, and driver reset issues—highlight new AI systems failure modes. We're planning to open source core log_analyze code too.

### Wind Tunnels for ASI
Speakers: Arun Darlie Koshy (Hewlett Packard Enterprise), Jean-Marie Verdun (Hewlett Packard Enterprise)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7172/ | materials: https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-AI
We build tools and scripts to deploy bespoke, private, perimeter-contained AI using open-source models like DeepSeek on high-performance infrastructure such as HPE with NVIDIA GPUs. This is done with an overall goal of building the scaffolding for testing & building not just the current state of the art but future approaches that are NOT based on LLMs/LRMs. But rather we head towards frontier AI reasoning systems and guided program synthesis. As a POC, we start off with a trivial bootstrap that quickly rolls out functioning instances of current state of the art open source models as APIs that can be then consumed by custom interfaces like a coding bot. All tools and techniques will be hosted at an OCP repository [https://github.com/opencomputeproject/OCP-AI]

### Diagnostics Testing on OCP Switch with Generative AI
Speakers: Wei-Jen Huang (Cisco Systems Inc.), Chih-Tsung Huang (Cisco Systems Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7568/ | materials: https://www.opencompute.org/products/565/cisco-8501-sys-mt-64x800g-osfp-switch
We will share our experiences with diagnostic test development leveraging the power of generative AI. We will explore how generative AI tools and methodologies have been applied to complex network hardware platforms running SAI, OCP-inspired Cisco 8501. Our discussion will focus on key aspects such as the capabilities of generative AI in automating diagnostic test creation, accuracy achieved in identifying and resolving issues and speed of implementation compared to traditional approaches. We will present a balanced view of the tradeoffs between these factors, highlighting where generative AI excels and where challenges remain. We will also touch on the opportunities for creativity and innovation that generative AI enables in diagnostic development, offering insights into how it can transform workflows and redefine the boundaries of test design. This session aims to provide a comprehensive overview of the potential and limitations of generative AI in modern network diagnostics.

### Manufacturing of AI Systems: Comprehensive Testing Approach
Speakers: Soumya Padmanabha (Meta), Gautam Nayak (Meta)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6816/ | materials: https://ai.meta.com/blog/meta-llama-3/
This presentation explores challenges encountered during AI systems testing in manufacturing environments and introduces an enhanced end-to-end test strategy designed to improve overall reliability and standardize testing processes. Challenges -Complexity of GPU/ASIC Architecture -Validating Scalability and Reliability -Simulating Real-World Environments,Evaluating Performance Metrics -Thermal Management Improved Test Methodology -Comprehensive Test Coverage:This approach encompasses testing from component level,rack level to multi-rack,ensuring that hardware meets performance specifications -Enhanced Thermal Testing Protocols:Simulate real-world scenarios using thermal testing techniques to ensure optimal thermal management -Benchmarking Testing:By running benchmarks and workloads in manufacturing, we verify performance and reliability - Multi-Rack Testing:Increased workloads help identify performance bottlenecks, enabling to optimize system performance

### Model-Based Testing in Action: Automating Test Generation and Boosting Coverage for Open-Source Software Components
Speakers: Mengqi Liu (Alibaba), shenglin zhu (Alibaba)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7275/
Test suites are critical for the correctness of open-source software components. The traditional approach of their manual maintenance is labor intensive and inherently limited in test coverage. Model-based testing (MBT) automates test generation by analyzing high-level behavior models, systematically exploring paths to create concrete tests. Alibaba shares its industrial adoption of MBT for open-source developments. We show how component models enable automatic test inputs generation and outputs calculation, significantly reducing human efforts while achieving high coverage at design and code levels. We discuss practical considerations for its sustainable adoption: (1) modeling language choices that enable developers unfamiliar with formal languages to write models; (2) infrastructural extensions to execute model-based tests; (3) benefits and positions among testing techniques, including brief comparisons with LLM-based approaches.

### SONiC Flaky Case Management
Speakers: Saikrishna Arcot (Microsoft)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7125/
The sonic-mgmt repo includes 1,000+ test cases run nightly across various hardware SKUs and platforms. To streamline failure detection, we use an internal system called Nightly Hawk that automatically surfaces issues from these runs. Failures are classified by retry behavior into two types: consistent (reproducible and easier to debug) and flaky (intermittent and often overlooked). While consistent failures are addressed quickly, flaky ones can mask real issues or signal test instability. This session introduces a system to manage flaky tests by collecting and tracking them across runs, categorizing failure types via summary analysis, surfacing them without duplication, and providing indicators to prioritize them in repair workflows.

### A Modern Approach to SoC Verification: From Virtual Models to Emulation
Speakers: Luis E. Rodriguez (Siemens), Karthik Padmakumar (Arm)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7207/ | materials: https://dvcontaiwan.org/wp-content/uploads/2024/09/DVCon-Taiwan-2024-Keynote-3-Siemens.pdf
Arm and Siemens EDA introduce a shift-left methodology to accelerate development of Arm® Neoverse™ V3 Compute Subsystem (CSS) designs—aligned with OCP goals for modular, standards-based infrastructure. The approach leverages Virtual OS Models, SystemVerilog Verification IP, emulation, and analog channel models to enable early hardware and interface validation, firmware and software testing, and performance profiling. By gradually replacing VIPs with real IP and incorporating physical channel models in RTL co-simulation, teams validate interoperability and compliance early—including SBSA and PCI-SIG CV testing. Emulation with real-world software provides high-fidelity performance insights to optimize system architecture. This framework reduces risk, shortens cycles, and supports scalable deployment of Neoverse-based solutions across the open data center ecosystem.

### DC-Stack: Google Server Manufacturing SDK Lessons Learned
Speakers: Sivagar Natarajan (Google), Eric Chuang (NVIDIA)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7470/ | video: https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=b9-MrNwjoCw
The challenges and lessons learned, over the last 2 years, deploying DC hardware through common S/W stack. We present the challenges in terms of codevelopment, validation and TTM. The goal is to understand if there is a need for a common open stack solution for the Hyperscalers and what would be the requirements for such a solution from Hyperscalers and hardware partners.

## Time Appliances Project (TAP)

### Precision Time Protocol for AI Back-End Networks: Modeling and Deployment Insights
Speakers: Ahmad Byagowi (TBD), Amit Oren (Broadcom), Bhaskar Chinni (Broadcom)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7537/
Precision Time Protocol (PTP)-based time synchronization is widely adopted in telecom and data center front-end networks. As AI networks—also known as data center back-end networks—rapidly scale to support the exponential growth of large language models (LLMs) and GPU clusters, the need for precise coordination becomes critical. Optimizing AI workloads on these high-value GPU infrastructures can significantly improve performance and return on investment.

### Firefly: Scalable, Ultra-Accurate Clock Synchronization for Datacenters
Speakers: Yuliang Li (Google)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/7109/
Cloud-based financial exchanges require sub-10ns device-to-device clock synchronization accuracy while adhering to Coordinated Universal Time (UTC). Existing clock sync techniques struggle to meet this demand at scale and are vulnerable to clock drift, jitter and path asymmetries. Firefly, a software-driven datacenter clock sync system scalably, cost-effectively and reliably achieves very high clock sync accuracy. It employs a distributed consensus algorithm on a random overlay graph to rapidly converge to a common time while applying gradual adjustments to device hardware clocks. To realize consistent sync-to-UTC (external sync) across devices while maintaining a stable device-to-device internal sync, Firefly uses a novel technique, layered synchronization, that decouples internal and external syncs. In a 248-machine Clos network, Firefly achieves sub-10ns device-to-device and ≤1µs device-to-UTC synchronization, and is resilient to time server failure and unstable clocks.

### Designing Reliable PTP Infrastructure: From PHC to SyncE and Clock Stability
Speakers: Sean Liu (Skyworks Solution), David Spencer (Skyworks Solutions Inc.)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6718/
Deploying Synchronous Ethernet (SyncE) not only extends PTP holdover performance but also potentially reduces the PTP message rate, thereby reducing network loading, which can be crucial in data centers due to the large number of nodes involved. SyncE enhances the accuracy of a PTP network, even with many Transparent Clocks (TCs) in the synchronization path, because the SyncE clock is more stable and accurate than the local oscillator of the TC nodes and therefore allows better residence-time measurement. However, using SyncE can be a double-edged sword since, as simulated in ITU-T G.8273.2, any disruption on the SyncE signal can significantly impact PTP performance. The loss of SyncE causes a spike in time error, and the subsequent restoration of SyncE causes a second spike in PTP time error due to any frequency offset of the SyncE signal.

### Advancing Timing Resilience in Edge-to-Core Systems: From White Rabbit to PCIe
Speakers: Ullas Kumar (Rakon), Greg Armstrong (Renesas)
page: https://2025ocpglobal.fnvirtual.app/a/schedule/6878/
AI workloads are driving demand for nanosecond-level synchronisation in data centres. Precision timing is distributed from GNSS or atomic sources through network hierarchies, steering local clocks at each stage. To minimise sync traffic and enable high accuracies, systems use narrow-loop bandwidths, requiring ultra-stable oscillators. Recent advancements enable low-profile, low-power clocks with <1ppb temperature stability and phase noise below -170 dBc/Hz, supporting higher order modulations in coherent optic transceivers. These clock references offer sub-100 fs jitter, 4–8h holdover, ppt-level digital tuning, and integration into discrete servos. System results show excellent dynamic performance, enabling telecom boundary clocks and future AI-scale timing architectures. These improvements pave the way for scalable, energy-efficient timing infrastructures, meeting the precision needs of advanced AI and edge computing workloads.
